# Release notes

## Unreleased

### Fixed
- Note history: when a project is over its 500 MiB history budget, the saved versions of the note being written go first (a quick series first, then the oldest; its newest saved version stays), then the writer's versions in other notes (oldest first, each note's newest stays), and only then the project's oldest. One member's edit loop no longer evicts other people's saved versions; the per-note limits (100 versions, 8 MiB) and the rule that an overwrite is never refused are unchanged. When the writer's own versions cannot free enough, the project's oldest still go, as before.
- Docs: a grant gives permission only; to find and read the granted project's notes from the receiving project's folder the owner also records `gw project depends add`. A project admin writes a team's notes only when they are also on the team's roster.

## 0.9.1

### New
- `scripts/compose-team-rehearsal.sh` rehearses a team gateway in Docker Compose on the real server before the team is invited: an isolated project (own name, ports and volumes, refuses an existing one), the owner workflow, an access matrix, knowledge review and note history, optionally the remote settings page, a backup and restore into a second project, and a sanitized report. See `docs/docker.md`, "Rehearsal before inviting the team".

### Fixed
- Remote settings: accepting or rejecting a replacement of a private note no longer writes a `kb.owner.read` audit row; the gateway's own refusal check reads without it (a read that returns the private text still does).
- The refusal for a client too old to accept a replacement now says "update minigw (0.9.0 or newer)" (no beta clause).
- Plugin (Windows, Linux): an early exit without a verdict (no signed checksums, or the client in `bin` unreadable) puts the plugin's `bin` on PATH only when the stored `.verified` record still matches the files, by the same rule the knowledge-base hook uses. Offline, a fresh folder prints one line that says both that the installed minigw cannot be verified and that no verified one can be downloaded; the no-verdict text no longer claims "nothing verified before"; the plugin's own copy is called "the latest release this plugin has seen" when nothing was fetched in that run.
- Docs: update every pushing gateway with or before the upstream one (`docs/usage.md`, upstream chapter).

### Known problems
- The rehearsal script was run only on Docker Desktop for Mac. On a real Linux server these parts are unproven: the ownership of the certificate folders (container user ids), a LAN `--bind`, the documented source filter (the rehearsal tests only the Compose network's own subnet), BusyBox variants of the tools it needs, and a real model request (left to the operator).
- Windows plugin: when the client in the plugin's `bin` cannot be read at a session start (opened exclusively by another process), that session gets no PATH entry for the plugin; the next start restores it.
- The known problems of 0.9.0 still apply.

## 0.9.0

Mini Gateway 0.9.0 is a stable release. It collects the eleven beta versions 0.8.1 to 0.8.11, which stable users never saw, and follows the stable release 0.8.0. The sections below describe what 0.9.0 is, not how each beta got there. Read **Upgrading from 0.8.0** and **Known problems** before you update a gateway that other people use.

### New
**Knowledge base: saved versions and review of a replacement**
- The gateway keeps the previous version of a note before an edit, an accepted replacement or a re-import overwrites it: title, text, class, kind and where it came from. At most 100 versions per note, 8 MiB per note and 500 MiB per project. When a note has more than 100 saved versions, versions from a quick series of edits by one person (each less than ten minutes after the one before) go first, the shortest-lived of them first, then the oldest. An overwrite is never refused because of these limits.
- `minigw note history <id>` lists the saved versions of a note (newest first, at most 100; `--revision n` shows one with its text, `--json` prints the answer). Each row says when the version was WRITTEN and by whom, and when it was REPLACED and by whom or by what (an edit, an accepted submission with its reviewer, an import, a restore), with the time zone:
  `revision 5  written 2026-11-17 09:12 UTC by Ana  replaced 2026-11-18 14:02 UTC by Tilen  structure  finding  1200 bytes  Invoice numbering`.
  `minigw note restore <id> --revision n` puts a saved version back as a new revision. The gateway owner has the same two commands as `minigw gw kb history` and `minigw gw kb restore`. Both clients (Go and Mac) print the same text. For a team note both commands need `--team` like `note edit`.
- Who may read which saved version: you see a saved version only if you may read the note now and your access also allows the class the version had then. A reader sees structure versions (and private ones of their own note), a developer also data, a project admin everything, a reader through a grant only structure and data of the granted project, a team member structure and data of the team's notes. A version you may not read is left out: no text, title, class, size or count of it is shown. The owner sees all; reading a private version is logged like any owner read of a private note.
- Restore is for project admins (a team assignment counts) and the gateway owner, not for the note's author. It never rewrites history: the text becomes a new revision, the version it replaces is saved first, and the note keeps its CURRENT class unless you ask for the saved one with `--class` (which can widen who reads it). Without a terminal, `restore` needs `--current <revision>` (the revision you saw); a note that changed in the meantime is refused, never overwritten. The restore is audited (`note.restore`, no text) and the new revision records `restoredFromRevision`. `note history` and `note restore` are not available inside an agent run, and not through the remote web settings.
- Review of a replacement shows both texts, on every surface. `minigw kb review` and `minigw gw kb review` (both clients) print the sizes, then `Current text (will be replaced): note N  revision C …` with its text, then `New text: …`:
  `current: 212 lines, 11429 characters -> new: 141 lines, 7602 characters (lines shorter: 71, 33 %)`.
  The Mini Gateway app's Knowledge base panel shows both texts in the detail and the sizes on the queue row. The remote web page shows two separately labelled and coloured blocks, each with its label kept in view while you scroll, and says "Shorter by 66 lines (33 %)" when a rewrite drops text.
- The accept is bound to the pair. The review version (hash) of a replacement also covers the replaced note's revision and text, and the accept carries the note's current revision. If the note was edited, restored, re-imported, changed in class or deleted after you read it, the accept is refused ("the submission changed since you read it") and nothing is published. A replacement written against an older revision of the note ("stale") or of a note that no longer exists cannot be accepted: reject it. When you reject such a one without typing a reason, the gateway stores one reason itself for everyone who can read it (the author, a reviewer, the owner): "the note this would replace changed or no longer exists: read the project's notes and propose the change again". It carries no revision and no note state.
- The app's panel has a separate action "Accept replacement…" with a confirmation. After an accepted replacement the output names the saved revision and the commands that show and restore it (`note history`, `note restore`). The audit entry says that a note was replaced and which revision was saved (numbers only).
- Hidden characters in the note's CURRENT text do not block a replacement: the review says how many there are and that accepting removes them from the note; the old text stays in the note's history as it was. Hidden characters in the NEW text still cannot be accepted.
- `minigw kb submissions` (both clients) prints the reason under a rejected submission (`    reason: <text>`), so the author learns why it was rejected; `--json` is unchanged. After `minigw kb submit`, a line says so when a submission with the same title in the same project was rejected in the last 30 days. The plugin's notes skill tells a session to read the reason before handing in again, and not to hand in the same text unchanged.

**Team gateway in Docker Compose**
- `deploy/docker/backup.sh` backs up and restores both volumes of a gateway in Docker Compose (it needs Python 3 on the server). The volume names come from Compose itself. It refuses a project without containers (unless `--offline`), a gateway container that uses other volumes than Compose resolves, any other container that is not stopped and mounts the volumes, an empty source, and a restore into a volume that is not empty or into another project (unless `--into-project`). `--stop` stops the services for the copy and starts again only those that ran before. The archives are streamed through a helper container (no folder of the server is mounted into it), Docker's own error messages are shown, and a backup that did not finish is removed. See "Backup" in `docs/docker.md`.
- `scripts/compose-recovery-e2e.sh` rehearses the three dangerous moments of a Compose deployment, and runs before every release: an update from the oldest release with container mode (0.7.6, database schema 12) to this version with a populated database (users, a team, a project with members, notes, a submission waiting for review, a provider): the database is upgraded and the same data is listed and found by search; going back to the old version with the backup made before the update; a restore into a separate project from the backup folder alone. An old version that meets a database upgraded by a newer one refuses it and leaves the file unchanged. `docs/docker.md`, "Updating and going back", has the exact commands, the message an old version prints for a newer database, and what the snapshot inside the volume is for (it is not a backup).
- Container logs of the Compose deployment are bounded (`json-file`, 10 MB, 3 files). `docs/docker.md` also describes the first setup on a team server step by step, what to do before an update, and which files belong to a version.
- Remote web settings (the Compose admin profile) now do more than providers and devices. A "Team (read-only)" page shows users, the user each device is bound to, teams and projects; a team (its members and what it assigns) and a project (who has access and why: directly or through which team) open on a click or Enter. Changes stay with `docker compose exec minigw minigw gw …`; the page says so. Requests with a method-override header are refused. Below 700 px the navigation of the settings page (local and remote) sits above the content, and on the remote Team page a value drops under its label when the row is narrow.
- The remote settings page has a "Review" page. It lists the knowledge-base submissions that wait for review in all projects and teams, shows one exactly as the owner's review read gives it, and accepts or rejects exactly the version that was read, after a confirmation view. The published note is the text that was shown: if the submission changed in between, the accept is refused and the new text is shown. The reason for a reject is one line of at most 1024 bytes.
- What cannot be accepted from the browser, by the page and by the gateway itself: a submission with hidden characters (it can only be rejected), a long one (over 20,000 characters), and a replacement whose note changed or is gone. A replacement can be accepted there while its note is unchanged and both texts are shown in full. Those that cannot be accepted are reviewed on the server; reject works everywhere. An accept of a replacement whose `currentRevision` is not the one in the fresh read is refused at the gateway's door (403, one `denied` row, not counted among the 30 decisions).
- Limits: at most 30 decisions in 10 minutes for the whole listener; the page then says from when you can continue. Decisions that the knowledge base itself refuses (the text changed since the read, an unknown number) count toward the 30. Requests with a malformed body do not; they have their own limit of 300 in 10 minutes. Every decision, also a refused one, is written to the remote audit log with the submission's number and the address it came from. The remote "Review" read of a replacement of a PRIVATE note writes the same `kb.owner.read` audit row as reading a private note or its history (the note id and the project or team, never the text).
- The gateway's remote listener and the proxy allow exactly the routes listed in the remote settings section of `docs/docker.md`, with their methods (the page says plainly that a remote accept publishes to the knowledge base, what cannot be accepted remotely and the two limits); a test keeps that table and the allow-list equal and fails when the `Caddyfile` gets a rule the test does not know.

**Gateways that talk to each other**
- Upstream push never creates a second copy of a note. A pushed update that the upstream's reviewers REJECTED, or that still waits for its review or can no longer be read, is not sent as a new note: a note whose first submission still waits for its review upstream is not sent again until it is decided there (waiting notes are checked when a push happens anyway: `minigw gw upstream push`, or an automatic push after a local change). After the review the newest local text goes as a replacement when it was accepted.
- When the upstream note changed since the version our text corresponds to (its revision is above the one our last accepted text made), the note is a conflict and nothing is sent, also after a replacement was rejected as stale. A revision mismatch at submit is the same conflict. The same holds when the upstream's note is deleted or no longer visible to the gateway's identity, or the earlier submission can no longer be read. `minigw gw upstream resolve <project> <id> --theirs` keeps our text unsent; `--ours` sends it as a replacement at the upstream's current revision for its reviewers (when the upstream note is not visible any more, `--ours` looks again, replaces it if it is visible again, else sends a new note and says so). The Mini Gateway app's Upstream gateways panel names the three kinds of conflict.
- A refusal that concerns one note (for example "Too large") no longer stops the whole run; it is listed as "last refusal", and a run that sent something is a success. The run stops only when the upstream says that the link as a whole cannot go on: the gateway's identity is not accepted, it has no role in the project, or its role allows no hand-in at all. Only a run where everything was refused is a failure with a back-off. `status` says "N notes waited for a review upstream at the last push; they are checked at the next push (not a failure)". The report says `create` for a new submission and `replace` only when an upstream note is replaced.
- `minigw gw upstream link <project> <upstream> --auto` (or `--manual`, `--class …`) on an existing link keeps the link's destination. Only an explicitly different destination is refused: unlink and link again to change it.
- The note history of the upstream gateway makes an overwrite there recoverable.

**Claude Code plugin**
- The plugin's knowledge base hook runs nothing that has not been checked. `kb-hook.sh` exits silently unless `minigw kb hooks on` is set for at least one project, and then runs only the file whose SHA-256 matches the record `bin/.verified` that `install-minigw.sh` writes when it has verified a client (and removes when it rejects one). A replaced or not yet checked file, a missing record or a missing `sha256sum`/`shasum` means no run. A client that was kept without verification (a computer without `ssh-keygen`) gets no record, so the hooks stay silent there.
- Windows session-start script: it never writes `bin/minigw` or `bin/minigw.cmd` and never moves `minigw.exe` aside, so there is no lock and no `.keep` (Git Bash treats `minigw` and `minigw.exe` as one file). It uses the installed minigw only through the launcher that `minigw plugin install` writes (exact text, pointing at an installed `minigw.exe` whose hash is in the signed checksums and that is not older than `MIN_CLIENT`); otherwise it uses its own `bin/minigw.exe`, downloaded and verified, which Git Bash finds as `minigw`. A launcher that does not qualify (unverified, too old, pointing nowhere) is removed so that it cannot shadow the own copy. A newer release is downloaded under a temporary name and put in place with ONE move; the signed checksums are published before it and the previous generation is kept (`SHA256SUMS.prev`), so a start killed at any step leaves a client that verifies. Two sessions that start at the same moment do not disturb each other. After the installed client is copied into place, `.asset.sha256` matches what is in place. A write-locked `minigw.exe` gives one line per start, without the raw tool error. A message about an old installed client says it is the plugin's own copy that is used.
- Offline behaviour: a session start in a fresh data folder (nothing verified before) does not remove a valid launcher; without any signed checksums there is no verdict, so no `.verified` record is written and one line says the installed minigw cannot be verified yet and that the next start tries again. A launcher is removed only on a real verdict (checksums available and the client's hash is not in them, the client does not exist, or it is older than `MIN_CLIENT` while a better own copy is in place). The "is not the current release ... update it" advice is printed only when checksums were fetched and verified in that very start.
- `minigw serve install` no longer answers "already up to date" when the installed gateway has the same version as the `minigw` that runs it. It says that it installs the version of the `minigw` that runs it, and how to get a newer release: update the Claude Code plugin and run `/minigw:gw update` again, or install the newer MSI. The plugin's `gw` skill and `docs/install.md` describe the same step.

### Changed
- "no answer from the gateway" now names its cause in both clients (connection refused, timed out after N s, connection reset, could not resolve the host, network unreachable, TLS), without any address or header.
- Titles in one-line rows (`minigw note list`, `note read`, search results, FAQ lists and the history list) are printed on one line, in both clients.
- Update hints: a client 0.9.0 with a gateway older than 0.8.8 shows `Current text: not shown (the gateway is older than 0.8.8)` and refuses to accept a replacement: update the gateway. With a gateway older than 0.8.7, `note history` and `note restore` say that the gateway must be updated.
- Security hardening: the gateway's database is opened with `secure_delete` and the two full-text tables have FTS5's own secure-delete option on (a persistent option row set at every start, no database upgrade; SQLite 3.54), so SQLite overwrites deleted text and deleted search postings at the write. In WAL mode the old page in `core.db` goes at the next checkpoint and the log's frames when they are overwritten or the log is truncated; earlier snapshots and backups keep what they held. `docs/how-it-works.md` says exactly this: it is not a forensic guarantee. The search rebuild (`kb rebuild-search`, a restore) and the removal of a read link with many mirrored notes run with the option off, merge the index afterwards (so the deleted postings leave the file) and turn it back on in the same transaction; deleting one note still overwrites its postings at once.
- The owner's review read of a PRIVATE note, its history, or a replacement of it (also over the remote page) writes a `kb.owner.read` audit row (the note id and the project or team, never the text).
- Documentation: the remote settings page is described as it is (teams and projects read-only, the Review page and its limits); `docs/docker.md` lists every route the remote listener allows.

### Fixed
- Docker Compose backup: the backup recipe in `docs/docker.md` built the volume name from the folder name, while the Compose project is named `minigw` by default. In a folder such as `deploy/docker` it could archive an empty, newly created volume. Backups made with that recipe must be checked: `tar -tf minigw-config.tar | grep serve.json` has to print the file; if it prints nothing, that backup is empty.
- Windows plugin: in 0.8.0 the plugin's own `minigw.exe` could be replaced or lost when an installed client appeared, or before an update download that then failed.
- A note title that contains a line break could forge a line in `minigw note list`, `note read`, search results and FAQ lists.
- Upstream push: a rejected update pushed again as a new note left a second copy next to the original (see New).
- `minigw serve install` said "already up to date" although nothing was installed (see New).

### Upgrading from 0.8.0
- The gateway's database is upgraded from schema 25 to 26 at the first start, and a snapshot is kept first (the knowledge base keeps saved versions from then on). **Going back to 0.8.0 afterwards needs the snapshot or backup made before the update** (see "Going back" in `docs/install.md`; in Docker Compose run `backup.sh --stop` before changing the version).
- Update clients and gateways both. A client older than 0.8.8 (the stable 0.8.0 from Homebrew, `install.sh` or the plugin) cannot accept a replacement on this release; the refusal names the ways out (update the client, use the Mini Gateway app's panel or the remote settings page, or reject). A client older than 0.8.7 does not know `note history` and `note restore`.
- Update every pushing gateway with or before the upstream one (see Known problems).
- Docker Compose with the admin profile: copy the release's `deploy/docker/Caddyfile` (and the compose files) into your deployment folder and run `docker compose restart admin-proxy`; until then the remote pages show a hint instead of the data. Before the update take a backup with `backup.sh` (see `docs/docker.md`).
- The Claude Code plugin updates itself. With a plugin older than 0.8.2 that does not happen in time: update it first (`claude plugin marketplace update mini-gateway`, `claude plugin update minigw@mini-gateway`), then run `/minigw:gw update`; or install the MSI of the new release by hand.

### Known problems
**Upstream push and gateways**
- A pushing gateway that stays on 0.8.0 keeps the old upstream behaviour against an upstream gateway on this release (a replacement at the upstream's newest revision; a rejected replacement returns as a new note): update every pushing gateway with or before the upstream one. The upstream gateway's note history makes an overwrite recoverable.
- The id of the submission that a newer one superseded is not kept locally, so a copy on the upstream whose last line names another earlier submission of ours still counts as our text. The upstream copy of a note that was deleted locally is shown among the upstream's notes again (its author is no longer known here).
- After this version is installed, a linked note whose last submission was rejected or is still waiting becomes a conflict at its next change when the upstream text is not exactly what this gateway last sent: resolve it once with `--ours` or `--theirs`.
- An automatic link whose only unsent note is refused every time (for example a note over the size limit) tries it again with a growing pause (up to about an hour) until the note changes. A conflict "deleted or no longer visible" is not cleared by itself when the note becomes visible again: `resolve --ours` replaces it then. `status` learns about a review upstream at the next push, not before.
- After restoring an OLDER local database, the gateway no longer knows what it sent after that backup: compare with the upstream before pushing (a push can create second copies).
- With an upstream gateway still on 0.8.0, an identity that was lowered to "reader" gets one refusal per changed note instead of one clear stop.
- The Mac client's part of the upstream push was checked with the shared tests of both clients, not by hand against two gateways. The retry of a replacement that the upstream refuses as outdated at hand-in was not provoked in the real round (it needs a race); tests cover it.
- "no answer from the gateway": the cause of a rare occurrence in the Mac app's tests is not found; the message now names the cause (refused, timed out, …).

**Knowledge base: saved versions and review**
- Text that was removed from a note, also something pasted by mistake, and the text of a note that was later made private, stays in the note's saved versions until it leaves the 100 or the note is deleted. That removes it from the database; backups made before still hold it. There is no command yet to remove one saved version.
- Many people editing one note in turns, or a long series of edits each ten minutes or more apart, can push its oldest saved version out of the 100. Within a project, the oldest saved versions of any note go first when the project's 500 MiB are reached, so one member who edits in a loop can push out the older saved versions of other notes in the same project. The limits count the stored text, not the size of the database file.
- Export, bundles and what a gateway sends upstream carry the current text only; a database backup carries the saved versions. A note that is a copy from an upstream gateway has its saved versions on that gateway (the commands say so; not tried with two real gateways).
- Revision numbers are kept, so a gap in the numbers of `note history` shows that versions exist which you may not read or which were not kept; nothing else about them is shown.
- Both texts of a replacement are shown one above the other; there is no line-by-line difference yet. In the app's panel the confirmation of "Accept replacement…" names the new title, not the note that is replaced: open the submission to compare first. The "Shorter by …" line also appears for very short texts (15 to 11 characters).
- The two history commands were tried against a real gateway in Docker Compose with five separately connected users, with the Go client; the Mac client prints the same text in the shared tests of both clients and was not tried by hand against a real gateway. Inside an agent run the refusal was not tried. The app's "Accept replacement…" action was checked in the tests of both clients, not by hand in the app's window.
- The hint after `kb submit` compares titles, not texts. It can be missing for an author with more than 1,000 submissions in one project, and for a submission that waited more than 30 days for its review.

**Remote settings and Docker Compose**
- When two reviewers accept the same version within about ten seconds, the second also sees "Accepted and published"; nothing is published twice. After a longer gap the page says that it was already accepted. The list shows the first 100 waiting submissions, oldest first, without paging.
- The pages were tried in one browser engine (Chromium). They use the CSS selector `:has()`: a browser older than about three years lacks it, and a very long name without spaces can then make the page scroll sideways at medium widths. The times on the Slovenian page (24-hour) and the text shown when the gateway gives no waiting time are covered by tests of the page's source only. The local settings page shares the narrow-screen layout; it was checked through the pages it shares with the remote one, not opened itself at a narrow width.
- A project that no longer exists is shown like a project without members ("none"); a team that no longer exists says "Not found". A device that is bound to a user but that the gateway has not seen is shown by its ID.
- The team gateway in Docker Compose is proven on Docker Desktop for Mac (`backup.sh`, the recovery rehearsal), not yet on a Linux host, with SELinux, rootless Docker, a remote Docker context or Podman. `backup.sh` does not estimate free disk space and does not notice a container that reaches a volume through a bind mount of its folder on the host. Restore does not inspect the content of the archives: a backup folder must be protected like the volumes themselves (it holds the provider keys). A backup with the admin profile (`compose.admin.yaml`) was not run in the automatic test; restore on a second, real Linux server was not tried yet.
- `minigw gw status` does not show the database schema number; after an update, check `projects: on` and your data as the documentation says.

**Claude Code plugin**
- A plugin older than 0.8.2 still shows the old hint and its skill lacks the step of `minigw serve install`: update the plugin first. The plugin's `release` branch moves only with a stable release.
- Windows plugin: a file named `minigw` (no extension) that something else put into the plugin's `bin` folder is left alone, and in Git Bash it is what `bin/minigw` runs instead of `minigw.exe` (the session-start hook still runs `minigw.exe`): remove that file. A client that cannot be read this time (opened exclusively by another process) is neither removed nor replaced: the start says so and tries again at the next session start (the hook does not run a file whose hash it cannot check).
- Windows plugin, wording only: offline, the start calls the plugin's own copy "the latest release" although it is only the latest one it has seen, and in a fresh folder without network it prints two lines where one would do.
- With knowledge base hooks switched on, each hook call on Windows takes about a second (the client file is hashed every time); with hooks off about 0.3 s. Measured on one computer. When the client program is replaced during a session (`brew upgrade`, an update with the MSI), the hooks stay silent until the next session start, because the replaced file no longer matches the checked one.
- The known problems of 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.11

### Fixed
- Windows plugin: an offline session start in a fresh data folder (nothing verified before) no longer removes a valid launcher (`bin/minigw` and `minigw.cmd` written by `minigw plugin install`): without any signed checksums there is no verdict, so the launcher stays, no `.verified` record is written and one line says the installed minigw cannot be verified yet and that the next start tries again. A launcher is removed only on a real verdict (checksums available and the client's hash is not in them, the client does not exist, or it is older than `MIN_CLIENT` while a better own copy is in place). The "is not the current release ... update it" advice is now printed only when checksums were fetched and verified in that very start; offline, with only stored checksums of an older release, nothing says what the current release is, so it says nothing (the plugin's own verified copy is used) or, when no client is usable, prints the same no-verdict line.
- A write-locked `minigw.exe` (the replacement `mv` fails) now gives one line per start, without the raw tool error. After the installed client is copied into place, `.asset.sha256` matches what is in place (only macOS reads that file, so a stale one never misled a later run on Windows or Linux).

### Known problems
- Windows plugin, wording only: offline, the start calls the plugin's own copy "the latest release" although it is only the latest one it has seen, and in a fresh folder without network it prints two lines where one would do.
- The known problems of 0.8.10 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.10

### Fixed
- "no answer from the gateway" now names its cause in both clients (connection refused, timed out after N s, connection reset, could not resolve the host, network unreachable, TLS), without any address or header.
- Upstream push: when the upstream note changed since the version our text corresponds to (its revision is above the one our last accepted text made), the note is a conflict and nothing is sent, also after a replacement was rejected as stale: the push no longer re-targets the newest revision unseen. `resolve --theirs` keeps our text unsent, `--ours` sends it as a replacement at the upstream's current revision for its reviewers. The remembered version is read from the accepted submission (no database change); a receipt whose version is not known is settled by content (the upstream note holds the text this gateway last sent: go on; else a conflict). A revision mismatch at submit is the same conflict, and the remembered version only moves up; the gateway trailer counts as proof only when it is exactly the one this gateway wrote for that note (built from the receipt and compared as text, nothing is parsed) at the very end of a text (a hub copy with text added on the trailer's line counts as changed); relay protection stays lenient (a received note whose last line looks like the trailer is never pushed onward) (an upstream note below it, restored from a backup, is a conflict).
- Security review of the next stable (nothing high; all fixed): the remote settings section of `docs/docker.md` now lists every route the remote listener allows, with its method, and says plainly that a remote accept publishes to the knowledge base, what cannot be accepted remotely and the two limits; a test keeps that table and the allow-list equal.
- The owner's review read of a replacement of a PRIVATE note (also over the remote page) writes the same `kb.owner.read` audit row as reading a private note or its history (the note id and the project or team, never the text).
- The default reason stored when a replacement whose note changed or is gone is rejected without a reason is one sentence for everyone who can read it (the author, a reviewer, the owner): "the note this would replace changed or no longer exists: read the project's notes and propose the change again". It carries no revision and no note state, whatever the author's access to the note is now.
- A remote accept of a replacement whose `currentRevision` is not the one in the fresh read is refused at the gateway's door (403, one `denied` row, not counted among the 30 decisions).
- The gateway's database is opened with `secure_delete` and the two full-text tables have FTS5's own secure-delete option on (a persistent option row set at every start, no database upgrade; SQLite 3.54): SQLite overwrites deleted text and deleted search postings at the write. In WAL mode the old page in `core.db` goes at the next checkpoint and the log's frames when they are overwritten or the log is truncated; earlier snapshots and backups keep what they held. `docs/how-it-works.md` says exactly this: it is not a forensic guarantee.
- Windows plugin, simplified: the session-start script no longer writes `bin/minigw` or `bin/minigw.cmd` and never moves `minigw.exe` aside, so there is no lock and no `.keep` any more (Git Bash treats `minigw` and `minigw.exe` as one file, which is what made that dance necessary). It uses the installed minigw only through the launcher that `minigw plugin install` writes (exact text, pointing at an installed `minigw.exe` whose hash is in the signed checksums and that is not older than `MIN_CLIENT`); otherwise it uses its own `bin/minigw.exe`, downloaded and verified, which Git Bash finds as `minigw`. A launcher that does not qualify (unverified, too old, pointing nowhere) is removed so that it cannot shadow the own copy. A newer release is downloaded under a temporary name and put in place with ONE move; the signed checksums are published before it, and the previous generation is kept (`SHA256SUMS.prev`), so a start killed at any step leaves a client that verifies. Leftovers of 0.8.1-0.8.9 (a `.keep` without `minigw.exe`, an `.aside.lock`) are cleaned up. A message about an old installed client now says it is the plugin's own copy that is used.

### Known problems
- Windows plugin: a file named `minigw` (no extension) that something else put into the plugin's `bin` folder is left alone, and in Git Bash it is what `bin/minigw` runs instead of `minigw.exe` (the session-start hook still runs `minigw.exe`): remove that file. A client that cannot be read this time (opened exclusively by another process) is neither removed nor replaced: the start says so and tries again at the next session start (the hook does not run a file whose hash it cannot check).
- Upstream push: the id of the submission that a newer one superseded is not kept locally, so a copy on the upstream whose last line names another earlier submission of ours still counts as our text.
- Upstream push: the upstream copy of a note that was deleted locally is shown among the upstream's notes again (its author is no longer known here).
- Upstream push: after this version is installed, a linked note whose last submission was rejected or is still waiting becomes a conflict at its next change when the upstream text is not exactly what this gateway last sent: resolve it once with `--ours` or `--theirs`.
- The cause of a rare "no answer from the gateway" in the Mac app's tests is not found; the message now names the cause (refused, timed out, …).
- Team gateway in Docker Compose is proven on Docker Desktop for Mac, not yet on a Linux host.
- The known problems of 0.8.9 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.9

A beta for gateways that push their notes to an upstream gateway (a team hub). It closes a way to get the same note twice upstream. No database upgrade. The upstream gateway and the pushing gateway should both be updated; mixed versions keep working (see Known problems).

### Fixed
- A pushed update that the upstream's reviewers REJECTED (for example because the note had changed there) is pushed again as a replacement of the SAME upstream note. Before, the next push sent it as a new note, and accepting that left a second copy next to the original. The same holds when the earlier submission is still waiting for its review or can no longer be read. Tried with two real gateways: every note ends up once.
- `minigw gw upstream link <project> <upstream> --auto` (or `--manual`, `--class …`) on an existing link keeps the link's destination. Only an explicitly different destination is refused: unlink and link again to change it. The client always sends the destination, read from the existing link, so an older gateway cannot move the link by mistake.

### Changed
- A note whose first submission still waits for its review upstream is not sent again until it is decided there. This is not a failure of the link and has no timer of its own: waiting notes are checked when a push happens anyway (`minigw gw upstream push`, or an automatic push after a local change). After the review the newest local text goes: as a replacement when it was accepted, as a new submission when it was rejected and the text differs. `status` says "N notes waited for a review upstream at the last push; they are checked at the next push (not a failure)".
- A run that sent something counts as a success also when one note was refused (for example "Too large"); the refusal is listed as "last refusal". Only a run where everything was refused is a failure with a back-off.
- A refusal that concerns one note no longer stops the whole run. The run stops only when the upstream says that the link as a whole cannot go on: the gateway's identity is not accepted, it has no role in the project ("the upstream project … was not found or this gateway's identity may not see it there"), or its role allows no hand-in at all ("may no longer hand in to this project upstream").
- When the upstream's note is deleted or no longer visible to the gateway's identity, or the earlier submission can no longer be read, the note becomes a conflict instead of being sent as new: `minigw gw upstream resolve <project> <id> --theirs` keeps the local text unsent, `--ours` looks again and replaces the upstream note if it is visible again, else sends a new note and says so. The Mini Gateway app's Upstream gateways panel names the three kinds of conflict.
- The report says `create` for a new submission and `replace` only when an upstream note is replaced.

### Known problems
- An automatic link whose only unsent note is refused every time (for example a note over the size limit) tries it again with a growing pause (up to about an hour) until the note changes.
- A conflict "deleted or no longer visible" is not cleared by itself when the note becomes visible again: `resolve --ours` replaces it then.
- `status` learns about a review upstream at the next push, not before.
- After restoring an OLDER local database, the gateway no longer knows what it sent after that backup: compare with the upstream before pushing (a push can create second copies).
- With an upstream gateway older than this release, an identity that was lowered to "reader" gets one refusal per changed note instead of one clear stop.
- The Mac client's part was checked with the shared tests of both clients, not by hand against two gateways. The retry of a replacement that the upstream refuses as outdated at hand-in was not provoked in the real round (it needs a race); tests cover it.
- The known problems of 0.8.8 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.8

A beta that closes the gap the last two betas worked toward: a reviewer who accepts a knowledge-base submission that REPLACES a note now sees the note's current text next to the new text, and accepts exactly that pair. No database upgrade.

### New
- Review of a replacement shows both texts, on every surface. `minigw kb review` and `minigw gw kb review` (both clients) print the sizes, then `Current text (will be replaced): note N  revision C …` with its text, then `New text: …`:
  `current: 212 lines, 11429 characters -> new: 141 lines, 7602 characters (lines shorter: 71, 33 %)`.
  The Mini Gateway app's Knowledge base panel shows both texts in the detail and the sizes on the queue row. The remote web page shows two separately labelled and coloured blocks, each with its label kept in view while you scroll, and says "Shorter by 66 lines (33 %)" when a rewrite drops text.
- The accept is bound to the pair. The review version (hash) of a replacement now also covers the replaced note's revision and text, and the accept carries the note's current revision. If the note was edited, restored, re-imported, changed in class or deleted after you read it, the accept is refused ("the submission changed since you read it") and nothing is published. A replacement written against an older revision of the note ("stale") or of a note that no longer exists cannot be accepted: reject it. When you reject such a one without typing a reason, the gateway stores the reason itself ("note N changed after this was written …: read the note and propose the change again"), so the author sees why with `minigw kb submissions`.
- The remote web page offers Accept for a replacement again (0.8.5 to 0.8.7 refused all of them), when the replaced note is unchanged and both texts are shown in full. The app's panel has a separate action "Accept replacement…" with a confirmation. After an accepted replacement the output names the saved revision and the commands that show and restore it (`note history`, `note restore`).
- Hidden characters in the note's CURRENT text do not block a replacement: the review says how many there are and that accepting removes them from the note; the old text stays in the note's history as it was. Hidden characters in the NEW text still cannot be accepted.
- The audit entry of an accepted replacement says that a note was replaced and which revision was saved (numbers only).

### Known problems
- Reviewers who use a client older than 0.8.8 (the stable 0.8.0 from Homebrew, `install.sh` or the plugin) cannot accept a replacement until the next stable release. The refusal says so and names the ways out: a 0.8.8 or newer beta client, the Mini Gateway app's panel or the remote settings page (they come with the gateway), or reject it. Accepting a submission that replaces nothing works as before. A new client with a gateway older than 0.8.8 shows `Current text: not shown (the gateway is older than 0.8.8)` and refuses the accept: update the gateway.
- Both texts are shown one above the other; there is no line-by-line difference yet. In the app's panel the confirmation of "Accept replacement…" names the new title, not the note that is replaced: open the submission to compare first.
- A local gateway that pushes notes to an upstream gateway: when a pushed update is REJECTED upstream (for example as stale) and the note is then changed again locally, the next push sends it as a NEW note; accepting that upstream creates a second note next to the original. Until this is fixed (next beta): after rejecting a pushed update on the upstream gateway, do not accept a later submission of the same note that shows no "replaces"; fix the note upstream by hand or accept the next one only if it replaces the note.
- The app's "Accept replacement…" action was checked in the tests of both clients, not yet by hand in the app's window. The remote page was tried in one browser engine (Chromium).
- The "Shorter by …" line also appears for very short texts (15 to 11 characters).
- The known problems of 0.8.7 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.7

A beta that makes the saved versions of notes (kept since 0.8.6) usable: you can look at them and put one back. The gateway and the client both change; no database upgrade.

### New
- `minigw note history <id>` lists the saved versions of a note (newest first, at most 100; `--revision n` shows one with its text, `--json` prints the answer). Each row says when the version was WRITTEN and by whom, and when it was REPLACED and by whom or by what (an edit, an accepted submission with its reviewer, an import, a restore), with the time zone:
  `revision 5  written 2026-11-17 09:12 UTC by Ana  replaced 2026-11-18 14:02 UTC by Tilen  structure  finding  1200 bytes  Invoice numbering`.
  The last good version of a ruined note is the one that was replaced by the edit that ruined it, and `minigw note restore <id> --revision n` puts a saved version back as a new revision. The gateway owner has the same two commands as `minigw gw kb history` and `minigw gw kb restore`. Both clients (Go and Mac) print the same text.
- Who may read which saved version: you see a saved version only if you may read the note now and your access also allows the class the version had then. A reader sees structure versions (and private ones of their own note), a developer also data, a project admin everything, a reader through a grant only structure and data of the granted project, a team member structure and data of the team's notes. A version you may not read is left out: no text, title, class, size or count of it is shown. The owner sees all; reading a private version is logged like any owner read of a private note.
- Restore is for project admins (a team assignment counts) and the gateway owner, not for the note's author. It never rewrites history: the text becomes a new revision, the version it replaces is saved first, and the note keeps its CURRENT class unless you ask for the saved one with `--class` (which can widen who reads it). Without a terminal, `restore` needs `--current <revision>` (the revision you saw); a note that changed in the meantime is refused, never overwritten. The restore is audited (`note.restore`, no text) and the new revision records `restoredFromRevision`.
- `note history` and `note restore` are not available inside an agent run, and not through the remote web settings.

### Fixed
- A note title that contains a line break could forge a line in `minigw note list`, `note read`, search results and FAQ lists (and in the new history list). Titles in one-line rows are now printed on one line, in both clients.
- Tests only: the loopback WebDAV server of the mount tests survives a transient accept error, a timed-out shell command takes its child processes with it, and a dead test server is reported at once. A release test run had stalled on this.

### Known problems
- The two commands were tried against a real gateway in Docker Compose with five separately connected users (admin, developer, reader, a member of another project with a grant, a team member), with the Go client. The Mac client prints the same text in the shared tests of both clients; it was not tried by hand against a real gateway. Inside an agent run the refusal was not tried (the test gateway had no agents).
- A client 0.8.7 with a gateway older than 0.8.7: `note history` and `note restore` say that the gateway must be updated. A client older than 0.8.7 does not know the two commands.
- A note that is a copy from an upstream gateway has its saved versions on that gateway; the commands say so. This was not tried with two real gateways.
- For a team note, `note history` and `note restore` need `--team` like `note edit`.
- There is still no command to remove one saved version, and the review of a replacement does not show before and after yet (the browser still does not offer accept for a replacement).
- Revision numbers are kept, so a gap in the numbers of `note history` shows that versions exist which you may not read or which were not kept; nothing else about them is shown.
- Within a project the older saved versions of any note go first when the project's history budget is reached, so one member who edits in a loop can push out the older saved versions of other notes in the same project.
- The known problems of 0.8.6 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.6

A beta that stops a silent loss in the knowledge base: until now an edit, an accepted replacement or a re-import overwrote a note's text and the old text was kept nowhere. **This beta upgrades the gateway's database (schema 25 to 26). Going back to 0.8.5 or older afterwards needs the snapshot or backup made before the update** (see "Going back" in `docs/install.md`; in Docker Compose run `backup.sh --stop` before changing the version).

### New
- The gateway keeps the previous version of a note before an edit, an accepted replacement or a re-import overwrites it: title, text, class, kind and where it came from. Every overwrite saves the previous version: at most 100 versions per note, 8 MiB per note and 500 MiB per project. When a note has more than 100 saved versions, versions from a quick series of edits by one person (each less than ten minutes after the one before) go first, the shortest-lived of them first, then the oldest: a storm of rewrites keeps the version from before it, the newest one, and the versions that stood longest. An overwrite is never refused because of these limits.
- Nothing shows or restores these versions yet: the commands `minigw note history` and `minigw note restore` come in the next beta. From this beta on, the text is no longer lost.
- `minigw kb submissions` (both clients) prints the reason under a rejected submission (`    reason: <text>`), so the author learns why it was rejected; `--json` is unchanged. After `minigw kb submit`, a line says so when a submission with the same title in the same project was rejected in the last 30 days. The plugin's notes skill tells a session to read the reason before handing in again, and not to hand in the same text unchanged.

### Known problems
- Text that was removed from a note, also something pasted by mistake, and the text of a note that was later made private, stays in the note's saved versions until it leaves the 100 or the note is deleted. That removes it from the database; backups made before still hold it. There is no command yet to remove one saved version.
- Many people editing one note in turns, or a long series of edits each ten minutes or more apart, can push its oldest saved version out of the 100. Within a project, the oldest saved versions of any note go first when the project's 500 MiB are reached. The limits count the stored text, not the size of the database file.
- Export, bundles and what a gateway sends upstream carry the current text only; a database backup carries the saved versions.
- The remote "Review" page and `minigw gw kb review` still do not show the old text next to a replacement (the browser still does not offer accept for one). That comes after the history commands.
- The hint after `kb submit` compares titles, not texts. It can be missing for an author with more than 1,000 submissions in one project, and for a submission that waited more than 30 days for its review.
- The known problems of 0.8.5 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.5

A beta toward a team gateway in Docker Compose: the owner can review what the team hands in to the knowledge base from a browser. This is the first action in the remote settings, besides providers and devices, that changes something.

### New
- The remote settings page has a "Review" page. It lists the knowledge-base submissions that wait for review in all projects and teams, shows one exactly as the owner's review read gives it, and accepts or rejects exactly the version that was read, after a confirmation view. The published note is the text that was shown: if the submission changed in between, the accept is refused and the new text is shown.
- What cannot be accepted from the browser, by the page and by the gateway itself: a submission with hidden characters (it can only be rejected), a long one (over 20,000 characters) and one that replaces an existing note. Those are reviewed on the server; reject works everywhere. The reason for a reject is one line of at most 1024 bytes.
- Limits: at most 30 decisions in 10 minutes for the whole listener; the page then says from when you can continue. Decisions that the knowledge base itself refuses (the text changed since the read, an unknown number) count toward the 30. Requests with a malformed body do not; they have their own limit of 300 in 10 minutes.
- The gateway's remote listener and the proxy allow four more routes (`GET kb/submissions`, `GET kb/submissions/{id}`, `POST kb/submissions/{id}/accept`, `POST …/reject`). The request body may hold exactly one key, spelled exactly. Every decision, also a refused one, is written to the remote audit log with the submission's number and the address it came from.
- After updating, copy the release's `deploy/docker/Caddyfile` into your deployment folder and run `docker compose restart admin-proxy`; until then the page shows a hint instead of the list.

### Known problems
- A submission that replaces a note: accepting it (on the server) overwrites the note's text, and the old text is not kept anywhere. Neither the page nor `minigw gw kb review` shows the old text next to the new one: read the note first (`minigw note read <id>`). This is why the browser does not offer accept for replacements.
- The author of a rejected submission sees the reason only with `minigw kb submissions --json`; the plain list says "rejected".
- When two reviewers accept the same version within about ten seconds, the second also sees "Accepted and published"; nothing is published twice. After a longer gap the page says that it was already accepted.
- The list shows the first 100 waiting submissions, oldest first, without paging.
- The times on the Slovenian page (24-hour) and the text shown when the gateway gives no waiting time were changed after the last check in a browser and are covered by tests of the page's source only.
- The page was tried in one browser engine (Chromium).
- The known problems of 0.8.4 to 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.4

A beta toward a team gateway in Docker Compose: the owner sees from a browser who has access to what.

### New
- The remote "Team (read-only)" page opens a team (its members and what it assigns) and a project (who has access and why: directly or through which team) on a click or Enter. The listener and the proxy allow three more `GET` routes, each with exactly one numeric id (`/teams/{id}`, `/teams/{id}/assignments`, `/projects/{id}/members`). After updating, copy the release's `deploy/docker/Caddyfile` into your deployment folder and run `docker compose restart admin-proxy`; until then the page shows the hint instead of the detail.
- Narrow screens (settings page, local and remote): below 700 px the navigation sits above the content, so the content gets the full width; on the remote Team page a value drops under its label when the row is narrow.

### Known problems
- The local settings page shares the new layout for narrow screens. It was checked through the pages it shares with the remote one (General, Devices, providers), not opened itself at a narrow width.
- A project that no longer exists is shown like a project without members ("none"); a team that no longer exists says "Not found".
- The page was tried in one browser engine (Chromium). It uses the CSS selector `:has()`: a browser older than about three years lacks it, and a very long name without spaces can then make the page scroll sideways at medium widths.
- The known problems of 0.8.3, 0.8.2, 0.8.1 and 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.3

A beta toward a team gateway in Docker Compose: the owner can look at the team's setup from a browser.

### New
- Remote web settings (the Compose admin profile) show users, the user each device is bound to, teams and projects, read-only, on a new page "Team (read-only)". Changes stay with `docker compose exec minigw minigw gw …`; the page says so. The gateway's remote listener and the proxy allow exactly four more `GET` routes (`users`, `bindings`, `teams`, `projects`); a test keeps the two lists equal and fails when the `Caddyfile` gets a rule the test does not know. Requests with a method-override header are refused.
- After an update, copy the release's `deploy/docker/Caddyfile` (and the compose files) into your deployment folder and run `docker compose restart admin-proxy`. With an older `Caddyfile` the new page shows a hint that says this, instead of data. `docs/docker.md` lists the files that belong to a version.

### Known problems
- The new page was checked by tests of the routes, by reading and by a security review, not yet by a person in a real browser.
- The page shows the lists only: who is a member of which project, and which team gives which role, still need `minigw gw member` and `minigw gw team` on the server.
- A device that is bound to a user but that the gateway has not seen is shown by its ID.
- The known problems of 0.8.2, 0.8.1 and 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.2

A beta toward a team gateway in Docker Compose. The gateway and client programs change in one message only; the rest is a rehearsal script and documentation.

### New
- `scripts/compose-recovery-e2e.sh` rehearses the three dangerous moments of a Compose deployment, and runs before every release: an update from the oldest release with container mode (0.7.6, database schema 12) to this version with a populated database (users, a team, a project with members, notes, a submission waiting for review, a provider): the database is upgraded and the same data is listed and found by search; going back to the old version with the backup made before the update; a restore into a separate project from the backup folder alone. An old version that meets a database upgraded by a newer one refuses it and leaves the file unchanged.
- `docs/docker.md`, "Updating and going back": the exact commands, the message an old version prints for a newer database, and what the snapshot inside the volume is for (it is not a backup).

### Fixed
- `minigw serve install` no longer answers "already up to date" when the installed gateway has the same version as the `minigw` that runs it. It says that it installs the version of the `minigw` that runs it, and how to get a newer release: update the Claude Code plugin and run `/minigw:gw update` again, or install the newer MSI. The plugin's `gw` skill and `docs/install.md` describe the same step. (Found in the first real update to 0.8.0 on Windows: with an old plugin the session start pointed to an update that then did nothing.)

### Known problems
- A plugin older than 0.8.2 still shows the old hint and its skill lacks the step above: update the plugin first (`claude plugin marketplace update mini-gateway`, `claude plugin update minigw@mini-gateway`). The plugin's `release` branch moves only with a stable release.
- The rehearsal runs with Docker Desktop on a Mac. A Linux server, SELinux, rootless Docker, a remote Docker context and Podman were not tried.
- `minigw gw status` does not show the database schema number; after an update, check `projects: on` and your data as the documentation says.
- The known problems of 0.8.1 and 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.1

A beta after stable 0.8.0. It fixes two defects of 0.8.0 found in the first real updates and deployments, and starts the work on a team gateway in Docker Compose. The gateway and client programs are unchanged except for the version; the changes are in the plugin's scripts, the Compose files and the documentation.

### Fixed
- Docker Compose backup: the backup recipe in `docs/docker.md` built the volume name from the folder name, while the Compose project is named `minigw` by default. In a folder such as `deploy/docker` it could archive an empty, newly created volume. Backups made with that recipe must be checked: `tar -tf minigw-config.tar | grep serve.json` has to print the file; if it prints nothing, that backup is empty.
- Windows plugin: writing or removing the `minigw` launcher in the plugin folder can no longer replace or delete the plugin's own `minigw.exe` (Git Bash treats `minigw` and `minigw.exe` as one file). In 0.8.0 the own copy could be lost when an installed client appeared, or before an update download that then failed. Two sessions that start at the same moment no longer disturb each other's files, and leftover checksum files are removed.
- The plugin's knowledge base hook no longer runs a `minigw` that the install step has not checked, and runs nothing while hooks are off. `kb-hook.sh` exits silently unless `minigw kb hooks on` is set for at least one project, and then runs only the file whose SHA-256 matches the record `bin/.verified` that `install-minigw.sh` writes when it has verified a client (and removes when it rejects one). A replaced or not yet checked file, a missing record or a missing `sha256sum`/`shasum` means no run. A client that was kept without verification (a computer without `ssh-keygen`) gets no record, so the hooks stay silent there.

### New
- `deploy/docker/backup.sh` backs up and restores both volumes of a gateway in Docker Compose (it needs Python 3 on the server). The volume names come from Compose itself. It refuses a project without containers (unless `--offline`), a gateway container that uses other volumes than Compose resolves, any other container that is not stopped and mounts the volumes, an empty source, and a restore into a volume that is not empty or into another project (unless `--into-project`). `--stop` stops the services for the copy and starts again only those that ran before. The archives are streamed through a helper container (no folder of the server is mounted into it), Docker's own error messages are shown, and a backup that did not finish is removed. See "Backup" in `docs/docker.md`.
- Container logs of the Compose deployment are bounded (`json-file`, 10 MB, 3 files).
- `docs/docker.md`: first setup on a team server step by step, what the remote web settings cover today (providers and devices), and what to do before an update.

### Known problems
- Windows, Mini Gateway installed with the MSI and the plugin still at 0.7.0: the session start says "0.8.0 is available: /minigw:gw update", but that command answers "already up to date". Update the plugin first (it updates by itself when the marketplace has automatic updates on; otherwise `claude plugin marketplace update mini-gateway` and `claude plugin update minigw@mini-gateway`), then run `/minigw:gw update`; or install the MSI of the new release by hand.
- Windows: after an update with the MSI, its `minigw plugin install` step writes the launchers `minigw` and `minigw.cmd` into the plugin folder next to the plugin's own `minigw.exe`. Both are the same version then; the plugin's script keeps the own copy and uses the launcher.
- With knowledge base hooks switched on, each hook call on Windows takes about a second (the client file is hashed every time); with hooks off about 0.3 s. Measured on one computer.
- `backup.sh` does not notice a container that reaches a volume through a bind mount of its folder on the host. Restore does not inspect the content of the archives: a backup folder must be protected like the volumes themselves (it holds the provider keys). A backup with the admin profile (`compose.admin.yaml`) was not run in the automatic test; restore on a second, real Linux server was not tried yet.
- `backup.sh` was run only with Docker Desktop on a Mac: not yet on a Linux server, with SELinux, rootless Docker, a remote Docker context or Podman. It does not estimate free disk space before the copy.
- With knowledge base hooks on: when the client program is replaced during a session (`brew upgrade`, an update with the MSI), the hooks stay silent until the next session start, because the replaced file no longer matches the checked one.
- Updating a Compose deployment from one version to the next with a database upgrade, and going back, was not rehearsed yet.
- The known problems of 0.8.0 still apply.

## 0.8.0

Mini Gateway 0.8.0 is a stable release. It collects the 39 beta versions 0.7.1 to 0.7.39, which stable users never saw, and is the first stable release since 0.7.0. It is the same product code as beta 0.7.39; only tests and the version number differ. The sections below describe what 0.8.0 is, not how each beta got there. Read **Changed**, **Upgrading from 0.7.0** and **Known problems** before you update a gateway that other people use.

### New
**Teams and users**
- Teams: the owner groups users and projects into teams with `minigw gw team add|list|rename|archive|unarchive`, `minigw gw team member add|remove <team> <user>` and `minigw gw team project add|remove <team> <project>`. A project belongs to at most one team; a user can be in several. Members see their teams with `minigw team list|show <team>`.
- Roles through a team: `minigw gw team access set <team> <project> <user> <role>` (also `remove` and `list <team>`) gives a team member a role in one of the team's projects. It makes the same membership as `minigw gw member`; when a user has several, the strongest role wins. Removing the user from the team, the project from the team, or archiving the team takes the access away at once. `minigw project list` shows the role and where it comes from.
- Team path label: `minigw gw team path <team> <label>`; `minigw project list --path acme` lists your projects of the teams whose label starts with it. A label never gives access.
- Team notes: a team has notes of its own. Every member reads them; a member who is admin of at least one of the team's projects writes them (`structure` and `data` only, never `private`). Use `--team <team>` instead of a project on `minigw kb submit|import|export|search|faq` and `minigw note list|read|push|edit|rm`.
- Project git identity: `minigw project remote add --here` and `minigw gw project remote add <project> <url>` register a project's git remotes; `minigw project link` without a slug then finds the project from the folder's `origin`. Credentials in the URL are removed on your computer first.
- Project dependencies: `minigw gw project depends add|remove|list <project> <other>`, or `--team <team>` for a team, at most 16 per project. A search of a project also covers what it depends on, only as far as you could read it directly. A dependency is a search hint, never a permission. `minigw project depends [<project>]` lists them.
- Device messages: when a device has no project access, the answer says which step is missing and what the owner runs (`minigw gw devices allow DEVICE-ID`, `minigw gw bind DEVICE-ID USER`).

**Knowledge base**
- Notes record who wrote them and from where (user, device, project, source, content hash, times, reviewer); `minigw note read <id> --json` shows it. A note edited by someone else shows who changed it.
- Submissions: `minigw kb submit <project> <file|->` proposes a note instead of publishing it (`--replaces <id> --revision <n>` for a new revision, `--kind faq` for a question and a short answer). `minigw kb submissions` lists your own, `minigw kb faq` the published questions. `minigw note push` still publishes directly. A pending submission is visible only to its author and the reviewers.
- Review: the owner uses `minigw gw kb review`, `minigw gw kb accept <id> --version <hash>` and `minigw gw kb reject <id> [--reason text]`. An admin of the project uses the same words without `gw`: `minigw kb review [<project>|--team <team>]`, `minigw kb accept <id> --version <hash>`, `minigw kb reject <id>`. Nobody reviews their own submission. An accept names the whole version hash that the reviewer read; if the writer changed the text since, it is refused and you review again. Without `--version` the command shows the text and asks in a terminal.
- Hidden characters: a submission with invisible or direction-changing characters is refused at once, with line, column and code point; reviews leave them out and say so, and such a text cannot be accepted. Emoji are visible text and are allowed. Windows line endings are stored as plain line ends.
- Search: `minigw kb search [<project>] <query> [--json]` searches the published notes you may read (title and text, at most 20 hits with snippets). Hits of the project asked come first, then its team's notes (`from team:<name>`), then dependencies (`from project:<slug>`).
- Agent runs: an agent started by the gateway can search and read the notes of its run's project (never `private`, never pending submissions); note texts are not put into its context.
- MCP server: `minigw kb mcp [--project <slug>]` is a read-only MCP server with the tools `kb_search` and `note_read`, with your rights; `--print-config` prints the entry for the MCP client. Results are marked as untrusted reference content and cleaned of control characters.
- Markdown copy: `minigw kb export <project> <dir>` writes one Markdown file per published note you may read; `minigw kb import <project> <dir|file.md> [--class c] [--submit]` creates notes from Markdown files. A second import updates or skips, it does not duplicate.
- Hooks in the plugin, off by default: `minigw kb hooks on|off|status [<project>]` switches, per project and on this computer, hooks that tell a session about the project's knowledge base and, once after a commit or a compaction, ask the model to hand in at most three short entries with `minigw kb submit`. Each hand-in goes through Claude Code's Bash prompt and the review. See "Hand findings in from a session" in the usage guide.
- Sessions of a project, without any prompt or answer text: `minigw project sessions [<project>] [--days n] [--model m] [--device d] [--json]`. A member sees own sessions; a project admin sees all of the project.

**Gateways that talk to each other**
- Copy a team to another gateway: `minigw gw export --team <team> [--project p ...] [--with-private] <file.zip>` and, on the other gateway, `minigw gw import --source <name> <file.zip> [--dry-run] [--map old=new ...]`. It carries the team, projects, members, roles and notes, no devices, tokens, keys or usage. A one-way copy, not a sync, not a backup. An import merges only into objects the same `--source` created before; a name that matches another local project, team or user stops it and lists the conflicts, and `--adopt project:<slug>,team:<name>,user:<name>` merges into exactly the ones you list.
- Upstream gateways: `minigw gw upstream add <name> <address>` (the upstream's Copy Token on stdin) makes this gateway a client of another one, with a device key of its own; the upstream's owner approves the device, binds it to a user and gives that user a role. `gw upstream link <project> <name>` (or `--team`), `status`, `unlink`, `list` and `remove` manage links.
- Push: `minigw gw upstream push [<project>] [--dry-run]` sends the published notes of a linked project as submissions for the upstream's reviewers; private notes only with `--with-private`. `--auto` on the link makes the gateway push by itself after a quiet minute, with back-off after failures; `--manual` is the default. A note that arrived from another gateway is never pushed onward.
- Publish mode: `minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --mode publish` writes notes on the upstream directly. If the note moved on there, our change is not written but recorded as a conflict; `minigw gw upstream resolve <project> <note> --theirs|--ours [--upstream <name>]` settles it (`--ours` sends a new submission, never an overwrite). A link's mode cannot change after it has sent notes.
- Read mirror: `minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --read [--read-class structure[,data]] [--read-sources <label,...>] --accept-reach` lets this gateway read an upstream's notes into a local mirror (`--no-read` turns it off). `minigw gw upstream pull [<project>|--team <team>] [--dry-run]` pulls by hand; with reading on, the gateway pulls every 15 minutes (`--pull-every <minutes>`, 5 to 1440). Members' search, `note read`, `kb mcp` and agent runs include the mirror, quoted as reference data from another gateway, never as instructions, never `private`, and hidden after 7 days without a complete pull.
- The Mac app and the settings page of `minigw serve` have an Upstream gateways panel for all of this.

**Usage and models**
- Labels on a run: `--minigw-phase` (plan, do, check, act), `--minigw-activity` (feature, bugfix, security, refactor, docs, test, release, research, ops) and `--minigw-task` (or `MINIGW_PHASE`, `MINIGW_ACTIVITY`, `MINIGW_TASK`). A label never refuses or delays a run. In a linked project inside a git repository an empty task is filled with the branch name. The plugin skills for models set phase and activity when they know them.
- Reports: `minigw gw usage --by phase|activity|task|project|model` (one or two, comma separated), the filters `--phase`, `--activity`, `--task` and `--label-source given|branch|model`, and a "cached in" column for tokens read from a provider's prompt cache.
- Model labels: when the owner turns it on (off by default), a worker fills the labels of unlabelled usage with a local model: Foundry Local on Windows and Linux, the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) on the Mac. Such a label is stored with its source `model` and shown as its own row (`do (model)`). The Model Labels pane has the settings and the agreement list. `minigw gw labels eval --file <examples.jsonl> [--show-errors]` checks the model against hand-labelled examples and stores nothing. A label is data, never a permission.
- `minigw running` lists your device's requests that run on the gateway now. A Codex, Grok or Copilot run (on the Mac: Codex and Copilot) shows its steps in `minigw -p` as they happen.
- Providers: xAI works as a provider (base URL `https://api.x.ai`). `--minigw-tools=edit` lets a provider model in the local Claude Code use Edit and Write inside the current folder (never `.git`, hooks, `.claude` settings); not on Windows clients. The owner sets the highest level per provider or model with `minigw gw provider tools <name> [none|read|edit|default] [--model m]`.
- `--minigw-profile <name>` or `MINIGW_PROFILE=<name>` picks a profile; a refusal from a gateway says which profile and address it came from when you have more than one.

**Running the gateway**
- Docker Compose (`deploy/docker`, [docker.md](docker.md)): a Linux gateway for providers only, built from the signed GitHub release (signature and checksum are checked). Claude CLI, Codex, Grok, Copilot and Foundry Local are off in container mode. A provider can be a Compose service name (`http://litellm:4000`, with `--insecure`) when it resolves only to private addresses. `minigw serve healthcheck` serves the Docker `HEALTHCHECK`. Tried on arm64 and, through emulation, amd64.
- Remote settings, off by default: an optional listener behind mutual TLS and a password (`minigw gw settings password`). It serves providers, models and devices only; changes that carry a key's destination or raise tool levels need a local session. For Compose, `deploy/docker/compose.admin.yaml` puts it behind an HTTPS-only Caddy proxy, with `deploy/docker/admin-certs.sh` for the certificates (see [docker.md](docker.md#remote-settings-from-a-browser-admin-profile)).
- Secrets: `minigw gw status` prints a `secrets:` line that says where secrets are kept (Credential Manager, Secret Service, Keychain, files), and `minigw status` a `Secrets:` line. A credential store that fails is now an error, not a quiet fall-back to `gateway-keys.json`.
- Shutdown (SIGTERM or Ctrl+C) of `minigw serve` stops new requests, lets running ones finish for up to 30 seconds, then closes the database.
- Backups: `minigw gw core restore` accepts only a backup that is exactly what this program's migrations make for its schema (every table, index, trigger and foreign key is compared); it migrates a staged copy, rebuilds the search index and hides upstream mirrors until a pull completes. The snapshot before an upgrade is `core-<schema>-<time>.db` in the `core` folder; the newest three are kept, and the oldest snapshot of the lowest schema for 30 days.
- A provider whose models are still loading shows "models loading", one that failed shows the reason, a disabled one shows "disabled" in `minigw gw status`.

**Panels and the plugin**
- Admin panels, drawn natively on the Mac (Settings window) and in the browser settings page of `minigw serve`, in English and Slovenian: Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base (review with Accept and Reject), Upstream gateways and Model Labels. Every access-changing action asks first. Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base panels.
- The plugin on Linux and Windows runs only a `minigw` it has verified against the signed `SHA256SUMS` of a release; it downloads the release's `minigw` once into its own folder when the installed one is not in those sums. On the Mac it checks the Developer ID signature. Inside Claude Code a plain `minigw` is the plugin's own copy. `/minigw:gw update` on a Mac also upgrades the `minigw` client cask. The session start says when the installed `minigw` is old and how to update it.
- The team onboarding page ([team-onboarding.md](team-onboarding.md)) and a parity table of macOS, Windows, Linux and Docker ([parity.md](parity.md)) are new.
- After an agent's work the end-of-run git report names mode and type changes (a symbolic link or an executable file added, changed or deleted), for example `link 120000->100644`.

### Changed
- Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway (the Mini Gateway app). It could not start since 0.6.0. Every Grok request answers 400 "Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway. Use another agent, or Grok through minigw serve on Windows or Linux."; the Mac Settings have no Grok pane. Grok through `minigw serve` on Windows and Linux works, and a Mac client can use its models.
- Accepting a submission needs the version that was read (`--version <hash>`). An older `minigw` cannot accept on a 0.8.0 gateway until it is updated.
- A grant gives no read access while the reading project is archived. An admin of an archived project no longer writes or reviews its team's own notes.
- A note that arrived from another gateway is not pushed onward, so two gateways linked both ways no longer copy each other's notes for ever.
- Installing `minigw` from the Mac app gives the token on standard input.
