- On Linux and Windows the plugin uses only its own verified `minigw` (see New). The plugin's session start takes longer on Windows (about 4.5 seconds against 2.6 on the test computer).
- Docs: an up to date parity table and onboarding page; the plugin's skills describe the current commands.

### Security
- Notes: the gateway checks the permission before it loads a note's text, and again before it repeats a saved answer to a retried request. A note that is missing and one you may not see give the same answer.
- Restoring a backup (offline, by the owner) could install a crafted file that hid a trigger under the name of an automatic index, or had lost foreign keys, cascades or checks, and so keep access that could not be revoked. A restore now refuses anything that is not exactly what this program makes, refuses rows that break a foreign key, and refuses a pre-schema-15 backup with a project in the range reserved for team segments.
- A team bundle import could take over local projects, users and teams that it only matched by name (an existing user could become team admin). It now merges only into objects the same `--source` created, or listed with `--adopt`. Still import only bundles you trust. Bundle import also refuses field names that match only after Unicode case folding, oversized note files and unlisted entries before decompressing, and `kb export` writes through the opened folder and refuses a destination that is a symbolic link.
- Review: the accept names the whole version hash, which also covers the provenance; the reviewer is never shown text they cannot see (hidden characters are left out and block the accept); a tab is shown; the panel shows the version of the row it accepts. `kb submit --replaces` does not reveal whether a note id exists elsewhere. A replacement keeps the kind of the note it replaces.
- The hand-in request of the hooks put a title in double quotes on the command line, so a title with `$(...)` or backticks copied from a hostile repository could run a command. The first line of the text on stdin is the title now.
- Git on your computer and on the gateway: calls in a repository minigw does not control refuse more of what that repository's own configuration could ask git to do (programs and transports), among them fetching the agent's branch back in worktree mode.
- Git watch after an agent's work: a protected path that the agent staged (`.envrc`, a hooks folder, `.claude/settings`) is removed from the index also when refs or history are too large to read; when a read of git fails, minigw warns with the path and the command to unstage it instead of removing it. The command is printed only for plain path names, and names are printed with control and direction characters written out.
- Providers: the Mac no longer follows HTTP redirects with a prompt; an http provider (`--insecure`, container service name) is never reached through a proxy; a Compose service name must resolve only to private addresses (not link-local such as the cloud metadata address, loopback, multicast) at add time and at every connection.
- Reading an upstream: everything an upstream sends is checked against its manifest and sanitised, quoted as reference data, hidden from members when stale or after a restore, and the mirror is deleted when the upstream refuses the device. Mac: the helper's upstream callouts are accepted only for the running route and the upstream it names.
- A project admin's usage report no longer includes the gateway-wide records of lost usage events.
- The share long poll and `kb export`/`import` back off and give up on a gateway that keeps answering 429 (30 attempts or 10 minutes).
- Mac: a repository that tracks symbolic links gets no commits by the agent through a mount (see Fixed).

### Fixed
- Mac, Claude CLI in direct mode on a remote client's mount: a file the agent created and committed was recorded as executable (100755), and a tracked symbolic link was committed as a plain file because the mount shows a link as a file. Files are now 100644 (an executable file in the repository keeps 100755), and a repository that tracks symbolic links gets no commits by the agent through the mount; the client decides at share time and prints why (an older client gets no commits through the mount).
- A long agent request that is silent could be dropped by Claude Code: the gateway now sends an empty text delta with every ping (#131).
- Mac gateway: every running Claude CLI agent held a thread of the app's shared pool while reading its error output, so with as many agent runs as the Mac has cores the gateway could stop answering. It has its own thread now; so has the listing of the temp folder by `minigw run`.
- Mac gateway: shares were dropped all at once when the gateway itself stalled or the Mac slept, and the next request of a running session failed. A late check now gives every share a fresh minute.
- A second sync conflict on the same file overwrote the first saved copy; further copies are `<name>.minigw-conflict-2`, `-3`, ... (up to 99).
- Mac: a provider's quota refresh waited for the rest of a large body (about a minute on a busy machine); it stops at the 1 MB limit.
- A read-only Codex run on a mounted folder (`minigw --minigw-ro`, direct mode) could not read that folder.
- Folder sync says so when the root `.gitignore` keeps top-level entries out of the copy.
- `minigw gw status` and the admin commands say that the gateway is starting while its database is opened or migrated, instead of "not running". A long database upgrade on the Mac is waited for (the app says "upgrading the database (schema A to B)"), and a snapshot written during it counts only when complete.
- Settings page of `minigw serve`: opening the Foundry Local pane without Foundry Local no longer logs a console error (the pane says it is not installed).
- Mac: `minigw status` prints the `Secrets:` line the Windows and Linux client has.

### Upgrading from 0.7.0
- The database schema goes from 8 to 25 at the first start. A snapshot `core-8-<time>.db` is written next to the database first and kept for 30 days.
- Update clients and gateways both. A 0.7.0 client against a 0.8.0 gateway treats `kb ...` and `team ...` as a prompt for an agent run and prints nothing useful. A 0.8.0 client against a 0.7.0 gateway says which version the gateway needs (for example "this gateway is 0.7.0; kb needs 0.7.10 or newer: update the gateway").
- The Claude Code plugin updates itself. Until the installed `minigw` is updated, it uses its own verified client and prints one line at the start of the session: `minigw plugin: using its own minigw <version>; the installed minigw is older than <needed> or its version could not be read: update it (<how>)`. To update the client: on a Mac `brew upgrade --cask` for the `minigw` cask, or run the installer again; on Windows run the new `minigw-windows-<arch>.msi` from the release page; on Linux run `install.sh` again (all in docs/install.md).
- Going back: 0.7.0 refuses the newer database (projects and notes answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models work). Install 0.7.0, stop the gateway and run `minigw gw core restore <core>/core-8-<time>.db --yes`; what was written after the upgrade is lost. The steps are in docs/install.md, "Going back to 0.7.0". It was tried with `minigw serve` on a Mac; the Mac app's steps were read from the code, not tried.

### Known problems
- A file name with a character that an HFS+ volume ignores (APFS does not) in front of a leading dot is not found by the separate read of protected paths (used only when the index listing is too large).
- An agent that may write to the folder directly (direct mode) can point git's `HEAD` at a branch that does not exist. minigw then cannot tell which protected path to reset, leaves a protected path that the agent staged in the index and warns, with the path and the command to unstage it. Run that command before you commit.
- `minigw plugin install` writes the plugin's launchers for the `minigw` that ran it, without the release check.
- Going back to 0.7.0 on Windows and Linux: the commands are the same as on the Mac but were not run there.
- The refusal of a submission with hidden characters was tried with the Go client; the Mac client passes the gateway's message through and was not tried.
- The "update the gateway" hint covers the kb and team commands, not `gw upstream` and `gw labels` (those talk to the gateway on the same computer).
- The extra snapshot that is kept for 30 days is the oldest one of the lowest schema present: restoring an even older database changes which one that is, and a clock change can end the 30 days early.
- An emoji joined with a zero-width joiner is shown and accepted when both sides are pictographs, at most seven joiners in a row; a joiner or a variation selector anywhere else is still a hidden character.
- A note that came from another gateway is recognised by the line the sending gateway adds at its end. Someone who may edit that note can change the line, and the note is then pushed onward as a new one; `upstream status` counts the recognised ones as foreign. There is no loop on its own: each further copy needs another edit.
- The hidden characters that a review refuses do not include the no-break space and the other special spaces.
- How the Mac app's Knowledge base pane shows a tab in a submission was not checked; the command-line review shows it.
- The Mac app waits up to an hour for its helper's database upgrade and then tries again, with no overall limit; the status says "upgrading".
- The plugin's session start on Windows takes about 4.5 seconds on the test computer (0.7.0: 2.6): it fetches the release's signed checksums and checks its `minigw` and the installed one every time.
- With no network and nothing cached (the first session after installing the plugin), a Linux or Windows computer whose installed `minigw` is not the current release has no `minigw` for the plugin: an unverified program is not run.
- The plugin changes since 0.7.33 were tried on a real Windows computer by running the install script by hand next to an installed 0.7.31; not yet as an installed plugin, and not yet on a Mac with only the Homebrew client.
- Whether a command typed as `! minigw ...` in Claude Code uses the plugin's copy was not tried on a computer with an older installed `minigw`: check `! minigw --version` first (the onboarding page says so).
- On Windows the plugin's own `minigw.exe` stays in its folder when an installed client is used, and is not updated while that is so: an MCP configuration that points at it keeps running that older copy.
- The plugin's install script cannot defend against someone who can already write to the plugin's data folder as you (they could replace a file between the check and the run, or put back an older signed pair): the checks are against a wrong or tampered download and a wrong installed client, not against code already running under your account.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin's own `minigw` is used unverified (the session start says so); the tools the check itself uses (`ssh-keygen`, `curl`, `shasum`, `codesign`) are taken from `PATH`.
- The plugin's scripts themselves are not signed: they come from the plugin's `release` branch on GitHub.
- The automatic pull, a member's search and read of a mirrored note, and the "differs" line of a note changed on the upstream were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway reading a Windows gateway on an older beta, one test project); a team link, the other direction (a Windows gateway reading a Mac gateway) and the panel in a real window were not.
- The panel additions (the Reading section and its actions) were tried through the shared fixtures and the renderers' tests, not in a real window.
- A hub that changes what we sent (its own trailer, other sanitising) makes a pushed note show twice (the local note and the hub's copy with the "differs" line); receipts written before this version, and after `resolve --theirs`, are compared by revision only.
- Reading off now forgets the link's classes and sources, an explicit empty list (`--read-sources none`, an empty panel field) means no extra sources, and only an omitted list keeps the current ones: an acceptance never brings back a reach that was not shown.
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is still a file in the config folder, also where the other secrets are in the credential store; this step only reports where it is.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session). `gw labels eval` was tried only with fake models.
- Agreement and eval numbers are agreement with explicit labels, a biased sample, not calibrated accuracy.
- A model of a provider (LiteLLM, OpenRouter, xAI) with `--minigw-tools` reads your folder with the tools of your own Claude Code on your computer: nothing is synced for it, so the root `.gitignore` does not limit what it can read (the line "syncing ... for agent models" is about agent models only). Use `--minigw-tools none`, or run it in a folder without secrets.
- Sync copies were checked for the root `.gitignore`: every sync mode on both clients leaves the listed entries out of what is sent (the same shared test fixture on both). But a file that was already copied before it was added to `.gitignore` stays in the gateway's copy (kept up to 14 days) until the owner removes the copy (`minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID`).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. It does not protect files that are run later without a person reading them (`Makefile`, `package.json` scripts, `.github/workflows`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json`, ...): review the diff.
- Direct mode on a Mac gateway with a remote client: the permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered, so a symbolic link staged later in the run can still be committed as a plain file; the end-of-run report names it afterwards. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there.
- Windows gateway: a link whose target names its UNC share by an 8.3 short alias is refused (it fails closed).

## 0.7.39

A candidate for the next stable release: the findings of its security review.

### Security
- After an agent's work, a protected path that the agent staged (`.envrc`, a hooks folder, `.claude/settings`) is again removed from the index also when the agent made git's refs or history too large to read in full: only an index that minigw could not read, a request still running, or a `HEAD` that does not resolve leaves the index untouched. When the index listing is too large, the protected paths are read on their own.
- The "unstage by hand" line prints a command only for plain path names; a name with other characters is listed without a command (a pasted command with such a name could run something else in cmd or PowerShell).
- Names of paths, branches and tags in the git warnings are printed with control characters and direction marks written out (`\x1b`, `\u{202e}`), so a name cannot erase or rewrite the warning in the terminal.
- The characters that a review treats as hidden now include the unassigned invisible code points (U+2065, U+FFF0-FFF8, U+E0080-E00FF, U+E01F0-E0FFF).

### Fixed
- On Windows the plugin removes its launchers for an installed `minigw` that no longer passes the check, so the plugin's own verified copy runs.

### Known problems
- A file name with a character that the Mac's file system ignores in front of a leading dot is not found by the separate read of protected paths (used only when the index listing is too large).
- `minigw plugin install` writes the plugin's launchers for the `minigw` that ran it, without the release check.
- Going back to 0.7.0 was tried with `minigw serve` on a Mac (the steps are in docs/install.md, "Going back to 0.7.0"): what was written after the upgrade is lost. For the Mac app the steps were read from the code, not tried; on Windows and Linux the commands are the same but were not run. A gateway that came through the betas before 0.7.37 may no longer have the `core-8-...db` snapshot.
- A 0.7.0 `minigw` against a newer gateway still treats `kb ...` and `team ...` as a prompt for an agent run and prints nothing useful: update the client (the plugin's session start and the onboarding page say how).
- The refusal of a submission with hidden characters was tried with the Go client; the Mac client passes the gateway's message through and was not tried.
- The "update the gateway" hint covers the kb and team commands, not `gw upstream` and `gw labels` (those talk to the gateway on the same computer).
- The extra snapshot that is kept for 30 days is the oldest one of the lowest schema present: restoring an even older database changes which one that is, and a clock change can end the 30 days early.
- An emoji joined with a zero-width joiner is shown and accepted when both sides are pictographs, at most seven joiners in a row; a joiner or a variation selector anywhere else is still a hidden character.
- Why one read of git failed once in a test run (the case the index fix is for) is not known.
- A note that came from another gateway is recognised by the line the sending gateway adds at its end. Someone who may edit that note can change the line, and the note is then pushed onward as a new one; `upstream status` counts the recognised ones as foreign. There is no loop on its own: each further copy needs another edit.
- The hidden characters that a review refuses do not include the no-break space and the other special spaces.
- How the Mac app's Knowledge base pane shows a tab in a submission was not checked; the command-line review shows it.
- The Mac app waits up to an hour for its helper's database upgrade and then tries again, with no overall limit; the status says "upgrading".
- The plugin's session start on Windows takes about 4.5 seconds on the test computer (0.7.0: 2.6): it fetches the release's signed checksums and checks its `minigw` and the installed one every time.
- With no network and nothing cached (the first session after installing the plugin), a Linux or Windows computer whose installed `minigw` is not the current release has no `minigw` for the plugin: an unverified program is not run.
- The plugin changes since 0.7.33 were tried on a real Windows computer by running the install script by hand next to an installed 0.7.31; not yet as an installed plugin, and not yet on a Mac with only the Homebrew client.
- Whether a command typed as `! minigw ...` in Claude Code uses the plugin's copy was not tried on a computer with an older installed `minigw`: check `! minigw --version` first (the onboarding page says so).
- On Windows the plugin's own `minigw.exe` stays in its folder when an installed client is used, and is not updated while that is so: an MCP configuration that points at it keeps running that older copy.
- The plugin's install script cannot defend against someone who can already write to the plugin's data folder as you (they could replace a file between the check and the run, or put back an older signed pair): the checks are against a wrong or tampered download and a wrong installed client, not against code already running under your account.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin's own `minigw` is used unverified (the session start says so); the tools the check itself uses (`ssh-keygen`, `curl`, `shasum`, `codesign`) are taken from `PATH`.
- The plugin's scripts themselves are not signed: they come from the plugin's `release` branch on GitHub.
- The automatic pull, a member's search and read of a mirrored note, and the "differs" line of a note changed on the upstream were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway reading a Windows gateway on an older beta, one test project); a team link, the other direction (a Windows gateway reading a Mac gateway) and the panel in a real window were not.
- The panel additions (the Reading section and its actions) were tried through the shared fixtures and the renderers' tests, not in a real window.
- A hub that changes what we sent (its own trailer, other sanitising) makes a pushed note show twice (the local note and the hub's copy with the "differs" line); receipts written before this version, and after `resolve --theirs`, are compared by revision only.
- Reading off now forgets the link's classes and sources, an explicit empty list (`--read-sources none`, an empty panel field) means no extra sources, and only an omitted list keeps the current ones: an acceptance never brings back a reach that was not shown.
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is still a file in the config folder, also where the other secrets are in the credential store; this step only reports where it is.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session). `gw labels eval` was tried only with fake models.
- Agreement and eval numbers are agreement with explicit labels, a biased sample, not calibrated accuracy.
- A model of a provider (LiteLLM, OpenRouter, xAI) with `--minigw-tools` reads your folder with the tools of your own Claude Code on your computer: nothing is synced for it, so the root `.gitignore` does not limit what it can read (the line "syncing ... for agent models" is about agent models only). Use `--minigw-tools none`, or run it in a folder without secrets.
- Sync copies were checked for the root `.gitignore`: every sync mode on both clients leaves the listed entries out of what is sent (the same shared test fixture on both). But a file that was already copied before it was added to `.gitignore` stays in the gateway's copy (kept up to 14 days) until the owner removes the copy (`minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID`).

## 0.7.37

Fixes from a pass over the upgrade from 0.7.0 and the first week of team use.

### Changed
- Emoji are visible text in submissions, reviews and pulled notes (`⚠️`, `👨‍💻`, `1️⃣`, flags, skin tones); emoji tag sequences (subdivision flags) stay refused as hidden characters.
- A submission with hidden characters is refused at once, with their line, column and code point; a leading byte order mark is dropped.

### Fixed
- A new client against an older gateway now says `this gateway is 0.7.5; kb needs 0.7.10 or newer: update the gateway` for the kb and team commands, instead of "not found or not visible" (the `gw upstream` and labels commands talk to the gateway on the same computer and are not covered).
- The core database keeps, besides its newest three snapshots, the oldest snapshot of the lowest schema for 30 days, so that after an upgrade over many schemas and a few later steps there is still a way back to the version you came from.
- After an agent's work, when a read of git failed or was cut short, minigw no longer removes a protected path from the index (a failed read could make a committed file look staged, and removing it staged its deletion): it warns that the path looks staged and prints the `git restore --staged` line to run by hand. When only the limit of new commits was reached, the path is removed as before.

### Known problems
- Going back to 0.7.0 after this version: 0.7.0 refuses the newer database (models work, projects and notes answer 503). The way back is 0.7.0's `minigw gw core restore <snapshot>` with the `core-8-...db` snapshot that the upgrade wrote next to the database; it is kept for 30 days from this version on. A gateway that came through the earlier betas may no longer have it.
- The refusal of a submission with hidden characters was tried with the Go client; the Mac client passes the gateway's message through and was not tried.
- The "update the gateway" hint covers the kb and team commands, not `gw upstream` and `gw labels` (those talk to the gateway on the same computer).
- The extra snapshot that is kept for 30 days is the oldest one of the lowest schema present: restoring an even older database changes which one that is, and a clock change can end the 30 days early.
- An emoji joined with a zero-width joiner is shown and accepted when both sides are pictographs, at most seven joiners in a row; a joiner or a variation selector anywhere else is still a hidden character.
- Why one read of git failed once in a test run (the case the index fix is for) is not known.
- A note that came from another gateway is recognised by the line the sending gateway adds at its end. Someone who may edit that note can change the line, and the note is then pushed onward as a new one; `upstream status` counts the recognised ones as foreign. There is no loop on its own: each further copy needs another edit.
- The hidden characters that a review refuses do not include the no-break space and the other special spaces.
- How the Mac app's Knowledge base pane shows a tab in a submission was not checked; the command-line review shows it.
- The Mac app waits up to an hour for its helper's database upgrade and then tries again, with no overall limit; the status says "upgrading".
- The plugin's session start on Windows takes about 4.5 seconds on the test computer (0.7.0: 2.6): it fetches the release's signed checksums and checks its `minigw` and the installed one every time.
- With no network and nothing cached (the first session after installing the plugin), a Linux or Windows computer whose installed `minigw` is not the current release has no `minigw` for the plugin: an unverified program is not run.
- The plugin changes since 0.7.33 were tried on a real Windows computer by running the install script by hand next to an installed 0.7.31; not yet as an installed plugin, and not yet on a Mac with only the Homebrew client.
- Whether a command typed as `! minigw ...` in Claude Code uses the plugin's copy was not tried on a computer with an older installed `minigw`: check `! minigw --version` first (the onboarding page says so).
- On Windows the plugin's own `minigw.exe` stays in its folder when an installed client is used, and is not updated while that is so: an MCP configuration that points at it keeps running that older copy.
- The plugin's install script cannot defend against someone who can already write to the plugin's data folder as you (they could replace a file between the check and the run, or put back an older signed pair): the checks are against a wrong or tampered download and a wrong installed client, not against code already running under your account.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin's own `minigw` is used unverified (the session start says so); the tools the check itself uses (`ssh-keygen`, `curl`, `shasum`, `codesign`) are taken from `PATH`.

## 0.7.36

Fixes from a second review of the changes made in 0.7.32.

### Security
- Restoring a backup taken in the middle of a pull from an upstream gateway no longer resumes that pull: the first pull after a restore reads everything again, so a note the upstream changed meanwhile is not shown in its old form.
- A tab in a submission is now shown in the review (where it is), so the text a reviewer reads is the text that is published.

### Fixed
- A note pulled from an upstream gateway with a lone carriage return keeps a line break there instead of losing the character, as in every other place.

### Known problems
- A note that came from another gateway is recognised by the line the sending gateway adds at its end. Someone who may edit that note can change the line, and the note is then pushed onward as a new one; `upstream status` counts the recognised ones as foreign. There is no loop on its own: each further copy needs another edit.
- The hidden characters that a review refuses do not include the no-break space and the other special spaces.
- How the Mac app's Knowledge base pane shows a tab in a submission was not checked; the command-line review shows it.
- The Mac app waits up to an hour for its helper's database upgrade and then tries again, with no overall limit; the status says "upgrading".
- The plugin's session start on Windows takes about 4.5 seconds on the test computer (0.7.0: 2.6): it fetches the release's signed checksums and checks its `minigw` and the installed one every time.
- With no network and nothing cached (the first session after installing the plugin), a Linux or Windows computer whose installed `minigw` is not the current release has no `minigw` for the plugin: an unverified program is not run.
- The plugin changes since 0.7.33 were tried on a real Windows computer by running the install script by hand next to an installed 0.7.31; not yet as an installed plugin, and not yet on a Mac with only the Homebrew client.
- Whether a command typed as `! minigw ...` in Claude Code uses the plugin's copy was not tried on a computer with an older installed `minigw`: check `! minigw --version` first (the onboarding page says so).
- On Windows the plugin's own `minigw.exe` stays in its folder when an installed client is used, and is not updated while that is so: an MCP configuration that points at it keeps running that older copy.
- The plugin's install script cannot defend against someone who can already write to the plugin's data folder as you (they could replace a file between the check and the run, or put back an older signed pair): the checks are against a wrong or tampered download and a wrong installed client, not against code already running under your account.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin's own `minigw` is used unverified (the session start says so); the tools the check itself uses (`ssh-keygen`, `curl`, `shasum`, `codesign`) are taken from `PATH`.
- The plugin's scripts themselves are not signed: they come from the plugin's `release` branch on GitHub.
- The automatic pull, a member's search and read of a mirrored note, and the "differs" line of a note changed on the upstream were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway reading a Windows gateway on an older beta, one test project); a team link, the other direction (a Windows gateway reading a Mac gateway) and the panel in a real window were not.
- The panel additions (the Reading section and its actions) were tried through the shared fixtures and the renderers' tests, not in a real window.
- A hub that changes what we sent (its own trailer, other sanitising) makes a pushed note show twice (the local note and the hub's copy with the "differs" line); receipts written before this version, and after `resolve --theirs`, are compared by revision only.
- Reading off now forgets the link's classes and sources, an explicit empty list (`--read-sources none`, an empty panel field) means no extra sources, and only an omitted list keeps the current ones: an acceptance never brings back a reach that was not shown.
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is still a file in the config folder, also where the other secrets are in the credential store; this step only reports where it is.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session). `gw labels eval` was tried only with fake models.
- Agreement and eval numbers are agreement with explicit labels, a biased sample, not calibrated accuracy.
- A model of a provider (LiteLLM, OpenRouter, xAI) with `--minigw-tools` reads your folder with the tools of your own Claude Code on your computer: nothing is synced for it, so the root `.gitignore` does not limit what it can read (the line "syncing ... for agent models" is about agent models only). Use `--minigw-tools none`, or run it in a folder without secrets.
- Sync copies were checked for the root `.gitignore`: every sync mode on both clients leaves the listed entries out of what is sent (the same shared test fixture on both). But a file that was already copied before it was added to `.gitignore` stays in the gateway's copy (kept up to 14 days) until the owner removes the copy (`minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID`).

## 0.7.34

Follow-up fixes for the plugin after another pass over how a new user meets it.

### Fixed
- Inside Claude Code a plain `minigw` is now the plugin's own copy: the plugin puts its folder first on the PATH, not last, so an old `minigw` from Homebrew or the installer no longer answers instead of it. `/minigw:gw update` on a Mac also upgrades the `minigw` client cask when it is installed.
- On Linux and Windows the session start again tells you when the `minigw` installed on the computer is not the current release, and how to update it (it is not run to find out). A `minigw` that is the current release is now trusted in the first session, without a download.
- On Windows the plugin no longer deletes its own `minigw.exe` when shims point at an installed `minigw`, so a `kb mcp --print-config` made earlier keeps working.

## 0.7.33

A candidate for the next stable release: the plugin's install script and the onboarding page, after a pass that asked how the release fails for a real new user.

### Changed
- On Linux and Windows the plugin now runs only a `minigw` it has verified: one whose SHA-256 is in the signed `SHA256SUMS` of a release the plugin downloaded (the signature is checked again at every session start). A `minigw` installed some other way (the Windows installer, a package, a copy) that is not in those sums is not run and not used; the first session after the update downloads the release's `minigw` once into the plugin's folder and uses that copy from then on. If it cannot be downloaded, the session start says so and the gateway skills stay off until the network is back. Without `ssh-keygen` nothing can be verified, and the copy the plugin already had stays. The Mac is unchanged: it checks the Developer ID signature before running anything.
- When the installed `minigw` is older than the plugin needs and there is nothing newer to use (offline), the plugin keeps using the old one and says so in one line at the start of the session.

### Fixed
- On a Mac, a `minigw` in the plugin's folder that fails the signature check and cannot be replaced is moved aside to `minigw.rejected`, so no skill can run it.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin says in one line at the start of the session that its `minigw` could not be verified against the release signature.
- The Claude Code plugin no longer keeps an old `minigw` that is installed on the computer (Homebrew, the app, the installer): with a client older than the plugin needs it uses its own copy and the session start says which one is old and how to update it. Before, `minigw kb ...` and `minigw team ...` could run through the gateway as a question to Claude, and the hooks stayed off without a message.
- On a Mac without the app, the session start now also tells when the installed `minigw` is older than the latest release.
- The team onboarding page says where `minigw` runs after installing only the plugin, and that a project `admin` is a reviewer of that project's knowledge base (the page and the parity table said only the owner reviews).

### Known problems
- The plugin's install script cannot defend against someone who can already write to the plugin's data folder as you (they could replace a file between the check and the run, or put back an older signed pair): the checks are against a wrong or tampered download and a wrong installed client, not against code already running under your account.
- On Linux and Windows without `ssh-keygen` the plugin's own `minigw` is used unverified (the session start says so); the tools the check itself uses (`ssh-keygen`, `curl`, `shasum`, `codesign`) are taken from `PATH`.
- The plugin's scripts themselves are not signed: they come from the plugin's `release` branch on GitHub.
- The plugin changes of this release were tried with the Go tests (fake tools) and the Linux Docker script, including Windows-style runs under a fake `uname`; not yet on a real Windows computer or a Mac with only the Homebrew client.
- The automatic pull, a member's search and read of a mirrored note, and the "differs" line of a note changed on the upstream were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway reading a Windows gateway on an older beta, one test project); a team link, the other direction (a Windows gateway reading a Mac gateway) and the panel in a real window were not.
- The panel additions (the Reading section and its actions) were tried through the shared fixtures and the renderers' tests, not in a real window.
- A hub that changes what we sent (its own trailer, other sanitising) makes a pushed note show twice (the local note and the hub's copy with the "differs" line); receipts written before this version, and after `resolve --theirs`, are compared by revision only.
- Reading off now forgets the link's classes and sources, an explicit empty list (`--read-sources none`, an empty panel field) means no extra sources, and only an omitted list keeps the current ones: an acceptance never brings back a reach that was not shown.
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).

## 0.7.32

A candidate for the next stable release.

### Changed
- Windows line endings (CRLF) and lone carriage returns at the end of a line are stored as plain line ends (LF) in every note and submission written from now on (writes, edits, Markdown import, submissions, and what other gateways push). A submission already waiting with CRLF keeps its version line, is shown with normal lines and can be accepted; the published note gets LF line endings. A carriage return inside a line of such an older submission still counts as a hidden character.

### Security
- Restoring a backup hides every reading link's upstream mirror from members until a pull completes after the restore (as a mirror with no recent pull is hidden); nothing is deleted. `status` and the panel show "restored: waiting for a pull". If the hub had revoked this gateway after the backup, the next pull is refused and purges the mirror as usual.
- Two gateways that push to each other no longer copy each other's notes for ever. A note that arrived from another gateway (its text ends with the line that gateway's push adds) is never pushed onward: not back to where it came from, and not to a third gateway. Before, two gateways linked both ways (in either mode) made a new copy of every note at each push until both projects reached their note limit, and a gateway in the middle passed notes, with their author's name, on to its own upstreams. `upstream status` counts these notes as "not sent: arrived from another gateway"; a member who removes that last line from such a note makes it local work again.
- Reviewers are no longer shown text they cannot see. The review reads (`kb review`, `gw kb review`, the Knowledge base panel) leave out invisible and direction-changing characters and say how many were removed, and such a submission cannot be accepted (reject it and ask for clean text). In the panel, Accept is not offered for a text longer than the panel shows (20,000 characters): use `minigw gw kb review` and accept by version.

### Fixed
- `minigw status` on the Mac now prints the `Secrets:` line the Windows and Linux client has had since 0.7.26 (`Secrets:   macOS Keychain`; never a value). A shared list of the status line labels, asserted by both clients' tests, keeps the two from drifting apart again.
- Mac: a long upgrade of the project database (a very large database or a slow disk) is waited for. The helper says "upgrading the database (schema A to B)" before the long step, the app shows it where it shows "starting" and no longer stops the helper after 5 seconds. A snapshot is written under a temporary name and renamed when complete, so a helper stopped in the middle leaves no snapshot that counts; the next start removes such leftover files.
- `minigw gw upstream remove` and the same action in the panels: when the secret store cannot delete the stored credentials, the answer now says so (the link is removed, the credentials could not be, with the store and the item name, never a value) instead of reporting them gone.
- Mac: a settings change no longer starts a second label worker beside the old one. The old worker is stopped first, and the hourly cap and the one-sample-at-a-time rule belong to the app, so they hold across settings changes.
- Settings page of `minigw serve`: opening the Foundry Local pane on a computer without Foundry Local no longer logs a console error. The models route answers 200 with `installed: false` and the pane says "Foundry Local is not installed on this computer"; a real failure is still an error.

### Known problems
- The automatic pull, a member's search and read of a mirrored note, and the "differs" line of a note changed on the upstream were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway reading a Windows gateway on an older beta, one test project); a team link, the other direction (a Windows gateway reading a Mac gateway) and the panel in a real window were not.
- The panel additions (the Reading section and its actions) were tried through the shared fixtures and the renderers' tests, not in a real window.
- A hub that changes what we sent (its own trailer, other sanitising) makes a pushed note show twice (the local note and the hub's copy with the "differs" line); receipts written before this version, and after `resolve --theirs`, are compared by revision only.
- Reading off now forgets the link's classes and sources, an explicit empty list (`--read-sources none`, an empty panel field) means no extra sources, and only an omitted list keeps the current ones: an acceptance never brings back a reach that was not shown.
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is still a file in the config folder, also where the other secrets are in the credential store; this step only reports where it is.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session). `gw labels eval` was tried only with fake models.
- Agreement and eval numbers are agreement with explicit labels, a biased sample, not calibrated accuracy.
- A model of a provider (LiteLLM, OpenRouter, xAI) with `--minigw-tools` reads your folder with the tools of your own Claude Code on your computer: nothing is synced for it, so the root `.gitignore` does not limit what it can read (the line "syncing ... for agent models" is about agent models only). Use `--minigw-tools none`, or run it in a folder without secrets.
- Sync copies were checked for the root `.gitignore`: every sync mode on both clients leaves the listed entries out of what is sent (the same shared test fixture on both). But a file that was already copied before it was added to `.gitignore` stays in the gateway's copy (kept up to 14 days) until the owner removes the copy (`minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID`).

## 0.7.30

### New
- Reading an upstream's notes, step C4 (the automatic pull and the panel; schema 25, snapshot first; an older program refuses the newer schema as always): a link with reading on, whose reach you accepted, is pulled by the gateway itself every 15 minutes (`minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --pull-every <minutes>`, 5 to 1440, or **Set Pull Interval** in the panel; per link). On Windows and Linux the gateway's timer does it; on the Mac the app's timer asks the helper for one step at a time through `POST /upstream-auto` (one link per request, paced by the helper's answer, never two requests at once, stopped at the start of shutdown). A step is one link, pushed or pulled, the two in turn. The pull is the same run as `pull` by hand (200 notes per run, "more" continues at the next tick from the cursor) but holds a link's lock for at most 45 seconds, so a push is never kept waiting for a long pull. Failures back off from a minute doubling to an hour with jitter, and a run that made no progress counts as a failure. A refusal (401/403, project gone) deletes the mirror at once, as before, and now also switches the timer of that link off with the error left visible; accepting the reach again switches it on (a pull by hand never switches the timer on; a refusal switches it off, whoever started the pull). A traversal in which the upstream could not be asked or did not answer properly in any of its runs never ends fresh (it ends as a failure and the next one starts from the beginning); a note the upstream answered about definitively (not served, failed checks) is only skipped, counted and shown in `status` and the panel ("N notes could not be read"), and the mirror stays fresh, the audit log has at most five rows per failure episode (a run that changed the mirror always has its row), and a pull reads the link, its remote and its accepted reach together under the link's lock (a link update is refused while a push or pull of the link runs) and refuses when they do not match. Audit rows (`upstream.auto-pull`, counts only): one for a run that did something, otherwise one per change of outcome. `status` shows the interval, the next pull, the failures in a row and "automatic pull off". With the timer on a mirror stays fresh, so "stale" (7 days) only happens when the upstream cannot be reached for a week.
- Upstream gateways panel: a section **Reading from upstream gateways** (the Mac pane and the `minigw serve` settings page; strings in C, en_US and sl_SI): per link reading on or off, classes, extra sources, interval, notes in the mirror, last complete pull, next pull, and in the detail the age, the failures, whether the timer is off, the last error and "hidden from members". Actions: Dry Run, Turn Reading On… and Turn Reading On With Data… (a confirmation names the upstream, the remote project or team, the classes and the extra sources and records the acceptance exactly as `--accept-reach` does), Change What Is Read… (a new confirmation for any change of the reach), Turn Reading Off, Set Pull Interval…, Pull Now (new route `POST upstream-pull` in the panel registries and the confirmation-required set; no secret is involved).

### Fixed
- A note this gateway published (publish mode) that was then changed on the upstream (a conflict, or an edit there) was hidden from members as "our own push", so a member could not see that the upstream's text differs. The upstream's copy is now judged by its content: it is hidden only while its text is what our push sent (a hash of class, kind, title and body without the trailer, recorded at push time; an older receipt falls back to the revision); otherwise members see it too, marked and quoted like any mirrored note, with one more line saying it differs from the local note (its number only for a member who may read that note). `status` says when a revision went backwards. The owner's status keeps showing the conflict; `resolve --theirs` and `--ours` behave as before; a copy of a note sent as a submission (no note id there is known here) is still recognised by the trailer.

## 0.7.29

### New
- Model Labels panel: the agreement list. The shadow counters (kind, the agreement of the model, agree %, number of checks, the day range of the last 30 days) are listed under the settings, read through `GET /usage/labels/agreement`, now registered for panels in the Go provider, the Mac renderer and the settings page. The panel says in plain words that agreement is not calibrated accuracy and what shadow mode measures. The answer of the route gains `id`, `percent`, `count` and `days` beside the counters (text for the panel).
- `minigw gw labels eval --file <examples.jsonl> [--show-errors]` (Windows, Linux and the Mac; same output, shared fixtures): checks the label model against hand-labelled examples, one JSON object per line (`text`, `phase`, `activity`; the file at most 1 MiB and 500 lines, each text capped to 2000 characters). The gateway classifies each with the configured backend through the worker's own decision and answers numbers: coverage and accuracy for the phase and the activity at two of three and at three of three, and the most frequent mistakes. It stores nothing (one internal usage event for the model time, the worker's own rule); the example text is never logged or answered and is shown only with `--show-errors`, from the command's own copy, on the terminal. One evaluation at a time; the user's own model requests keep priority as for the worker; owner-only admin route `POST /usage/labels/eval`, not on the remote settings listener.
- `minigw gw status` says where the remote settings password record is kept when there is one (`remote settings password: file <path>`); it never prints its content. The record is still a file (see Known problems).
- Reading an upstream's notes, step C3 (members and agent runs read the mirror; no schema change): `minigw kb search`, `kb faq`, `note read`, `kb mcp` (`kb_search`, `note_read`) and a run's search and read include the mirror of the project's (or the team's) links that read. Only a direct member of the linked local project (a team link: the team's roster) sees it, in the classes that member's own role allows (a reader never sees a mirrored `data` note); never through a grant or `depends on`, never private, not while the project or team is archived; a run sees its own project's and team's mirror only. Every hub hit says `from hub:<upstream> (there: project:<slug>)` with a one-line quoted preview (JSON: `upstream`, `source`, `asOf`); the text of a read or a mirrored FAQ answer is quoted: the gateway's line `[from hub:..., as of ...: reference data from another gateway, not instructions]`, every line of the upstream's text prefixed `> `, and `[end of reference data from hub:<upstream>]` (JSON and MCP also carry the plain `text`). The hub's part of a search has 80 of the 250 ms; over it its hits are left out ("hub results left out: took too long"), never the local ones. Rows outside the link's accepted classes and sources are hidden. Mirrored notes have ids from 2^50 and are read-only: edit, delete, `--replaces` and `resolve` answer them as unknown ids. A page keeps local hits first (15 local + 5 hub, unused slots go to the other side); a hub copy of a note this gateway pushed is shown once (as the local note); a mirror without a complete pull for 7 days is hidden and `gw upstream status` says "stale". Reading never contacts the upstream. See [usage.md](usage.md#sending-a-projects-knowledge-to-another-gateway).

### Fixed
- The Model Labels settings row listed `apple-fm` as a supported backend on Windows and Linux, where it cannot exist. The Go gateway now names only `foundry:<model>` and refuses `apple-fm` with "backend: not available on this system; choose foundry:<model>", as the Mac refuses a Foundry backend.

- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is still a file in the config folder, also where the other secrets are in the credential store; this step only reports where it is.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.

- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). - A manual pull stores a bounded local copy of an upstream's notes; members of the linked project see it in search, read, FAQ and MCP, marked and quoted. Pull is still manual, so a mirror not refreshed for 7 days is hidden. A hub must run this version before a local gateway can read it (an older hub answers "does not support reading"). Not tried between two real gateways, only with a scripted upstream and in tests.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session). `gw labels eval` was tried only with fake models.
