## 0.7.28

### New
- Usage labels on the Mac: the app's worker fills the phase and activity labels of usage reports with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns the setting on and chooses `apple-fm` (`Sources/RouterCore/Decide.swift`, `LabelWorker.swift`). Off by default; nothing leaves the Mac; if the model is unavailable the worker only reports "on-device model unavailable". It never delays a request, yields to a user's request for the on-device model (no sample starts while one runs; a running sample is cancelled and retried once), and the text it classifies is never stored or logged. The Mac settings route accepts only `apple-fm`. The helper has a new internal call `label-decision` (the decision's own usage event) and `event-labels` and `label-agreement` take the settings `revision`. See [usage.md](usage.md#model-labels).
- Reading an upstream's notes, step C2 (the mirror and the manual pull; schema 24, snapshot first): `minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --read [--read-class structure[,data]] [--read-sources <label,...>] --accept-reach` turns reading on for a link (refused without `--accept-reach`; the link's push settings stay), `--no-read` turns it off. `minigw gw upstream pull [<project>|--team <team>]` fills a local mirror from the upstream's manifest (at most 200 note requests per run, a per-link cursor, the Mac within the time of one helper call), applies deletions only from a manifest that was not cut, deletes the link's mirror at once when the upstream refuses the device or no longer knows the project or team, and stores nothing from an upstream that is too old. Everything the upstream sends is checked against its manifest and sanitised; the mirror holds at most 5000 notes per link and 64 MiB per gateway, is deleted on unlink, upstream removal and reading off, and is not in export bundles or pushes. The acceptance covers the exact reach (upstream, remote project or team, classes, sources): a link changed to another remote turns reading off and deletes its mirror until it is accepted again. One `pull` command stops after 25 runs or 10 minutes ("more remains"); a full mirror ends the pull without fetching; a note that is not valid UTF-8 is skipped; invisible characters are removed; the 64 MiB bound counts the disk cost (three times the text); a restore rebuilds the mirror's search index. `gw upstream status` shows read on/off, the accepted reach, the mirror, the last pull, its age and the last error. See [usage.md](usage.md#sending-a-projects-knowledge-to-another-gateway).

### Security
- `gw core restore` of an older backup now migrates the staged copy itself and validates it as a current-schema database (segments, memberships, objects, foreign keys) before it replaces the live one; a failure leaves the previous database in place. A pre-schema-15 backup with a project in the id range (from 2^40) or name range (`@...`) reserved for team segments is refused (it would have let a team read that project's notes). The search index is rebuilt after that migration, for every schema.
- A grant no longer gives read access while the READER project is archived: an archived project confers nothing. This only removes access (reads, search, `depends on`, the upstream manifest, a run's grant reads and the project lists follow the same rule).

### Fixed
- `minigw gw core restore` refused a snapshot or backup made at schema 17 or older (`no such table: project_team_depends`; schema 12 also failed on the search index). Every restore check now reads only the tables that schema has; a test restores a backup of every schema from 8 on.
- Folder sync says so when the root `.gitignore` keeps top-level entries out of the copy (one stderr line, first time in a run, up to five names), instead of leaving the agent without them silently.
- On the Mac the helper's upstream callouts for `POST /upstream-pull` were refused (every pull, also the dry run, ended with "no answer from the upstream"): the route is allowed with the callout kinds it needs, and one shared fixture (`client/testdata/upstream-callouts.json`) lists every route that needs callouts, asserted by Swift and Go.
- Waits for an upstream's rate limit on Windows and Linux end with the request instead of holding the link's lock.

### Known problems
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is a file in the gateway's config folder (also on a signed-in Windows gateway), not an entry in the credential store.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- A manual pull stores a bounded local copy of an upstream's notes, but members do not see it yet: search, `note read`, FAQ and MCP do not include the mirror (step C3). A hub must run this version before a local gateway can read it (an older hub answers "does not support reading"). Not tried between two real gateways, only with a scripted upstream.
- Labels can be filled on Windows and Linux with Foundry Local and on the Mac with the on-device model (Apple Foundation Models) when the owner turns it on. Not tried with a real model on either (the Mac worker was tried with a fake model in tests, not in a real session).
- A pull is manual: there is no automatic pull and no panel (step C4).

## 0.7.27

### New
- Project admins review the submissions of their own project: `minigw kb review [<project>|--team <team>]`, `minigw kb accept <id>` and `minigw kb reject <id> [--reason text]` (same output as the owner's `gw kb` commands), on the member routes `/minigw/v1/projects/{id}/kb/review…`. Admin means the role `admin` in that project (directly or through a team assignment; a grant never); a team's own notes are reviewed by whoever may write them. Nobody reviews their own submission (four eyes: another admin or the owner does), and an accept needs what the reviewer could write themselves. The note keeps the writer as author; its provenance and the audit name the reviewing user. `minigw project list` shows "N submissions wait for review" to a project's admins. The owner's review is unchanged. See [usage.md](usage.md#review-by-project-admins).
- Usage labels (Windows and Linux): a worker in `minigw serve` fills the labels of the usage reports with a Foundry Local model when the owner turns the setting on (`client/decide`, `client/serve_labels.go`). Not tried with a real model.

### Changed
- Accepting a submission now needs the version that was read: `kb review` and `gw kb review` print a line `version <hash>` (the whole review hash), and `minigw kb accept <id> --version <hash>` / `minigw gw kb accept <id> --version <hash>` accept only that version (a shortened hash is refused); without `--version` the command shows the text and asks "Accept this text? [y/N]" in a terminal, and refuses without one. An older `minigw` cannot accept on a 0.7.27 gateway until it is updated (the gateway refuses its accept with "name the version you read").

### Security
- The version an accept names is the WHOLE review hash (SHA-256, 64 hex), compared whole by minigw and the gateway: a short tag could be made to collide by the writer, who writes both texts. The hash also covers the submission's stored provenance (device, source, times), so the same text sent again from another device is a new version.
- The Knowledge base panel's detail of a submission reads it as the version of its list row (`GET /kb/submissions/{id}/versions/{hash}`), so the text it shows is the text that row's Accept publishes; when the submission changed since the list was loaded, the detail says so.
- `kb accept` asks its question only on a real terminal (as `isatty`): another character device such as `/dev/zero` is not one.
- An accept now names the version its reviewer read: the review reads carry `reviewHash`, and every accept (the owner's admin route and panel, and the member route) requires it and is refused with "the submission changed since you read it: review it again" when the writer rewrote the pending submission in between (for example with `kb import`). `kb accept` and `gw kb accept` print what they accept before sending it. An older `minigw gw kb accept` is refused with "name the version you read": update minigw.
- When the writer may no longer write the note at accept time, the reviewer gets its own answer (409 `writer_lost`), never the writer's 403 or 404 as if it were the reviewer's.
- Changed rule: an admin of an ARCHIVED project no longer writes (or reviews) the own notes of the project's team; only admins of active team projects do.
- `minigw kb review` without a project reads only the projects the caller reviews, at most 100 rows a page.
- `kb submit --replaces <id>` no longer reveals whether a note id exists elsewhere or is a faq: the replaced note's kind (and its size limits) is looked up only after the check that the caller may change that note, so a note of another project or team answers exactly like a missing one (404).

### Fixed
- A read-only Codex run on a mounted folder (`minigw --minigw-ro`, direct mode) could not read that folder.

### Known problems
- Member review is command-line only: the Knowledge base panel is the owner's, and members have no panel for it.
- A project with a single admin leaves that admin's own submissions for the owner.
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is a file in the gateway's config folder (also on a signed-in Windows gateway), not an entry in the credential store.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Nothing is pulled yet: `pull` without `--dry-run` is refused, there is no local copy of an upstream's notes and search does not include them. A hub must run this version before a local gateway's dry run can read it (an older hub answers "does not support reading"). The dry run has only been tried through tests.
- Windows and Linux can fill labels with a Foundry Local model when the owner turns it on; the Mac step comes next. Not tried with a real model.
- `minigw gw core restore` refuses a snapshot or backup made at schema 17 or older ("no such table: project_team_depends"); restore those with the release that made them. Found by trying the restore on copies of a real gateway's snapshots (schema 18 and 19 restore).

## 0.7.26

### New
- `minigw gw status` prints a `secrets:` line saying where secrets are kept (Windows Credential Manager, the Secret Service, Keychain, platform files, or a file with the reason), and `minigw status` on a client a `Secrets:` line. The admin API's `GET /status` carries it as `secrets`. Nothing moves yet: no secret changed its place, and a credential store that fails now gives an error instead of a quiet fall-back to `gateway-keys.json`.
- A note edited by someone other than its author now shows who changed it: `minigw note read` prints "by A, last changed by B", `minigw note list` ends such a row with "(edited: B)", the Knowledge base panel has a "Last changed by" column, and the member and admin note answers carry an additive `editor` field (absent when the author last changed it). No schema change; the Markdown export and the upstream push are unchanged.
- Reading an upstream, step 1 (the hub side only): an upstream gateway now answers `GET /minigw/v1/projects/{id}/kb/manifest` for a bound member with the list of published, non-private notes that member could read there (the project, its team's notes and what it depends on, each by the member's own rights): ids, revisions, class and kind only, at most 5000 entries (`truncated` says more exist), counted against the notes-list rate limit. `minigw gw upstream pull [<project>|--team <team>] --dry-run` asks the upstream for that list and prints, per source, how many notes of which class and kind this gateway could read; it stores nothing. The Mac app admits that one new request for its helper (a GET of exactly that path, no query).
- Model labels, the storage and the settings (nothing fills labels yet): a phase or activity that a model chose is stored with its source `model` and an agreement score, and shown in `minigw gw usage --by phase|activity` as its own row (`do (model)`); `--label-source given|branch|model` on `gw usage` and `gw usage-events` keeps the events that have a label from that source. An explicit label is never replaced by a model's, and a label never gives access to anything. Schema 21 here, 22 for the import table and 23 for the automatic push below (one snapshot of the old database is kept; an older program refuses the newer file).
- A new Model Labels pane (settings page and Mac app) with the owner's settings, off by default: backend, agreement needed (all three samples or two of three), most decisions per hour, a shadow check against explicit labels and the list of activities a model may choose. Owner only.

### Changed
- Documentation brought up to date with beta 0.7.25: four admin panels (not three) in the README, `usage.md` and `gateway.md`; the Compose admin setup is no longer listed as unsupported in `docker.md`; a new parity table of macOS app, Windows, Linux and Docker per feature group ([parity.md](parity.md); the control-by-control list stays in [windows-parity.md](windows-parity.md)); a one-page [team onboarding guide](team-onboarding.md).
- The plugin's skills describe the current commands: upstream `--auto|--manual`, `--mode submit|publish` and `upstream resolve`, the four panes, `gw settings password`, `kb mcp`, `kb export`/`kb import`, team commands, `project remote`, `project depends` and `project sessions`. The plugin version is not raised here: users get the new skill text with the next plugin release.

### Security
- Offline restore (`minigw gw core restore`, gateway stopped) accepted a crafted backup file that hid a trigger under the name of an automatic index, and one whose tables had lost their foreign keys, cascades or checks or whose unique index was not unique. Such a file could keep access that could not be revoked. It needed the owner to restore that file by hand: no network route restores. A restore now refuses any object that is not exactly what this program's migrations make for the backup's schema (no exemption by name), compares the definition of every table, index and the search table, refuses a backup with rows that break a foreign key, and refuses an epoch or project mark outside a sane range (a huge value overflowed the epoch and broke a later cleanup).
- Repeated failing starts of an upgrade no longer push out the snapshot taken before the upgrade: the oldest snapshot from before an unfinished upgrade is kept besides the newest three, and a failed start on an unchanged file writes no new snapshot.
- Mac: provider requests no longer follow HTTP redirects (as the Go clients), so a prompt is never sent to a host the owner did not configure.
- An http provider (`--insecure`: private host, `.local`, container service name) is never reached through a proxy, in the provider and the quota requests of the Go client and the Mac app, so its key is not sent to a proxy in cleartext. https providers keep the proxy settings.
- Mac: the helper's upstream callouts (connect, forget, do) are accepted only while the matching upstream route is running, and only for the upstream that route names.
- `minigw gw import`: a team bundle could take over local projects, users and teams that it only matched by name. If the owner imported a bundle from elsewhere without `--dry-run`, a project of this gateway that was in no team could be attached to the bundle's team, an existing user could become a team member and get the admin role on it, grants from local projects could be added, and notes written into them. An import now merges only into objects that the same `--source` created before; a name that matches any other local project, team or user stops the whole import before anything is written and lists each conflict. `--adopt project:<slug>,team:<name>,user:<name>` merges into exactly the objects you list (a user is never adopted implicitly; an adopted object keeps its local roles, grants from an adopted project need the project in the list, and the audit row names what was adopted). `--dry-run` shows the same list. Re-importing from the same source needs no flag. Bundles imported by an earlier version left no record: their objects show up as conflicts once. Still import only bundles you trust.
- Re-importing a Markdown file that replaces a published note could change the note's kind (finding to faq, or faq to finding past the faq size limits) when the pending replacement was updated; a replacement now keeps the kind of the note it replaces.
- The hand-in request of the hooks (and the notes skill) put a title in double quotes on the command line, so a title copied from hostile repository text with `$(...)` or backticks could run a command in the shell. The request now gives no `--title`: the first line of the text on stdin is the title.
- A project admin who asked for the usage of their project also got the gateway-wide records of lost usage events (counts and time windows, no content and no names). Members no longer get them; the owner's report does.

### Fixed
- A second sync conflict on the same file no longer overwrites the first saved copy: further copies are `<name>.minigw-conflict-2`, `-3`, ... (up to 99; after that the file is not synced and the note says so).
- Automatic push: an edit made between two batches of a large link, or notes refused in an earlier batch, could be left unsent or forgotten. The link now stays marked until a full pass finds nothing left. Schema 23 restarts old push cursors once.
- Resolving a conflict now names the upstream (`minigw gw upstream resolve ... --upstream <name>`; the panel sends it): resolving on one upstream no longer settles the same note on the others.
- Unlinking or removing an upstream while a push of it runs now waits or is refused, and the push's audit row is always written.
- `minigw gw settings password` now confirms on success ("remote settings password set", that remote sessions are signed out, and whether the listener is on); it printed nothing when the password came on standard input.
- The sessions report of a project no longer reads every event of the project on each call: a project with more than 100000 events inside the retention made the report fail for good.
- The confirmations and the help for linking an upstream now say that each note carries its author's name and that `--with-private` sends the private notes of all members of the project.

### Known problems
- The four admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base, Upstream gateways) have only been tried through tests, not in a real window. In the Teams and projects pane team notes (a team's own notes) are not shown, only in the Knowledge base pane; in the Knowledge base pane notes and questions are read, not edited, and a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference. The "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI.
- The panels on the settings page of `minigw serve`, the Upstream gateways pane and `gw settings password` were tried on one Windows gateway in a headless Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it.
- Remote settings have no team, project or knowledge base administration: the listener serves providers, models and devices only, so a Docker team gateway has no web page for teams, projects or review (use `docker compose exec ... minigw gw`).
- The remote settings password record is a file in the gateway's config folder (also on a signed-in Windows gateway), not an entry in the credential store.
- The Mac app has no remote settings listener and no `gw settings password`, by design: on the Mac the admin UI is the native app.
- Docker Compose: the admin setup (`compose.admin.yaml`) was tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux. The gateway itself was tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way. Deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them).
- Automatic push and publish mode, with a conflict and `resolve --ours`, were tried between two real gateways on 2026-11-18 (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway; a test project with three notes; one push for five quick edits; a conflict was not overwritten). Replacements of accepted notes, team notes and `resolve --theirs` have only been tried in tests.
- Only the owner reviews submissions; a project admin cannot. With one reviewer this is the main bottleneck of a team.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; the owner has no write path to them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- Worktree mode runs the top repository's own git clean/smudge filters (intended for LFS): use sync for an untrusted repository.
- Nothing is pulled yet: `pull` without `--dry-run` is refused, there is no local copy of an upstream's notes and search does not include them. A hub must run this version before a local gateway's dry run can read it (an older hub answers "does not support reading"). The dry run has only been tried through tests.
- Nothing fills labels yet: the model step comes in the next betas, so turning the setting on changes nothing in the reports today.
- `minigw gw core restore` refuses a snapshot or backup made at schema 17 or older ("no such table: project_team_depends"); restore those with the release that made them. Found by trying the restore on copies of a real gateway's snapshots (schema 18 and 19 restore).

## 0.7.25

### New
- Upstream gateways panel: per link it now shows automatic or manual, the mode (submit or publish), the sync state (changed here since, last push, last automatic success, last failure, next attempt, last refusal) and the number of conflicts. It can switch a link between automatic and manual (turning automatic on asks first), switch its mode (the gateway refuses once the link has sent notes, and the panel shows its message), link a project or team in publish mode (with a confirmation that notes are then written on the upstream without its review), and list a link's conflicts (note title; changed there or deleted there) and settle each with "Keep Theirs" or "Send Ours as Proposal", both after a confirmation. New owner-only read route `GET /upstream-links/{id}`; `PUT upstreams/{}/links/{}` and `POST upstream-resolve` now need a confirmation in every panel.
- Docker Compose: an optional admin setup (`deploy/docker/compose.admin.yaml`, added to `COMPOSE_FILE`; adding it recreates the gateway container once) puts the remote settings listener behind an HTTPS-only Caddy reverse proxy (digest-pinned, mutual TLS to the gateway, forwarding headers replaced, only the settings page, login/logout and the permitted admin paths proxied), with `deploy/docker/admin-certs.sh` (link and browser certificates, a name-constrained browser CA, `renew`) for the certificates; see [docker.md](docker.md#remote-settings-from-a-browser-admin-profile).

### Known problems
- The Upstream gateways panel additions (automatic push, publish mode, conflicts) have only been tried through tests.
- The admin profile has been tried only with its test script (`scripts/compose-admin-e2e.sh`) on one Mac through Docker, not on the team server and not with a real browser. The default HTTPS port 443 and a host port other than 443 for a real certificate name are not covered by the test (it uses a high port). On Docker Desktop and rootless Docker the proxy sees the Docker gateway address for every browser, so `allowedSourceCIDRs` and the per-source lockout do not tell browsers apart there; real source filtering needs rootful Docker on Linux.
- Remote settings are not tried in a real browser or on Windows yet.
- The Upstream gateways panel has only been tried through tests, not in a real window or browser. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it (the check runs when the page opens). On the Mac, the routes answer 501 until the app answers the helper's callouts (a generic "the gateway reported an error" in the panel).
- The upstream push was tried once between two real gateways (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway, four notes); replacements of accepted notes and team notes have only been tried in tests.
- Archived projects and teams cannot be chosen in the Knowledge base pickers any more; read their notes with `minigw`.
- The panels on the settings page were tried on one Windows gateway in a Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not yet in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page.
- In the Teams and projects pane the "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI: the format has no cell that lists pairs of a team and a role. The structured list is in the answer (`teams`).
- The Teams and projects pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Team notes (the team's own notes segment) are not shown there, only in the Knowledge base pane.
- The Knowledge base pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Notes and questions are read, not edited; a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference.
- Labels are data, never permissions: a member sees them only on sessions and usage already visible to them, and a label filter never widens whose events are read. A call made by hand gets labels only when the caller sets them (or the branch fills the task); classifying unlabelled calls is not done yet.
- A role of the form `<phase>.<activity>` set by older scripts is not read as labels.
- The three admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base) are new and have only been tried through tests, not yet looked at in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page there is unchanged).
- A project linked to a subfolder of another project's folder gets no automatic dependency; the owner sets it with `gw project depends add`.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac; not yet on the team server. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way (nothing is pulled); deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them). Automatic push, publish mode and `resolve` are new and have only been tried in tests, not between two real gateways.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.24

### New
- Upstream gateway, step B (part 1): **automatic push**. `minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --auto` (and `--manual` to turn it off again; manual stays the default) makes the gateway push a link by itself: when a note of the project (or team) is published, edited, imported or accepted, the link is marked as having unsent changes (an outbox of two timestamps per link, in the database, so a restart loses nothing), and a background pusher sends it once the link has been quiet for a minute (at the latest ten minutes after the first change, so a stream of edits cannot hold it back for ever), one link at a time. A burst of edits is one push. After a failure it tries again after a minute, then 2, 4 and so on up to an hour (with jitter), and a write limit of the upstream is waited out, not counted as a failure. `gw upstream status` shows per link: auto or manual, dirty since, last success, last failure (and how many in a row), the reason, the next attempt, and the sent notes that were deleted or made private here (neither is propagated; the copy stays on the upstream until you remove it there). One audit row per automatic run, with counts only. It contacts only an upstream you added and a link you set to `--auto`. On the Mac the app's timer asks the helper (`POST /upstream-auto` on the upstream lane). Database schema 20.
- Upstream gateway, step B (part 2): **publish mode**. `minigw gw upstream link <project> <name> --mode publish` writes the notes on the upstream directly through its ordinary member routes (a note there, not a submission for its reviewers; the owner's device on the upstream needs the write role). A second push updates the same note there, but only if it is still as we left it: when its revision moved on the upstream (or the note is gone) our change is NOT written, the note is recorded as a **conflict**, shown by `gw upstream status` and in the push report until `minigw gw upstream resolve <project> <note> --theirs|--ours`: `--theirs` keeps the upstream's version and forgets our change for it, `--ours` sends our version as a new submission for the upstream's reviewers (never an overwrite). The mode of a link cannot be changed after it has sent notes (unlink and link again). A refusal is reported and the link keeps its mode. The Mac app admits exactly two more request shapes for it (create and update of a note, shared fixture). Works with the automatic push.
- Upstream gateway, step B (fixes): an automatic run that ends with "more" now remembers where it got to and goes on from there (it restarted at the first note), counts a run that sent nothing as a failure (the normal back-off), waits out a write limit's Retry-After and writes an audit row only when the outcome changes; the automatic push stops when the shutdown starts, not after the drain; one push of a link at a time (a second is refused with a clear answer, the timer skips it); after `resolve --ours` later edits of the note go on as submissions (no repeated conflict, no duplicate note).
- Docker Compose: in container mode a provider can be given by a Compose service name without a dot (`http://litellm:4000`, with `--insecure`). The name must resolve to a private address when the provider is added and at every connection (a name that later resolves to a public address is not followed); outside a container such a name is still refused.
- Docker Compose: the build and the deployment test (`scripts/compose-e2e.sh`) cover linux/amd64 as well as linux/arm64, and the test checks that the image build fails for a binary that does not match `SHA256SUMS`, a bad signature, a changed `SHA256SUMS`, a missing release and a signed version that is not the one asked for.
- `minigw gw settings password` sets the password for remote settings.
- Opt-in remote settings listener behind mutual TLS and a password login, off by default. The remote scope is limited
  to providers/models and devices; changes that carry an API key's destination (`baseURL`, `insecure`) or raise tool
  levels need the key again or must be made locally. The Compose reverse-proxy profile comes later.
- A panel **Upstream gateways** in the Mac Settings window and on the settings page of `minigw serve`: add an upstream (its token goes in a password field and is never shown again), see the device ID to approve there and what the upstream says, link projects and teams, run a dry run or push by hand (a push asks first and says what leaves), unlink and remove (both ask first). Nothing is sent automatically.
- Panel format: a field type `secret` (a password field whose value goes only in the body of a route that the provider registered as taking a secret, is cleared after every send and is never repeated in an error) and an action `result` (the answer shown after it succeeded). The admin API answers carry the extra fields a panel needs: `GET /upstreams/{name}/probe` (checks the connection when asked), link ids and class text, `?team=true` on a link's DELETE, `linesText` in a push report, and an empty `remote` on a link means the same name.

### Security
- Docker Compose: a provider given by a service name must resolve only to private addresses; link-local (such as the cloud metadata address `169.254.169.254`), loopback, unspecified and multicast addresses are refused at add time and at every connection. Such a name is dialled directly, not through `HTTP(S)_PROXY`, so the provider key is not sent to a proxy.

### Fixed
- The Mac gateway reads a provider's quota answer in chunks and stops at the 1 MB limit without waiting for the rest of the body, so a refresh on a busy machine no longer takes about a minute.

### Known problems
- The Upstream gateways panel does not yet show or set automatic push, publish mode and conflicts (use `minigw gw upstream ...`).
- Remote settings are not tried in a real browser or on Windows yet.
- The Upstream gateways panel has only been tried through tests, not in a real window or browser. Opening an upstream's detail page contacts it (the check runs when the page opens). On the Mac, the routes answer 501 until the app answers the helper's callouts (a generic "the gateway reported an error" in the panel).
- The upstream push was tried once between two real gateways (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway, four notes); replacements of accepted notes and team notes have only been tried in tests.
- Archived projects and teams cannot be chosen in the Knowledge base pickers any more; read their notes with `minigw`.
- The panels on the settings page were tried on one Windows gateway in a Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not yet in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page.
- In the Teams and projects pane the "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI: the format has no cell that lists pairs of a team and a role. The structured list is in the answer (`teams`).
- The Teams and projects pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Team notes (the team's own notes segment) are not shown there, only in the Knowledge base pane.
- The Knowledge base pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Notes and questions are read, not edited; a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference.
- Labels are data, never permissions: a member sees them only on sessions and usage already visible to them, and a label filter never widens whose events are read. A call made by hand gets labels only when the caller sets them (or the branch fills the task); classifying unlabelled calls is not done yet.
- A role of the form `<phase>.<activity>` set by older scripts is not read as labels.
- The three admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base) are new and have only been tried through tests, not yet looked at in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page there is unchanged).
- A project linked to a subfolder of another project's folder gets no automatic dependency; the owner sets it with `gw project depends add`.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried on arm64 and, through emulation, on amd64 on one Mac; not yet on the team server. Any container on the same Docker network can take a service name's alias and would then receive that provider's key: put only trusted containers on that network.
- Upstream gateway: one way (nothing is pulled); deletions and notes that became private are not propagated (status lists them). Automatic push, publish mode and `resolve` are new and have only been tried in tests, not between two real gateways.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.23

### Security
- Installing `minigw` from the Mac app now gives the gateway token to `minigw connect` on standard input (`--token -`) instead of on the command line, so other local users cannot see it in the process list while the connection is made.

### Fixed
- The share long poll and `minigw kb export`/`import` no longer retry without a limit: an answer that is not a usable job (or a 204 that came back at once) now waits with a growing delay up to 30 s, and a gateway that keeps answering 429 is given up on after 30 attempts or 10 minutes with a clear message.

### Known problems
- The upstream push was tried once between two real gateways (a Mac gateway to a Windows gateway, four notes); replacements of accepted notes and team notes have only been tried in tests.
- Upstream gateway: one way (nothing is pulled), manual (no automatic push yet), submit mode only (no direct publish), and deletions are not propagated. On the Mac, a long push is done in steps of at most about ten seconds (the helper answers one call within 30 seconds), so a rate-limited upstream makes `push` wait and ask again; the app makes the encrypted connection to the upstream itself, with no local listener, and accepts from its helper only the requests a push makes. The upstream routes have a connection of their own to the helper, so a slow upstream cannot hold up other requests. A run sends at most 1000 notes (new or changed ones; unchanged ones are passed over) and says where it stopped (`more`, `next`); the CLI asks again for that link and stops when a call gets no further. A push to a team's notes needs a gateway-bound user who is an admin of a project of the team there. The panel for upstreams is a later step.
- Archived projects and teams cannot be chosen in the Knowledge base pickers any more; read their notes with `minigw`.
- The panels on the settings page were tried on one Windows gateway in a Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not yet in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page.
- In the Teams and projects pane the "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI: the format has no cell that lists pairs of a team and a role. The structured list is in the answer (`teams`).
- The Teams and projects pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Team notes (the team's own notes segment) are not shown there, only in the Knowledge base pane.
- The Knowledge base pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Notes and questions are read, not edited; a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference.
- Labels are data, never permissions: a member sees them only on sessions and usage already visible to them, and a label filter never widens whose events are read. A call made by hand gets labels only when the caller sets them (or the branch fills the task); classifying unlabelled calls is not done yet.
- A role of the form `<phase>.<activity>` set by older scripts is not read as labels.
- The three admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base) are new and have only been tried through tests, not yet looked at in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page there is unchanged).
- A project linked to a subfolder of another project's folder gets no automatic dependency; the owner sets it with `gw project depends add`.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.22

### Fixed
- `minigw gw upstream add` now makes the new device appear in the upstream's list of waiting devices (it makes the first contact an ordinary client makes), and prints `waiting for approval on the upstream as <name> (<id>)`, so the upstream's owner can approve it without being told the ID separately. A gateway that sends notes also shows its client description (`gateway, minigw <version>`) and last contact on the upstream's device list, and an unknown device that only uses the project routes now waits for approval there too.
- `minigw gw kb review` shows the writer's name (not a number) when the gateway sends it.
- Right after an update, `minigw gw status` and the admin commands say that the gateway is starting (its project database is being opened or migrated) instead of "not running", on `minigw serve` (it sees that the database is held by the starting process) and on the Mac app (the helper is up but not ready).

## 0.7.21

### New
- Upstream gateway, step A (one way, by hand, submit mode): `minigw gw upstream add <name> <address>` (the upstream's Copy Token on stdin) makes this gateway a client of another one with a device key of its own; the upstream's owner allows the device, binds it to a user and gives that user a role. `gw upstream link <project> <name>` (or `--team`), `status`, `unlink`, `list`, `remove` and `gw upstream push [<project>] [--dry-run]` send the published notes of a linked project as submissions for the upstream's reviewers; a second push skips unchanged notes and sends a changed one as a replacement of the accepted note (or a new submission that names the earlier one). Private notes only with `--with-private`. The secret is kept like a provider key, never in the database or a backup; what an upstream answers is data only. Database schema 19. On the Mac app the app itself does the network and Keychain work for its helper (the credentials are Keychain items "upstream:<name>" next to the provider keys), and `minigw gw upstream` works the same as on `minigw serve`.
- Upstream gateway details: a push report shows the totals and at most 200 lines; `gw upstream add --device-name <text>` chooses the name shown on the upstream's device list (default `gateway <id>`, never the computer's name); the note's trailer carries only an opaque reference and the author's display name; `minigw uninstall --purge` also removes the upstream credentials; an archived project or team cannot be linked and nothing is sent for one that is archived later; removing an upstream removes its credentials even when the database row is already gone.

### Known problems
- Upstream gateway: one way (nothing is pulled), manual (no automatic push yet), submit mode only (no direct publish), and deletions are not propagated. On the Mac, a long push is done in steps of at most about ten seconds (the helper answers one call within 30 seconds), so a rate-limited upstream makes `push` wait and ask again; the app makes the encrypted connection to the upstream itself, with no local listener, and accepts from its helper only the requests a push makes. A push to a team's notes needs a gateway-bound user who is an admin of a project of the team there. The panel for upstreams is a later step.

## 0.7.20

### New
- A project can depend on a team: `minigw gw project depends add <project> --team <team>` (`remove` likewise; `list` shows teams too, and the Teams and projects pane has a "Depends on teams" list). The people of the project who are on the team's roster then also find the team's notes when they search the project (`from team:<name>`) and can open them with `minigw note read`; for everyone else nothing changes. It is a search hint, never a permission, and an agent run gets nothing from it. The cap of 16 counts both kinds; an archived team gives nothing; the project's own team is refused. A team dependency is not carried by a bundle (export/import). Database schema 18.

### Fixed
- `minigw gw status` shows a provider that is switched off in the settings as "disabled" (it said "models loading" for ever), and `minigw gw provider list` marks it `(disabled)`; the Mac commands do the same.
- `minigw gw team unarchive <team>` exists on both gateways (the API and the pane could already do it). Every archive pair (projects, teams) and activate pair (users) now has both commands in both command sets.
- A project of an archived team can be moved: adding it to another team now says which team holds it (and that it is archived) and how to release it (`minigw gw team project remove <team> <project>`, which works on an archived team). Adding to an archived team says which team and how to unarchive it.
- `minigw kb search --team <team>` (or with another project's name) prints one line under the hits saying how to read them (`minigw note read <id> --team <team>`, or from a folder linked to that project), and the "not found or not visible" answer of `note read`, `note edit` and `note rm` adds a line that a note of a team or another project needs `--team` or such a folder. Client-side text only: the gateway still does not tell a missing note from an invisible one. The MCP `note_read` reads in the same project as its `kb_search`, so it has no such gap.
- The bundle import counts team dependencies in the cap of 16 too (it counted only project dependencies). `minigw project depends` lists the teams you are on besides the projects. A restore now compares the body of every guard trigger with the one this program creates, not only its name: a backup whose trigger keeps the name but never fires is refused.
- Refusals that are sentences no longer read "invalid ..." in front: team, project, user, grant, remote and dependency refusals ("a team with this name already exists", "a project cannot depend on itself", ...), also in the API answers.

- Archived projects and teams cannot be chosen in the Knowledge base pickers any more; read their notes with `minigw`.

- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.

- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.19

### Fixed
- `minigw gw status` shows a provider whose model list is still loading as "models loading (not loaded yet)", no longer as a failure, and one whose load failed as "models failed to load: <reason> (see the gateway log)". The gateway log now has one line for that failure with the provider name and the reason (no key, no address with credentials). The Mac gateway words the status the same way.

### Known problems
- Archived projects and teams cannot be chosen in the Knowledge base pickers any more; read their notes with `minigw`.
- The panels on the settings page were tried on one Windows gateway in a Chromium browser (they work; no Content Security Policy error); not yet in Edge or Firefox, and not on Linux. A detail page opens in place of the panel with a Back button. The Mac app does not use this page.
- In the Teams and projects pane the "through teams" column shows the role names of the API in English (`acme (reader)`) also in the Slovenian UI: the format has no cell that lists pairs of a team and a role. The structured list is in the answer (`teams`).
- The Teams and projects pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Team notes (the team's own notes segment) are not shown there, only in the Knowledge base pane.
- The Knowledge base pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Notes and questions are read, not edited; a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference.
- Labels are data, never permissions: a member sees them only on sessions and usage already visible to them, and a label filter never widens whose events are read. A call made by hand gets labels only when the caller sets them (or the branch fills the task); classifying unlabelled calls is not done yet.
- A role of the form `<phase>.<activity>` set by older scripts is not read as labels.
- The three admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base) are new and have only been tried through tests, not yet looked at in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page there is unchanged).
- A project linked to a subfolder of another project's folder gets no automatic dependency; the owner sets it with `gw project depends add`.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.18

### Fixed
- `minigw kb export` followed by `minigw kb import` on the same gateway and project no longer makes a second copy of every note: a file now names the gateway and project it was exported from (`origin` in its front matter), and importing it back there is the same note: unchanged content is skipped, an edited file updates that note (a note changed here since the export is kept and reported as a local edit). A file never claims a note by its number alone: a file from another gateway or project, a note you may not write, or one that no longer exists is imported as a new note, as before. Exports made before this version have no origin and import as they did.
- A refusal or "not found" from a gateway (for example "this device is not bound to a user") now says which profile and address it came from when you have more than one profile, with the hint `--minigw-profile <name>`. `MINIGW_PROFILE=<name>` is the environment form of `--minigw-profile`.
- Importing a gateway's own bundle now says "this bundle was exported by this gateway: a gateway cannot import its own bundle"; the other bundle refusals that were glued to "invalid" or "refuse" read as sentences too.
- The gateway log names the snapshot it takes of the project database before a schema upgrade. The snapshot is `core-<schema>-<time>.db` in the `core` folder next to `core.db` (not in `core\backups`, which holds `minigw gw core backup` files); the newest three are kept.
- Panels on the settings page: the OK button of a form now enables as soon as the form is valid (it waited until the field lost focus), Enter in a field sends the form and Escape cancels it. The Mac pane already did both.
- Panels, both apps: an empty value in a detail row shows an en dash instead of a bare label ("Through teams:" with nothing after it).
- Panels: pickers (the project and team choosers of Knowledge base, the person and project choosers of Teams and projects, the person chooser of Users and devices) offer only active entries: no archived project or team, no inactive user. The panel format gets one small optional key for it, `skip` on a `refs` entry. Names of archived or inactive entries still show where a value refers to them.
- Panels: roles show their technical name beside the friendly one ("Manage (admin)", "Develop (developer)", "Read (reader)"), so the panel matches `minigw gw` and the documentation.
- Settings page: the browser's automatic `/favicon.ico` request no longer ends in a 401 line in the console: the settings server answers it with 204 (same Host and loopback checks as the page), and the page carries an empty icon so the browser does not ask at all.

## 0.7.17

### New
- The settings page of `minigw serve` (Windows, Linux, Docker) draws the same three admin panels as the Mac app: Users and devices, Teams and projects and Knowledge base. Same rules as the Mac renderer (registered routes only, one plain path segment per value, confirmations before destructive or access-changing actions, plain-text rendering of every text, English or Slovenian by the browser's language). It is covered by a script test over the same fixtures as the Go validator and the Swift model.

### Security
- Git on your computer and on the gateway: the git calls minigw makes in a repository it does not control now refuse more of what that repository's own configuration could ask git to do (programs and transports). Two calls were affected on your computer: fetching the agent's branch back in worktree mode, which could follow a configuration that redirected the fetch or listed refs through a program of the repository's choosing. The gateway's own calls were not affected. A test now lists every place minigw starts git and fails when one lacks the shared guard.

### Fixed
- On the Mac gateway, `minigw gw usage --by ...` and `--phase`, `--activity`, `--task` answered "unknown option" (the Mac command's own option check had not been given the new options). They work now, with the same output as on Windows and Linux, and `--legacy` refuses them as it does the other report options. A test now feeds every option that the help text of `gw usage`, `gw usage-events`, `gw project-sessions` and `minigw project sessions` names to the real argument parser (Mac and Go).

## 0.7.16

### New
- Mac Settings: a **Teams and projects** pane (a panel): teams (add, rename, path label, archive, people, projects, roles through the team), projects (add, archive, people with the role and where it comes from, set or remove a direct role, git identities, dependencies) and grants between projects. Every access-changing action asks first and says what happens to running agent runs. The admin API answers carry the extra fields it needs (counts, names, `directRole` and `fromTeams`, `memberCount`), `DELETE /teams/{id}/assignments` also takes `?project=&user=`, and the owner's remotes route takes `addRemote` and `removeRemote`. No format change.
- Mac Settings: a **Knowledge base** pane (a panel, like Users and devices): review the pending submissions of projects and teams with **Accept** and **Reject…** (with a reason), open one to read its text and, for a replacement, the note it replaces, and read the notes and the questions and answers of a project or a team. The admin API gains three read routes for it, `GET /kb/notes`, `GET /kb/notes/{id}` and `GET /kb/faq` (owner only, admin channel only), and the review answers carry the author's and the project's or team's name. The owner reads every class there, `private` notes included. The panel format gained a `record` element, a list `scope` picker, a `when` for a record in a detail page and an empty JSON body (`"body": {}`).
- Usage by phase, activity and task: a run can carry three optional labels, `--minigw-phase` (suggested: plan, do, check, act), `--minigw-activity` (feature, bugfix, security, refactor, docs, test, release, research, ops) and `--minigw-task` (an issue number, a branch), or `MINIGW_PHASE`, `MINIGW_ACTIVITY`, `MINIGW_TASK`. A label never refuses or delays a run: a missing one is stored empty, an unusual value is kept as given when it is a short safe word, and a value that is not one is dropped with one warning line. In a folder linked to a project and inside a git repository, an empty task is filled with the current branch name (marked `branch`; read from the repository's files, no git command runs). Each label records where it came from (`given`, `branch`).
- `minigw gw usage --by phase|activity|task|project|model` (one or two, comma separated, for example `--by project,phase`) shows requests, share, failures, tokens, cached tokens, cost and failed agent minutes per group; usage without a label is its own row, `(unlabelled)`, with its share. `--phase`, `--activity` and `--task` filter `gw usage`, `gw usage-events`, `gw project-sessions` and `minigw project sessions`; sessions show the labels of their first request. The table shows the top 50 groups and one `(other)` row; `--json` with `--by` gives the same groups and numbers (without `--by` it is the API answer). A report split by label returns at most the newest 5000 rows and says when it was cut. Filter values are matched like stored ones (`--phase Do` finds `do`); a value that is not a safe word matches nothing.
- The plugin skills for models (ask, codex, review, copilot) tell Claude to set the phase and activity when it knows them (review: check; codex: do) with `--minigw-phase` and `--minigw-activity` (these skills need a client that knows the flags; an older one is told to leave them out).

### Changed
- The project database schema is now 17 (a snapshot is taken before the migration). An older gateway cannot open it. Old usage rows have no labels. Daily totals older than 400 days (folded) carry none and show as unlabelled.
- `minigw gw project-sessions` and `minigw project sessions` on the Mac no longer send an empty `after=` on the first page (as on Windows and Linux).

### Fixed
- The plugin's knowledge base hook could wait without end for git: it read git's output until the pipe closed, and another process that had inherited the pipe kept it open. It now reads without waiting for that (this also hung the project's own tests on small machines since 0.7.14).

### Known problems

- The Knowledge base pane has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Notes and questions are read, not edited; a replacement shows both texts, not a line difference.
- Labels are data, never permissions: a member sees them only on sessions and usage already visible to them, and a label filter never widens whose events are read. A call made by hand gets labels only when the caller sets them (or the branch fills the task); classifying unlabelled calls is not done yet.

- The three admin panes of the Mac app (Users and devices, Teams and projects, Knowledge base) are new and have only been tried through tests, not yet looked at in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page there is unchanged).
