## 0.7.15

### New
- Admin panels: the owner's admin API has a new read route, `GET /panels`, that describes panels declaratively (one validated format, no code or markup; texts are keys, with per-language tables from `GET /panels/strings`, English and Slovenian), and the Mac app draws them natively. The first panel is **Users and devices** in the Settings window, after Devices: add, rename, deactivate and activate users, bind and unbind devices. Device approval stays in Devices. See "Panels" in the admin API document.
- Project dependencies: the owner records that a project depends on another (`minigw gw project depends add <project> <other>`, `remove`, `list`), across teams, at most 16 per project. A person's search of a project, and `note read` of a hit, then also cover the projects it depends on, each only as far as that person could read it directly (membership or grant): a dependency is a search hint and gives no permission. Such a hit ends with `from project:<slug>`. `minigw project depends [<project>]` lists the dependencies you can read. Team bundles carry the relation between projects that are both in the bundle (`gw import --dry-run` lists what it would add and skips names the gateway does not have).
- `minigw kb submissions --team <team>` lists your own submissions to a team's notes, and `minigw kb submissions` without an argument now also lists those of the teams you are on (labelled `team <name>`). `minigw team show <team>` says how many team notes exist and how to read them (`minigw note list --team <team>`); nothing for a team you are not on.

### Changed
- The project database schema is now 16 (a snapshot is taken before the migration). An older gateway cannot open it.
- Only one level is followed: a dependency of a dependency is not searched. A cycle is allowed and harmless.
- An agent run gets nothing from a dependency by itself: it reads another project only through an owner grant, exactly as before; a dependency only labels and orders the hits such a grant already allows.

### Fixed
- Search in a large knowledge base was slow in 0.7.14: the new order (own project first) made the database sort every match before taking the first 20. The query now takes two bounded selections; the order of hits is the same.
- A hit, note or submission from a team's own notes no longer prints a hidden number as its project: the line says `team <name>` (the `from team:<name>` ending stays only for a hit that comes from another source than the one asked), and the MCP tools show the team name, not the id. `note_read` still takes the note id it was given.

### Known problems
- The Users and devices pane is new and has only been tried through tests, not in a real window. Windows and Linux have no panel renderer yet (the settings page and `minigw gw` still cover the same routes); the terminal UI neither. A detail page of a panel opens as a sheet, and no built-in panel uses one yet.
- A project linked to a subfolder of another project's folder gets no automatic dependency; the owner sets it with `gw project depends add`.
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.14

### New
- Team notes: a team now has notes of its own (its "general segment"). Every member of the team reads them; a member who is admin of at least one of the team's projects writes them (`structure` and `data` only, never `private`). Use `--team <team>` instead of a project on `minigw kb submit|import|export|search|faq` and on `minigw note list|read|push|edit|rm`. The owner reviews a submission to a team with `minigw gw kb review --team <team>`.
- A search of a project also covers its team's notes, as far as you may read them. A hit from the team ends with `from team:<name>`, and `minigw note read <id>` opens it. Your own project's hits come first. Inside an agent run, search and `note read` also cover the team notes when the person who started the run is on the team; `note list` stays the run's own project.
- `minigw gw export` and `import` carry the team's notes (files `@team/<id>.md`); `--project @team` exports only them. `minigw team show --json` and the team API show `segment`, the id the notes routes take for the team's notes.

### Changed
- The project database schema is now 15 (a snapshot is taken before the migration). An older gateway cannot open it, and it refuses a bundle that carries team notes.
- Search hits list the hits of the project asked first, then the others, each by recency (a run's hits from granted projects follow its own project's).

### Known problems
- Team notes have no `private` class, and only a project admin of the team writes them; there is no owner shortcut into them (the owner reviews submissions and exports bundles).
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.13

### New
- Opt-in session hooks in the Claude Code plugin: `minigw kb hooks on|off|status [<project>]` switches, per project and on this computer only, hooks that tell a session about the project's knowledge base and, once after a commit or a compaction, ask the model to hand in at most three short entries with `minigw kb submit`. Nothing is on after installing or updating the plugin; every submit goes through Claude Code's Bash prompt (unless you allow-listed minigw) and the members' review. The plugin calls the client only from version 0.7.13. See "Hand findings in from a session" in the usage guide.

### Known problems
- The hooks ask after a commit or a compaction, not at the end of a session: Claude Code gives a hook no way to talk to the model then. A failed hand-in is not retried. Checked by tests of both clients and of the plugin script, but not yet tried in a real Claude Code session (neither on a Mac nor on Windows).

- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).

## 0.7.12

### New
- Git identity of a project: a project admin or the owner registers the git remotes of a project (`minigw project remote add --here`, `minigw gw project remote add <project> <url>`). `minigw project link` without a slug then finds the project from this folder's `origin`. Credentials in the URL are removed on your computer before anything is sent, and the gateway keeps only `host/path` in lower case.
- Team path label: the owner can give a team a free-text path such as `acme/backend` (`minigw gw team path <team> <label>`), and `minigw project list --path acme` lists your projects of the teams whose label starts with it. A label never gives access.
- Knowledge entries have a kind, `finding` (the default) or `faq` (a question and a short answer): `minigw kb submit --kind faq`, `minigw kb faq` lists the published questions, and search, submissions, review, Markdown copies and team bundles show the kind.

### Changed
- The project database schema is now 14 (a snapshot is taken before the migration). An older gateway cannot open a schema 14 database, and it refuses a team bundle that carries the new fields (`team_path`, `remotes`).

### Known problems
- Project git identity, team path labels and entry kinds are new and not yet in daily use. `minigw project link` without a slug needs `git` on the computer; on Windows it has only been built, not tried.
- A replacement of a `faq` entry keeps the kind `faq`, and an import never changes the kind of an existing note: to change a kind, remove the entry and hand it in again.
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.11

### Security
- Bundle import: a manifest field name that matches a known field only after Unicode case folding (such as `uſers` for `users`) is refused before decoding, so the collection limits cannot be dodged; names must be spelled exactly.
- Bundle import: a note file declared larger than the note size limit plus front matter, and any archive entry the manifest does not list, are refused from the archive's metadata before any body is decompressed.
- `minigw kb export` now writes through the opened destination folder, so swapping the folder during the export cannot send notes elsewhere; a destination that is a symbolic link is refused.

### Known problems
- `minigw gw import`: an `INDEX.md` inside a bundle has no size limit of its own (only the 100 MiB total). Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export` on Windows: the protection of each exported file is still set by path after the file is created through the opened folder; not tested on Windows yet.
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.10

### New
- Knowledge base as Markdown: `minigw kb export <project> <dir>` writes one Markdown file per published note you may read, with an index; `minigw kb import <project> <dir|file.md> [--class c] [--submit]` creates notes from Markdown files (with `--submit`: proposals for review). A second import of the same files updates or skips, it does not duplicate, and a note edited here since the last import is kept and reported.
- Copying a team and its knowledge base to another gateway (owner, on the gateway computer): `minigw gw export --team <team> [--project p ...] [--with-private] <file.zip>` writes one file with the team, its projects, members and roles, and the notes; `minigw gw import --source <name> <file.zip> [--dry-run] [--map old=new ...]` on the other gateway (`--source` is your own name for the gateway the file came from; later imports from it must use the same name) shows what would be created and then imports in one step. The file carries no devices, tokens, keys, usage or gateway identity; private notes only with `--with-private`; users who do not exist on the target are created inactive and without devices. It is a one-way copy, not a sync, and it is not a database backup.

### Changed
- When a device has no project access, the answer now says which condition is missing and what the owner runs: the device is not on the gateway's device list (`minigw gw devices allow DEVICE-ID`), or it is not bound to a user (`minigw gw bind DEVICE-ID USER`), with the caller's own device id. `minigw status` on the gateway computer no longer says "(approved)" for a client that is accepted only as a local client: it says that it is not on the device list and has no project access. `minigw gw bind` warns when the device is not on the device list yet.

### Known limits
- Database downgrade: this version's database schema is 13. An older program refuses it; the way back is restoring the snapshot written before the first migration.

### Known problems
- Copying a team to another gateway (`minigw gw export --team`, `minigw gw import`) is new and has not been used between two real gateways yet; try `--dry-run` first. The file is not signed: the gateway cannot prove where it came from, the owner names the source with `--source`.
- `minigw gw import`: an oversized note or a file not listed in the bundle is unpacked into memory (at most 100 MiB in total) before it is refused. Import only bundles you made yourself.
- `minigw kb export`: export into a folder whose parent only you can write. With a parent that another user can write, the folder can be swapped during the export and notes written elsewhere.
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.9

### Changed
- `minigw kb mcp`: the two tools now say what they do in their descriptions (search the project's knowledge base; read one note by id), so a model that lists the tools knows when to use them. The warning that results are untrusted reference content stays in the descriptions, in the server's instructions and in front of every result.

### Fixed
- Mac gateway: shares are no longer dropped when the gateway itself was stalled or the Mac slept. A share is dropped after a minute without a sign of its client; when the gateway's own check ran late, it used to drop every share at once, and the next request of a running session failed. Now a late check gives every share a fresh minute.
- Go gateway: a project whose slug is `sessions` got a wrong answer for its own project page.
- `minigw kb mcp` on the Mac client keeps large note ids exact.

### Known problems
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and not yet in daily use.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.8

### New
- `minigw kb mcp [--project <slug>]`: a read-only MCP server for the knowledge base that an MCP client (Claude Code and others) starts as a command. It has two tools, `kb_search` and `note_read`, with your rights in that project; inside an agent run of the gateway it uses the run's rights and takes no project. Results are marked as untrusted reference content, cleaned of control characters and limited in size. `minigw kb mcp --print-config` prints the entry for the MCP client's configuration (no token in it).
- Sessions of a project, without any prompt or answer text: `minigw project sessions [<project>] [--days n] [--model m] [--device d] [--json]` lists who ran what and when (user, device, model, start, duration, requests, tokens, cost, outcome). A member sees own sessions; a project admin sees all sessions of the project. The owner has the same list on the gateway computer.

### Fixed
- Mac gateway: a `minigw run` command listed the temp folder on a thread of the app's shared thread pool; with a very full temp folder and several runs at once the gateway could stop answering for seconds. It now runs on its own thread.
- `minigw kb search --json`, `note read --json` and `kb submissions --json` write terminal control characters in note text as JSON `\\uXXXX` escapes, so a note cannot steer the terminal of a person who prints the JSON; a program that parses it gets the same text as before.
- `minigw kb search` shows the time of a note in local time, like `note list`.

### Known problems
- `minigw kb mcp` and `minigw project sessions` are new and proved with tests and reviews, not yet in daily use. A project whose slug is `sessions` gets a wrong answer from the Go gateway for its own project page.
- GitHub's Swift tests: the hang is fixed and they finish; that the job is green with Go installed on the runner is not yet confirmed.
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.7

### New
- `minigw gw usage` shows the input tokens read from a provider's prompt cache in a column "cached in" (it appears when any model used a cache); `--csv` and `--json` carry cache read and cache write tokens. With automatic prompt caching most of the input is cache reads, which the report did not show.

### Fixed
- Mac gateway: every running Claude CLI agent held one thread of the app's shared thread pool while it read the agent's error output, and listeners and connections were started on queues that cannot start while that pool is full. With as many agent runs or blocked calls as the Mac has cores, the gateway could stop answering until one of them ended. The read now runs on its own thread and the queues have their own priority.
- Restoring a database backup now rebuilds the knowledge base search index from the notes, so a damaged index in a backup is not installed; if the rebuild fails, the restore fails and the old database stays.

### Known problems
- That the Swift tests now finish on GitHub's 3-core runner is not yet confirmed (they finish here with a pool of one thread).
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64; a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot; the connect line shows the container's own address.
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a modified client can declare a lower tool level than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules. Not available on Windows clients.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.6

### New
- `--minigw-tools=edit`: a provider model (LiteLLM, OpenRouter, xAI) in the local Claude Code may also use Edit and Write, only inside the current folder and never in `.git`, hooks, `.claude` settings, `.envrc` or `.vscode/tasks.json`; still no Bash, MCP or sub-agents. Allowed inside Claude Code; an error with `--minigw-ro` and for agent models. The gateway owner sets the highest level per provider or model (`minigw gw provider tools <name> [none|read|edit|default] [--model m]`, Settings, admin API; default `read`); clients refuse `edit` above it and the gateway refuses requests above it (a modified client can lie; the folder is protected by the client's own rules).
- Linux gateway in Docker Compose (`deploy/docker`, [docker.md](docker.md)), providers only: the image is built from the signed GitHub release (signature, signed version and checksum are checked); Claude CLI, Codex, Grok, Copilot and Foundry Local are off and cannot be turned on in container mode (`MINIGW_CONTAINER=1`, set by the image), in the settings, an imported `serve.json` or at run time. The first start creates the config without printing the token (`docker compose exec minigw minigw gw token`).
- `minigw serve healthcheck`: a bounded request to `/health/live` on loopback, no credentials and no provider calls (used by the Docker `HEALTHCHECK`).
- Shutdown (SIGTERM or Ctrl+C) of `minigw serve` now stops new requests, lets running ones finish for up to 30 seconds, cuts the rest, and only then closes the project database and saves the state.

### Known limits
- `--minigw-tools=edit` is refused on Windows clients: its path rules cannot cover trailing dots and spaces or 8.3 short names.
- `--minigw-tools=edit` does not protect files that are run or obeyed later without a person reading them (`Makefile`, `package.json` scripts, `.github/workflows`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.vscode/settings.json`, …): review the diff.

### Known problems
- Docker Compose: tried only on arm64 (an amd64 build is not yet confirmed); a provider address without a dot such as `http://litellm:4000` is refused, use a network alias with a dot (`litellm.local`); the connect line shows the container's own address, replace it with the host's; the failure paths of the signature check were not exercised.
- `--minigw-tools=edit` and the gateway's tool level are new: the level a client declares is checked by the gateway, but a modified client can declare less than it uses; the folder is protected by the client's own rules.
- `minigw running` and the steps of a run: a real long Grok run against a Windows gateway is not yet confirmed.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.5

### New
- Knowledge base, provenance: every note records who wrote it and from where (user, device, project, kind of source, content hash, times, reviewer). `minigw note read <id> --json` shows it. Notes written before this version show `UNKNOWN` for the device and the source.
- Knowledge base, submissions: `minigw kb submit <project> <file|->` proposes a note (or a new revision with `--replaces <id> --revision <n>`) instead of publishing it; `minigw kb submissions` lists your own. The owner reviews with `minigw gw kb review`, `gw kb accept <id>` and `gw kb reject <id> [--reason text]`. A pending submission is visible only to its author and the owner and never appears in note lists, reads, agent runs or counts. `minigw note push` still publishes directly.
- Knowledge base, search: `minigw kb search [<project>] <query> [--json]` searches the published notes you may read (title and text; at most 20 hits with short snippets). An agent started by the gateway can search the notes of its run's project the same way and is told so; note texts are still not put into its context. Pending submissions and notes you may not read never show up, also not in counts. The owner can rebuild the index.
- A Codex, Grok or Copilot run (Mac gateway: Codex and Copilot) shows its steps in `minigw -p` as they happen, paths relative to your folder; the answer on stdout never contains them. The Mac client understands the step lines of a Windows or Linux gateway.
- `minigw running` lists your device's requests that run on the gateway now: model, folder, time, steps and last step. Both gateways.

### Known limits
- Database downgrade: this version's database schema is 12. An older program refuses it; the way back is restoring the snapshot written before the first migration.
- Only the owner reviews submissions; project admins cannot yet. Agents started by the gateway cannot hand in submissions yet.
- Search is by words, in one project at a time; search across related projects and by meaning comes later.

### Known problems
- `minigw running` and the steps of a run are new and proved only with test agents; a real long Grok run against a Windows gateway is not yet confirmed.
- `kb search --json` prints note titles and snippets without removing terminal control characters (the plain output removes them). After restoring a backup the search index is not rebuilt automatically: the owner can rebuild it.
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered. Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).

## 0.7.4

### New
- xAI works as a provider (base URL `https://api.x.ai`): mid-conversation `system` messages and tool schemas without `required` are rewritten for it on both gateways.

### Fixed
- Mac, Claude CLI in a direct-mode mount: a file the agent creates and commits is recorded as 100644, not 100755 (git ignores the executable bit on the mount, whose files all show as -rwx------); a file that is executable in the repository keeps 100755.
- Mac gateway, direct-mode mount: a repository that tracks symbolic links gets no commits by the agent through the mount, because a mounted folder shows a link as a plain file and `git add -A` committed it as one. The client decides at share time and prints why; the gateway reads a missing decision as closed (an older client therefore gets no commits through the mount). The agent's note says so.
- The end-of-run git report (both clients) now names mode and type changes (a symbolic link or an executable file changed, added or deleted) in the commits reachable at the end and for the same path in the index, for example `link 120000->100644`. It covers the final reachable state: a commit amended away or left unreachable before the end, and an entry that was renamed and changed type in the index, are not seen.
- While Codex, Grok, Copilot or a Claude CLI agent works silently, the gateway now sends an empty text delta with every ping, so Claude Code sees a real model event and no longer drops a long agent request (#131).

### Known problems
- The Mac permission to commit through a mount is a snapshot taken when the share is registered: a symbolic link staged or checked out later in the run can still be committed as a plain file; the report above names it afterwards.
- Linux: an agent with write access to an allowed folder can still plant or alter git metadata there (as in 0.7.0, #127).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables. Grok as an agent is not supported on the Mac gateway (xAI works as a provider).
- A long agent request that is cut is started again by Claude Code without a message; the cause seen so far (a silent stream) is fixed in this version on both gateways, but a real long run against a Windows gateway is not yet confirmed.

## 0.7.3

### Fixed
- `minigw gw member remove` for a user whose access comes only from a team now says so and names the `gw team access remove` command, instead of "not found".
- Restoring a database backup now refuses a backup whose project memberships do not match their sources.
- Team, user and project names with control or invisible formatting characters (such as a line break) are refused.
- `minigw team list` and `team show` on the Mac report an unreadable gateway answer as an error instead of showing an empty list or a team with ID 0.
- Reading the database: a schema number outside the valid range is refused instead of being cut to a smaller number.
- Tests: the automated tests on GitHub (Windows and macOS runners) failed or hung since 0.6.0 because of assumptions about the test machine; they are bounded and corrected. No change in the programs.

### Known problems
- Windows gateway: a link whose target names its UNC share by an 8.3 short alias is refused (it fails closed).
- Direct mode on a Mac gateway with a remote client: files that an agent creates and commits are recorded as executable (mode 100755), and a tracked symbolic link can be committed as a regular file. Check `git diff --summary` before pushing such commits.
- Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway. Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables.

## 0.7.2

### New
- Teams (access): `minigw gw team access set <team> <project> <user> <role>` gives a team member a role in one of the team's projects, `... access remove` takes it back, `... access list <team>` shows them. A team assignment creates the same project membership as a direct one (`minigw gw member`); when a user has several, the strongest role wins, and removing a team assignment does not remove a direct one. Removing the user from the team, the project from the team, or archiving the team takes that access away at once. `minigw project list` shows the role and where it comes from.

### Known limits
- Database downgrade: this version's database schema is 10. An older program refuses it; the way back is restoring the snapshot written before the first migration, by path, with `minigw gw core restore` (`core-9-<time>.db` when you come from 0.7.1, `core-8-<time>.db` from 0.7.0).

### Known problems
- `minigw gw member remove` for a user whose access comes only from a team answers "not found"; the team still gives the access. Use `minigw gw team access remove`.
- Unarchiving a team does not bring back its assignments; set them again.
- Direct mode on a Mac gateway with a remote client: files that an agent creates and commits are recorded as executable (mode 100755), and a tracked symbolic link can be committed as a regular file. Do not let an agent commit in a repository that tracks symbolic links; use worktree mode there. Fixes follow.
- Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway. Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables.

## 0.7.1

### New
- Teams (first step): the owner can group users and projects into teams with `minigw gw team add|list|rename|archive`, `minigw gw team member add|remove <team> <user>` and `minigw gw team project add|remove <team> <project>`; a member sees their own teams with `minigw team list|show <team>`. A project belongs to at most one team; a user can be in several. In this version a team is a directory only: being in a team gives no project role and no access to notes yet.

### Changed
- Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway (the Mini Gateway app). It worked in 0.5.29 and could not start since 0.6.0. Every Grok request answers 400 "Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway. Use another agent, or Grok through minigw serve on Windows or Linux." with `x-should-retry: false`; the Mac Settings no longer have a Grok CLI pane, the Mac admin API answers the same 400 for `/agents/grok`, and `minigw gw grok` says so. Grok through `minigw serve` on Windows and Linux is not affected, and a Mac client can use its models.

### Security
- Notes: the gateway now checks the permission before it loads a note's text (for member reads, reads by an agent run, edit, delete and class change), and checks it again before it repeats a saved answer to a retried request. A note that is missing and a note you may not see give the same answer.

### Known limits
- Database downgrade: this version's database schema is 9. An older program refuses it; the way back is restoring the snapshot `core-8-<time>.db` from the core folder by path with `minigw gw core restore`.

### Known problems
- Direct mode on a Mac gateway with a remote client: files that an agent creates and commits are recorded as executable (mode 100755), and a tracked symbolic link can be committed as a regular file (the mounted folder cannot represent links). Do not let an agent commit in a repository that tracks symbolic links; use worktree mode there. Fixes follow.
- Grok is not supported on the Mac gateway (see Changed).
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables.
- A commit by an agent in a repository without `user.name` and `user.email` gets git's automatic identity of the gateway computer.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices. Usage days are UTC days.

## 0.7.0

Mini Gateway 0.7.0 is a stable release. It collects the eleven beta versions 0.6.0 to 0.6.10, which stable users never saw, and is the first stable release since 0.5.29. It is the same code as beta 0.6.10. The sections below give the detail per beta. Read **Changed**, **Known limits** and **Known problems** before you update a gateway that other people use.

### New
- Project database ("core"): the gateway keeps users, device bindings, projects, members, grants, notes, usage events and an audit trail in a SQLite database. The owner manages it with `minigw gw user|bind|project|member|grant|core|usage-events|quota`, the same on the Mac app and on `minigw serve`. Roles are reader, developer and admin. If the database cannot be opened, model requests still work and project routes answer 503 `core_unavailable`. An upgrade or an uninstall never removes it; only `--purge` does.
- Projects and notes on the client: `minigw project list|link|unlink|show` and `minigw note list|read|push|edit|rm`. A folder linked to a project passes the project to agent runs; the agent can read the project's notes (never `private` ones) with `minigw note list|read`. Plugin skills `/minigw:project`, `/minigw:notes` and `/minigw:usage` were added.
- Usage statistics: `minigw gw usage` shows requests, tokens and cost per provider and model and per day (`--days`, `--provider`, `--model`, `--json`, `--csv`, `--sort failed`). A reported cost is shown plain, an estimated one with "~". Failed requests and failed agent minutes are counted. `minigw usage` shows a member's own events.
- Usage page: the Mac Settings and the browser Settings of `minigw serve` show requests, tokens, spend and failed time per day and model for 7, 30 or 90 days.
- Quota: `minigw gw quota` shows the Codex quota and the OpenRouter and LiteLLM credit; the Claude quota is off until you turn it on (`--claude-probe=on`). `minigw gw quota --history [--days N] [--provider P]` lists the stored probes of the last 400 days.
- Tool limits for provider models (LiteLLM, OpenRouter) in the local Claude Code: `--minigw-tools=none|read`. `none` gives no tools, `read` gives Read, Grep and Glob only. `--minigw-ro` now means `read` for these models.
- `minigw gw sync list --device ID` and `minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID` free synced-folder slots without waiting 14 days.
- Kernel sandbox on the Mac: the Claude CLI without "Allow local tools" runs inside a Seatbelt profile under the Claude Code rules. A self-check turns it off if `claude` cannot run under it, and says so in the log, in `minigw gw status` and in Settings.
- Direct mode on the Mac gateway: an agent can commit in your repository (`git add`, `commit`, `branch`, `checkout`, `stash`, `tag`, `merge` of a local branch). The end-of-run git watch is stricter.
- `minigw status` shows the gateway's version. `minigw project list` against an older gateway says which version it is.
- `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` lists the licences of the built-in Go modules.

### Changed
- `minigw gw usage` is the new usage report. The old per-folder and per-device counters need `minigw gw usage --legacy`.
- Usage reports show the provider in lowercase, and the `provider` filter ignores case.
- Direct mode (all clients): git writes inside a submodule or a nested clone, and to `refs/replace`, are refused. Use worktree mode for them. `git rebase` and multi-commit cherry-pick by an agent fail.
- `--minigw-ro` with a provider model gives Read, Grep and Glob only and loads no user, project or local Claude settings or hooks. A refused `@` in a prompt says to pass the text on stdin.
- Mac, restricted Claude CLI runs: they do not load auto-memory or your user hooks and plugins, and `~/.claude.json` is read-only for them. A run whose session or allowed folder is `/` is refused.
- Mac, Codex in write mode: only the session and allowed folders and a private temporary folder are writable; other places in `/tmp` are not.
- Windows and Linux (`minigw serve`), restricted Claude CLI: the same tool and path restrictions the Mac has had since 0.5.28 (no Task or MCP tools, permission mode `auto` or `plan` is used as `dontAsk`, stricter Write and `.git` rules, allowed folders with special characters are refused).
- Windows and Linux: where an agent has no sandbox, the core stays closed until the owner runs `minigw gw claude --accept-agents-read-notes=true` and restarts. Until then project routes answer 503.
- An allowed folder is refused, with the reason shown (Settings, log, `minigw serve` browser Settings), when its real path cannot be resolved, or when its recorded volume id is no longer reported. Remove the folder and add it again. A refused folder is logged once per reason.
- A link that leaves a shared folder is listed over WebDAV as a refused entry (403 with the reason), not as an empty file.
- `minigw share remove` with a name that is not shared is an error (exit status not 0).
- The answer when a device has reached its number of synced folders names `minigw gw sync remove`.
- A spending or key limit of OpenRouter is reported as "OpenRouter: spending limit reached" and does not make the client retry, also when the provider is renamed.
- After a failed agent stream, the repeat request of Claude Code gets the same error at once (`x-should-retry: false`) and no second agent run starts. A Grok run that needs command approval ends with a clear 400.
- `minigw gw core backup` writes only into `<core>/backups` (a name, not a path). Copy a backup out if you want to keep it, because it is deleted with the core folder.
- Mac: requests with a project and the project routes answer 503 `core_unavailable` while the helper `minigw-core` is not running. Model requests without a project are not affected.
- `minigw serve`: the Claude quota probe is off by default. The Windows and Linux `minigw` is about 4.5 MB larger (SQLite driver); the Mac app gains the helper `minigw-core`.
- Allowed folders that contain or are inside the gateway's own folders are refused (since 0.5.29), and a hard link to a core file makes the core and every agent launch refuse until the link is removed.

### Security
- No agent can read the core folder (Mac, Linux, Windows); the credential homes of other agents are closed to every agent of another kind; a hard link to a core file or a junction to the core folder is refused.
- Linux (`minigw serve`), Claude CLI with Allow local tools: the agent's shell could not be stopped from rewriting a `.git` that is a file (a linked worktree or submodule) to point git at a folder of its own, or from planting `.git/rebase-merge`, `shallow`, `grafts`, `remotes`, `branches`, `objects/info/alternates` or `refs/replace`, which the owner's git then runs or follows. They are closed now, the same list as on the Mac, except that `shallow` is closed only where it exists and the other new entries only where their folder exists (Linux binds an empty file over a missing denied path, which broke `git gc` and the first commit of a new repository).
- A forged `X-Minigw-Project` header cannot choose a project; a request head that cannot be rewritten safely closes the connection.
- DAV and sync check link chains first and refuse remote targets, other drives and automount paths.
- Direct-mode git watch: reads the repository without replacements, checks every ref and detached HEAD, diffs merges against each parent, reports new refs and moved tags, and says INCOMPLETE when a limit is reached or git fails. The share drains before the end report and the index restore.
- Deny rules for `.git` and `.claude` also match any letter case (NTFS, APFS).
- Mac: time limits for hung `claude` checks run on their own thread; the restricted profile cannot signal processes outside it.
- A refreshed login is carried back from a run's home only from a safe, regular file.

### Fixed
- Windows (`minigw serve`), Claude CLI: the rules for the git entries of each folder are four per folder (the file tools stay out of `.git` whole) instead of one per entry, tool and letter case, and a run whose command line would pass the Windows limit is refused with a message that names the limit (before: "The filename or extension is too long").
- Mac, Claude CLI in direct mode: the agent can now really commit in the direct-mode folder of a remote client (a real `.git` directory; the client's guard and the git watch apply there). `git` failed on the unreadable `~/.gitconfig`, and the sandbox refused the folder's own `.git` (`index.lock`). Git runs without user and system configuration there (`.git/config` of the folder still applies; a folder without a git identity commits as your computer's user name and host name). The gateway Mac's own folders (local session folder, allowed folders, a linked worktree) keep their whole `.git` closed for the agent's shell. Claude Code's sandbox closes the whole `.git` to the agent's shell unless the empty folders `.git/remotes` and `.git/branches` exist, so the client's guard now lets those two EMPTY folders be created (nothing inside them, no rename, no delete). An AppleDouble `._` file inside `.git` (PUT, DELETE, LOCK, MOVE) is no longer printed as a refused agent write.
- Mac: Claude CLI in direct or sync mode on a remote client's mount or a sync copy could read or edit nothing (0.5.28 regression, already fixed in 0.5.29); the deny rules for the gateway's own folders now leave the run's own folders open.
- Mac: removing a synced copy could answer 500 although it was removed, and a big removal no longer blocks other share and sync operations.
- A link target that passes an autofs mount is refused; a `..` after a link applies to the link's target (WebDAV MOVE guard works again).
- Mac: Settings marks refused allowed folders; a refused folder share prints the gateway's reason; a message said "router" instead of "gateway".
- A folder given with `minigw serve --folder` is saved with its identity record. An elevated Windows `minigw` that cannot set the token owner refuses most commands.
- `minigw -p` with a model of another provider puts the whole answer in the final message.
- A stream that breaks after HTTP 200 is recorded as an error, not as a success. Token counts that cannot be read stay unknown, not 0.

### Known limits
- Database downgrade: this version's database schema is 8. An older program refuses it (projects answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models keep working). The first start that migrates an existing database first writes a snapshot `core-<old schema>-<time>.db` into the core folder (`<config>/core`; on the Mac `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`). The way back: stop the gateway (on the Mac quit the app), run `minigw gw core restore <path to that core-<old schema>-<time>.db>`, start the older version. What was written to the core after the migration is lost.
- Coming from 0.5.29: 0.5.29 had no such database, so the first start creates a new one and writes no snapshot. There is nothing to restore; going back to 0.5.29 means installing it again, and the new core folder stays unused. Only users who ran a beta first have snapshots.
- Direct-mode git watch reads what git reports; two packs with the same object id and different content, a forged commit-graph, and a nested bare repository are not detected.
- Allowed folders without a volume UUID use the filesystem type, mount source and inode as their identity.
- Mac: the helper `minigw-core` serves one request at a time. Settings has only a Projects row, no project pages; restore needs the app quit. The Claude CLI agent reads notes only with "Allow local tools", Codex only with its network switch on.
- Only the owner manages members and grants. A role change cannot take back what a model has already read; a conversation that holds notes is not resumed after revocation.

### Known problems
- Grok is switched off on the Mac gateway in this version: its sandbox is being reworked. It worked in 0.5.29. If you need Grok on a Mac gateway, stay on 0.5.29 for now. Grok through `minigw serve` on Windows and Linux works.
- A Grok run that may not write (read-only, `--minigw-ro`, worktree) cannot run commands: a headless Grok cannot ask for approval.
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment variables.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices. The subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot) show no money, and their token counts are what the agent's program reports.
- Usage days are UTC days.
- The Usage views show data only from the first start of 0.6.x or newer. Quota history has gaps by design (only probes that ran are stored). Failed minutes exist only for requests made with 0.6.7 or newer.

## 0.6.10

### Changed
- Changed (Mac app, security review): Grok is now refused on every run on the Mac gateway, not only while its home lies inside a hidden folder (before, a Scratch folder that fell back to the system temp folder would have let Grok run with the shared home that two reviews rejected). The refusal comes first, before anything is synced or started, as 400 `invalid_request_error` with `x-should-retry: false` and the same message (a 502/503 would get "try again" from the client); Grok is no longer listed among the Mac gateway's models while it is switched off. Grok through `minigw serve` is not affected.

### Fixed
- Fixed (Mac app, security review): removing a big sync copy no longer blocks the shares actor. The copy is renamed inside the actor and the delete runs off it, so other share, sync and agent operations go on meanwhile (Go already deleted outside its lock). A removal still survives the leftover cleanup deleting the renamed folder, and leftovers of a crashed removal are still neither listed nor counted.

### Security
- Security (Mac app, security review): the restricted Claude profile now has `(deny signal (target others))`: a run can still signal its own processes (Claude Code stops its tools that way) but no process outside the profile.

### Known problems
- Grok is switched off on the Mac gateway until its sandbox is reworked (#126); it works through `minigw serve` on Windows and Linux.
- Agents started by the gateway inherit the gateway's environment.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices.
- Usage days are UTC days.

## 0.6.9

### New
- New (both gateways): quota history. Every real quota probe (Claude when its probe is on, Codex after a run, OpenRouter credit, LiteLLM budget), also a failed one, is stored in the core for 400 days; `minigw gw quota --history [--days N] [--provider P] [--json]` (and `GET /quota/history`, owner only) lists them without running a probe. `/quota` is unchanged.

### Known problems
- Grok on the Mac gateway is off until its sandbox is reworked (0.6.8). Grok through `minigw serve` (Windows, Linux) is not affected.
- Quota history holds only probes made with this version or newer, and only when a probe actually ran (a cached read or a disabled probe records nothing), so it has gaps by design.
- The Usage views (settings pages, charts) have not been checked on screen yet.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info (`/model/info`) has prices; otherwise their cost stays unknown.
- Usage days are UTC days, not local days.

### Known limits
- Downgrade to 0.6.8 or older: the first start of this version migrates the core database to schema 8 and first writes a snapshot `core-7-<time>.db` into the core folder (`<config>/core`; on the Mac `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`). An older program refuses schema 8 (projects answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models keep working). The way back: stop the gateway (on the Mac quit the app), run `minigw gw core restore <path to that core-7-<time>.db>`, start the older version; what was written to the core after the migration is lost.

## 0.6.8

### Fixed
- Fixed (both gateways, #121): no second agent run after a failed stream. A failure that happens after the stream has started (the 200 head goes out after 30 seconds) is remembered for 60 seconds, so the non-streaming repeat that Claude Code sends after a stream error gets the same error with `x-should-retry: false` at once instead of starting the agent again (before, only usage limits and changes that could not be copied back were remembered, so a failed 25 minute run ran twice). The memory is keyed by model, device, session, folder and a fingerprint of the request without `stream`, so another request of the same session never gets that failure; any success (also of the Claude CLI) removes it; it is consumed once and holds at most 128 entries.
- Fixed (both gateways, #121): a Grok run that stops because a command needs approval, which a headless run cannot give, ends with a clear 400 ("grok needed approval to run a command, which a headless run cannot give …") instead of a 502 that Claude Code shows as "try again". The evidence is the same on both gateways: exit status 0, not cancelled by the client, no error event, an empty answer, a cancelled `end` event and the refusal text in the last 4000 bytes of stderr. A Grok run that may not write is also told that shell commands cannot be approved and to use its file tools.

### Changed
- Changed (Mac app, #121): Grok is refused with a clear message on the Mac gateway: 503 with `x-should-retry: false`, "Grok is switched off on the Mac gateway in this version: its sandbox is being reworked (Grok cannot read its own folder under the current rules). Use another agent, or Grok through minigw serve on Windows or Linux." Since the Scratch folder was added to Grok's deny list, the Grok home lies inside a denied folder and every Grok request ended in "Failed to load config: Operation not permitted" (502). The model list still shows the Grok models (it only checks that Grok is enabled and installed). The refusal is decided from the same lists the profile is built from, before any folder is synced, and lifts by itself when the home is reachable.

### Known problems
- Grok on the Mac gateway is off until its sandbox is reworked (see Changed). Grok through `minigw serve` (Windows, Linux) is not affected.
- A Grok run that may not write (read-only, `--minigw-ro`, worktree) cannot run commands, on `minigw serve` either: a headless Grok cannot ask for approval, and approving automatically would let a command use the network and read the whole disk.
- A long Grok run (#121: 8 minutes and more on 0.5.29) was reported to end in 502 and, on 0.5.29, to be retried ten times by Claude Code. Not reproduced: the Grok CLI itself finished a silent 11 minute command and the gateway pings every 10 seconds. The ten retries were the 0.5.29 answer, a 502 without `x-should-retry: false`; since 0.6.x an agent failure carries it. The cause of the 502 at 8 minutes is open.

## 0.6.7

### New
- New (both gateways): failed agent time. Every request that reached its model now records how long it ran (from dispatch, not from admission or the queue) and, for an error, why (a closed list: `http`, `limit`, `timeout`, `unreachable`, `stream`, `exit`, `other`; never an error text) and the status the upstream answered. `minigw gw usage` adds a "failed min" column when any model has failed agent minutes and a total line such as "7 errors, 58.0 failed agent minutes"; `--sort failed` lists the models with the most failed time first; the CSV gains `errors`, `duration_ms`, `failed_duration_ms` and `unknown_duration_events` at the end. `/usage/events` rows gain `errorEvents`, `cancelledEvents`, `interruptedEvents`, `durationMs`, `failedDurationMs` and `unknownDurationEvents`; nothing existing changes.
- New (Mac app and browser Settings): the Usage view has a fourth measure, "Failed time" (minutes of requests that ended in an error per day and model).

### Fixed
- Fixed (both gateways): an upstream stream that breaks after HTTP 200 (connection lost, timeout) is recorded as an error, not as a success; an agent run that exits non-zero or is stopped by the 30 minute limit of `minigw serve` but still left text is recorded as an error; a client that goes away mid-stream is "cancelled"; a run cut off by a stop of the gateway is "interrupted" with an unknown duration, as before.

### Known limits
- Downgrade to 0.6.6 or older: the first start of this version migrates the core database to schema 7 and first writes a snapshot `core-6-<time>.db` into the core folder (`<config>/core`; on the Mac `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`). An older program refuses schema 7 (projects answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models keep working). The way back: stop the gateway (on the Mac quit the app), run `minigw gw core restore <path to that core-6-<time>.db>`, start the older version; what was written to the core after the migration is lost.
- Requests recorded before this version have an unknown duration (counted as "unknown", never as zero minutes). Failed minutes of runs cut off by a crash are unknown too.

### Known problems
- The Usage views (Mac Settings and the browser Settings) have passed automated tests of their numbers but have not been checked on screen.
- Failed agent minutes exist only for requests made with this version or newer; older days show the number of failed requests without their time.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices; the subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot) show no money.
- Usage days are UTC days.
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.

## 0.6.6

### Fixed
- Fixed (both gateways): a spending-limit error from OpenRouter is recognised by the provider's address (openrouter.ai), not by its name, so a renamed OpenRouter provider no longer makes the client retry.
- Changed (both gateways): usage reports show the provider in lowercase and the `provider` filter ignores case, so `OpenRouter` and `openrouter` are one provider on both gateways.
- Fixed (both gateways): a response whose token counts the new usage parser cannot read (deeply nested, a usage object over 64 KB, or usage in an unusual place) keeps the counts the older extraction finds instead of "unknown"; an aborted Claude CLI stream is "unknown" instead of "0 tokens".

### Known problems
- The two Usage views (Mac Settings and the browser Settings) have passed automated tests of their numbers but have not been checked on screen.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices; the subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot) show no money.
- Usage days are UTC days.
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.

## 0.6.5

### New
- New (Mac app): a "Usage" page in Settings shows requests, tokens and spend per day as a stacked chart by model, with the top models listed next to it. Choose 7, 30 or 90 days. Estimated amounts are marked with "~", and a note says how many requests have no known cost.
- New (`minigw serve`): the Settings page in the browser has the same "Usage" view.

### Known problems
- The two new Usage views have passed automated tests of their numbers but nobody has looked at them on screen yet; layout, colours and dark mode may need work.
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info has prices; the subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot) show no money.
- Usage days are UTC days.
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.

## 0.6.4

### New
- New (both gateways): cost estimates for requests whose provider reports no cost. `minigw gw usage` shows an estimate with `~` (for example `~0.42`), a reported charge plain, a mix as `0.96 + ~0.42`, and still counts the unknown ones; the CSV has separate `reported_cost_usd` and `estimated_cost_usd` columns, and `/usage/events` rows gain `costUsd`, `estimatedCostEvents`, `subscriptionEvents` and `unknownBillingEvents`. The price comes from the provider's own list (OpenRouter models list, LiteLLM `/model/info`), is frozen when the request starts and never changes later. Requests with cached tokens, images, audio or tools, and all subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot CLI), get no estimate.

### Known problems
- LiteLLM models show a cost only when the proxy's model info (`/model/info`) has prices; otherwise their cost stays unknown.

### Known limits
- Downgrade to 0.6.3 or older: the first start of this version migrates the core database to schema 6 and first writes a snapshot `core-5-<time>.db` into the core folder (`<config>/core`; on the Mac `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`). An older program refuses schema 6 (projects answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models keep working). The way back: stop the gateway (on the Mac quit the app), run `minigw gw core restore <path to that core-5-<time>.db>`, start the older version; what was written to the core after the migration is lost.

## 0.6.3

### New
- New (both gateways): `minigw gw usage` now shows where requests, tokens and money go: totals per provider and model (sorted by reported cost), a per-day table and a total line, from the usage events, for the last 7 days or `--days N`. Options `--provider`, `--model`, `--json` (the API answer unchanged) and `--csv` (one row per day, provider and model). The older per-folder and per-device counters, with their `--device` and `--folder` filters, are now `minigw gw usage --legacy`.

### Changed
- Changed (both clients): `minigw status` now shows the gateway's version next to the minigw version (a `Version:` line), as `minigw plugin status` already did (#118).

### Known problems
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.
- Cost is shown only when the provider reports it (OpenRouter does); LiteLLM models and the subscription agents (Claude, Codex, Grok, Copilot) show "-". Estimates from price lists are not in this version.
- Token counts of the subscription agents are what the agent's program reports and can be far below the real use.

## 0.6.2

### Changed
- Changed (both gateways): an allowed folder whose real path cannot be resolved (access denied, an invalid name) is now logged once with the reason and listed as refused, instead of being left out silently (#123).

### Security
- Fixed (Mac app): the time limits that stop a hung `claude` (the sandbox self-check, the version check and the tools list) ran on a shared timer that could fire late on a busy gateway, so a check that ran over its limit could count as passed and a hung process was not stopped in time. They now run on their own thread; a check that runs over its limit is "inconclusive", and the "off" setting of the self-check expires.
- Changed (Mac app, restricted Claude runs): a run whose session or allowed folder is `/` is refused, the Copilot cache is closed to every confined run, and Claude Code's project folder may be created by the run but nothing outside the session's own files in it is writable.

### Known problems
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.
- The exact answer of OpenRouter for a reached key limit is assumed; if it differs, the message is "access denied" with the provider's text instead of "spending limit reached".

## 0.6.1

### New
- New (both gateways): usage statistics keep the provider and, when the provider reports it, the cost of each request (`/usage/events` rows: `provider`, `reportedCostUsd`, `eventsWithoutCost`); an OpenRouter spending or key limit is now reported as "OpenRouter: spending limit reached" instead of "check the API key".
- New (`minigw serve`): the Settings page in the browser marks allowed folders that are refused, with the reason, as the Mac Settings do.

### Changed
- Changed (both gateways): a link that leaves a shared folder is listed over WebDAV as a refused entry (403 with the reason) instead of an empty file.
- Changed: `minigw share remove` with a name that is not shared says so and exits with an error; before it answered "no longer shared".
- Changed (both gateways): the answer when a device has reached its number of synced folders names the command to remove one (`minigw gw sync remove`).
- Changed (both gateways): a refused allowed folder is logged once per folder and reason, with the folder name and reason quoted; at most 8 reasons per folder are remembered, then one line says further refusals are not logged.

### Fixed
- Fixed (Mac app): removing a synced copy (`minigw gw sync remove`) could answer with error 500 although the copy was removed, when the cleanup of leftovers ran at the same moment.
- Fixed (Mac app): a message of the admin connection said "router" instead of "gateway".

### Known limits
- Downgrade to 0.6.0: the first start of this version migrates the core database to schema 5 and first writes a snapshot `core-4-<time>.db` into the core folder (`<config>/core`; on the Mac `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`). A 0.6.0 program refuses schema 5 (projects answer 503 `core_unavailable`; models keep working). The way back: stop the gateway (on the Mac quit the app), run `minigw gw core restore <path to that core-4-<time>.db>`, start 0.6.0; what was written to the core after the migration is lost.

### Known problems
- The self-check of the Mac sandbox layer for restricted Claude runs can report "pass" when the check run exceeds its time limit (it should report "inconclusive"). A fix is in work.
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.
- The exact answer of OpenRouter for a reached key limit is assumed; if it differs, the message is "access denied" with the provider's text instead of "spending limit reached".

## 0.6.0

Mini Gateway 0.6.0: users, projects and notes on the gateway, usage events and quota. Read **Changed** and **Known limits** before you update a gateway that other people use.

### New
- New (both gateways): `minigw gw sync list --device ID` and `minigw gw sync remove <copy id> --device ID` reclaim synced-folder slots without waiting 14 days; stop sessions and agents before removal (`local` selects local Mac copies); listing works during active sessions.
- New (Mac gateway): the Claude CLI without "Allow local tools" runs inside a Seatbelt profile as a second, kernel-enforced layer under the Claude Code rules (which are unchanged). The kernel denies every file read and write except the system libraries, the `claude` program, the session folder, the allowed folders (write only with edit tools and not read-only), claude's own state in `~/.claude` (an allow list: this session's transcript, `dev-mods`, the model catalog cache; not shell snapshots, credentials, history or other projects), no writes to `.git` (and `.claude` with project/local setting sources) in writable folders, the login keychain files and a private temp folder per run; network only to port 443 and DNS. A self-check after each new `claude`/macOS/profile (one tiny real run plus plain `cat`/`touch` under the profile) turns the layer off if `claude` cannot run under it, and then says so: a log WARNING, `minigw gw status` and the Claude CLI pane of Settings ("Claude CLI without local tools runs without the system sandbox: …"), never silently. Not covered: WebFetch to any host on port 443, and the login keychain files stay readable and writable by the confined process (encrypted), and the confined claude can read the account name and project list in `~/.claude.json`. Modes with "Allow local tools" and worktree are not wrapped (Claude Code's sandbox does not nest). See `docs/gateway.md`.
- New (both clients, #119): `--minigw-tools=none|read` limits the tools of a provider model (LiteLLM, OpenRouter) that runs in the local Claude Code: `none` gives it no tools, `read` gives it Read, Grep and Glob only. It also works inside Claude Code (it only removes tools). `--minigw-ro` (or a folder saved read-only) now means `read` for these models: the model gets Read, Grep and Glob only. Such a run loads no user, project or local Claude settings or hooks (their hooks would run outside the tool list) and refuses options that could widen the set. A refused `@` in a prompt now says to pass the text on stdin. Docs: commands.md, usage.md.
- New (both gateways): the project database ("core"). The gateway keeps users, device bindings, projects, members, grants, notes, usage events and an audit trail in a SQLite database (`minigw serve`: the `core` folder next to `serve.json`; Mac app: `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/core`, run by the new helper `minigw-core` inside the app). If the database cannot be opened the gateway still starts: project routes answer 503 `core_unavailable`, model requests work as before. The database survives an upgrade and an uninstall. Docs: gateway.md, admin-api.md.
- New (both gateways): the owner's commands `minigw gw user|bind|project|member|grant|core backup|core restore|core audit|usage-events|quota`, with the same arguments, output and exit codes on the Mac app and on `minigw serve` (a shared fixture, `client/testdata/admin-commands.json`, is asserted by both). Roles: reader, developer, admin; a grant gives another project read access to the `structure` or `data` notes. Docs: commands.md, usage.md ("Projects in 10 minutes").
- New (both clients): `minigw project list|link|unlink|show` and `minigw note list|read|push|edit|rm`. A folder is linked to a project you are a direct member of; the link is stored on your computer, per gateway (`projects.json`), and `.minigw-project` in a repository only suggests it. `note push` is safe to repeat. Inside an agent run only `note list` and `note read` work. Docs: usage.md.
- New (both gateways): agent runs in a linked folder carry the project. The gateway admits it only for a direct role (otherwise 403 `no_project_access`, never a run without the project), the agent can read the project's notes with `minigw note list|read` (never `private` notes, nothing of your other projects), reads of a running agent fail after a revocation (a removed device, a changed binding, a lowered role), and conversations and the agent homes of Codex, Grok and Copilot are kept per user, project and epoch. Requests without a project are unchanged. Docs: how-it-works.md, gateway.md.
- New (both gateways): usage events. Every model invocation is an event (user, device, project, provider, model, outcome, tokens and where the tokens come from). `minigw usage` shows a member's own events (a project admin sees the project's), `minigw gw usage-events` shows the owner everything, with the gaps where events were lost. The 0.5.x `minigw gw usage` counters are unchanged. Docs: usage.md, gateway.md.
- New (both gateways): `minigw gw quota` shows the subscription quota (Codex windows from the run's session log) and provider credit (OpenRouter credit, LiteLLM spend) with source, time and staleness. The Claude quota is off until you turn it on (`minigw gw quota --claude-probe=on`, on the Mac also Settings → Claude CLI → Show the subscription quota): it runs `claude -p /usage` with no tools in an empty private folder, at most every 10 minutes. Docs: gateway.md.
- New (both gateways): `minigw gw status` prints `projects: on` or `projects: off (reason)`, the admin `status` has a `core` field, and the Mac Settings → General has a Projects row (helper not running, database cannot be opened, a hard link to a core file).
- New (both clients): `minigw project list` against a gateway older than 0.6.0 adds a hint with the gateway's version instead of only "not found or not visible".
- New (release files): `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` lists the Go modules built into minigw, minigw-tui and minigw-core with their licences. It is in the Mac app, in `minigw.zip`, in the MSI install folder and a release file next to the Linux binaries; the release build fails when a linked module has no entry.
- New (plugin): the skills `/minigw:project`, `/minigw:notes` and `/minigw:usage` for the client side, and `/minigw:gw` knows the owner commands, quota and `--accept-agents-read-notes`. They need minigw 0.6.0 and say so.
- New (Mac gateway, direct mode, all clients): an agent in direct mode can commit in your repository (#112). `git add`, `git commit`, `git branch`, `git checkout`, `git stash`, `git tag` and `git merge` of a local branch work; the client (Go and Swift, one shared table) and the gateway's sandboxes (Claude CLI, Codex) let the agent write git's own data and keep closed what git runs or follows: `.git/config`, `hooks`, `info`, `commondir`, `shallow`, `grafts`, `worktrees`, `objects/info/alternates`, and `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply`, `sequencer` (so `git rebase` and multi-commit cherry-pick by an agent fail; they would let it leave a todo with an `exec` line for you to continue). Inside a submodule or a nested clone git writes are refused in direct mode (see Changed; use worktree mode). Read-only, sync and worktree mode are unchanged; a git repository still gets worktree by default. The agent's system note now says which git commands work in the run (direct, sync, read-only, no shell), and a refused rebase or gc write names the alternative. Docs: how-it-works.md (Protected paths), gateway.md.

### Changed

- Changed (Mac app, Grok): Grok runs in a home of its own that the gateway keeps under Scratch (it holds the sandbox profile that keeps Grok out of the core folder). Your `~/.grok/config.toml` defaults are not applied to Grok runs on the Mac any more, and sessions from before in `~/.grok` are not resumed. The login stays where it is. Windows and Linux gateways keep linking `config.toml`.
- Changed (Mac app, Codex): Codex in write mode runs under a permission profile; besides the session and allowed folders only its private temporary folder (`/private/tmp/minigw-codex-<id>`, the run's `TMPDIR`) is writable. Other places in `/tmp` are not writable any more.
- Changed (Windows and Linux gateways, `minigw serve`, Claude CLI without "Allow local tools"; the Mac app has these since 0.5.28): the restricted mode drops the tools the path rules do not cover (Task, MCP tools and the like; `Bash` stays, its sandbox confines it), a permission mode of `auto` or `plan` is refused when saved and used as `dontAsk` (a real run read and wrote outside the folders with `auto`), `Write`, `MultiEdit` and `NotebookEdit` are scoped through the Edit rules (Claude Code ignores `Write(...)` path rules), nested `.git` folders and a submodule's git directory get the same denies as `.git` (`config`, `config.worktree`, `hooks`, `info`, `commondir`, `worktrees`), unless the setting sources are empty or only `user` the edit tools cannot write `.claude/**`, a plain `WebFetch`/`WebSearch` entry is the only form kept as written, and allowed folders with `( ) * ? [ ] { } \ !` or a space at an end of a name are refused when saved and skipped at run. The Codex tree profile on the Mac and in Go keeps `.git/modules` read-only except the submodule git directories that exist at the start of a run.
- Changed (Windows and Linux gateways, `minigw serve`): where an agent has no sandbox (Linux without `bwrap`; Windows without the Windows sandbox; Codex and Copilot on Windows) the core does not open until the owner accepts that those agents can read all project notes: `minigw gw claude --accept-agents-read-notes=true` and a restart. Until then project routes answer 503. The Mac has no such setting: its agents are sandboxed.
- Changed (Windows and Linux gateways): an agent that could read the core folder is refused while old project data is on disk and the core is not open (refused, failed, an earlier period); nothing is deleted, and no usage spool exists while the core is not open. On every gateway a hard link to a core file makes the core refuse to open and every agent launch refuse until the link is removed.
- Changed (both gateways): `minigw gw core backup [name]` and `POST /core/backup {"name"}` write only into `<core>/backups` (a name, not a path; restore accepts a name or, offline, a path). A backup there is deleted with the core folder: copy it to a place agents cannot read if you want to keep it.
- Changed (uninstall): the `core` folder is never removed by an upgrade or an uninstall. Only purge removes it (`minigw uninstall --purge`, Windows MSI `PURGE=1`, Linux `install.sh --uninstall --purge`, `brew uninstall --zap`), and it then deletes the projects, members, notes, usage events and audit trail for good. Take `minigw gw core backup` first.
- Changed (Mac app): requests that carry a project (`X-Minigw-Project`) and the project routes answer 503 `core_unavailable` while the helper `minigw-core` is not running (starting, restarting, version mismatch). Model requests without a project are not affected. Removing a device still takes effect at once.
- Changed (Mac app, usage events): events wait in the app's memory while the helper is down (4096 at most; a longer outage drops the oldest and records a gap). When the app quits they are written as one gap and reported at the next start. If the app crashes or is killed, the events still queued are lost and no gap is recorded.
- Changed (size): the Go `minigw` for Windows and Linux contains the SQLite driver and is about 4.5 MB larger (Windows amd64 10.5 to 14.8 MB, Linux amd64 9.8 to 14.3 MB). The Mac app gains the helper `minigw-core` (8.5 MB).
