- Changed (`minigw serve`): the Claude quota probe is off until `minigw gw quota --claude-probe=on`; the Windows sandbox gets loopback only when the agent may ask other models (a project run without it has no notes there); project runs of Codex, Grok and Copilot have their own agent home per user, project and epoch; restore refuses a backup with triggers, views or unknown tables.

- Changed (both clients and the Mac sandboxes, direct mode): git's own data is writable only under the share's own `.git`. A nested repository (`sub/.git/...`) and a submodule's git directory (`.git/modules/...`) are closed whole: `git add` or `git commit` inside a submodule or a nested clone does not work in direct mode (use worktree mode for it), because the git watch audits the share's own repository only and a commit there would carry a protected path past it. The git note the agent gets says so.
- Changed (Mac app; `minigw serve` on Linux and macOS): an allowed folder whose volume id was recorded is refused when its volume no longer reports one (for example a network disk that is mounted differently now). Settings marks it. Remove the folder and add it again. Folders recorded without a volume id keep the inode check.
- Changed (Mac app, restricted Claude CLI runs): they do not load auto-memory or your user hooks and plugins, and `~/.claude.json` is read-only for them. Runs work without these.
- Changed (direct mode, all clients): git writes to `refs/replace` are refused, and so are git writes inside a submodule or a nested clone (use worktree mode).

### Security

- Security (#98, #102, both clients): DAV and sync inspect link chains before resolving targets, refusing remote or other-drive targets on Windows and `/net` or `/Network` automount paths on Unix (case-insensitively on macOS); MOVE and COPY still work with a configured UNC share, including MOVE of links whose targets stay inside that share.

- Security (#106, all gateways): no agent can read the core folder, which holds the notes of all projects. Mac: Codex always runs under its permission profile (also in read-only, chat and plain write mode), Claude CLI, `minigw run` and Copilot hide it like Scratch, and Grok runs in its own sandbox profile (Grok's own `--deny` is not enforced). Linux: `bwrap` hides the config folder. Windows: the Windows sandbox denies it for Claude CLI and Grok. An allowed folder that contains the core folder or is inside it is refused.
- Security (#106, all gateways): the credential homes of the other agents (`~/.grok`, `~/.codex`, `~/.copilot` and the gateway's own `grok-home` and `copilot` folders under Scratch) are denied to every agent of another kind.
- Security (0.6.0, `minigw`, both clients): a forged project header cannot choose a project: a request that carries more than one `X-Minigw-Project` header is refused (400), minigw's proxy drops the header an agent's own settings set, and a request head that cannot be rewritten safely (a line starting with a space or tab, no end within 64 KiB) closes the connection.
- Security (Mac app, Windows, Linux): a hard link to a core file is the same file under another name and bypasses every deny by path; the core refuses to open and every agent launch (also `minigw run` on the Mac) refuses while one exists.
- Security (Mac, Linux): a refreshed login (`auth.json`: on the Mac Codex runs with a project, on Linux Codex and Grok) is carried back from a run's home only when it is a regular file of the user with no other name, opened without following a link, at most 256 KiB, written during this run and a JSON object. On Windows nothing is carried back.
- Security (Windows gateway): a junction from an allowed folder to the core folder is refused, and the deny uses the final path.
- Security (Mac app, Grok): Grok's kernel profile now also denies the Scratch folder with the sync copies, worktrees and mirrors of other clients (and its `.old-` neighbours), with the way down to the session's own folder left open. Measured with Grok 1.0.77: a literal path in the list is cheap (1500 start), a glob is not (40 start, 43 do not: macOS compiles them into regexes), so the list holds at most 34 globs and the class prefix on the levels down to the session gives way first (12 down to 3 characters); a list that cannot fit refuses the run. Checked with a real Grok for a sync copy, a worktree clone, an app-folder mount and a folder outside the gateway's own.
- Security (both clients, direct mode): the git watch reads the repository without replacements (`--no-replace-objects`), checks the history from every ref and from HEAD itself (a commit on a detached HEAD no longer escapes), diffs merge commits against each parent (the paths a merge brings in are reported) and reports new or changed refs in every namespace; a `refs/replace/` entry gets its own warning. The agent can no longer write `refs/replace` (or its log) through the share.
- Security (both clients, direct mode): when the 200-commit or the 1 MiB limit of the git watch is reached, or a git command fails, the end report says the check is INCOMPLETE instead of staying silent, and the stored start state stays for the next start. The end report no longer runs while writes are still accepted: the share stops taking writes, waits for the running requests (up to 30 s) and only then checks and restores the index.
- Security (both clients, direct mode): the git watch no longer restores the index when the share did not drain in time (it reports the staged paths and keeps the baseline), says so when the repository cannot be read at the end, keeps an incompletely compared baseline at the next start (a week at most) and reports an existing tag that now points elsewhere.
- Security (both clients, direct mode): the Go watch now runs git with the pins the Swift watch had (`core.commitGraph=false`, `core.multiPackIndex=false`, no user git config), shared by a fixture; `objects/info/commit-graph*` stays writable because `git gc` fails without it. The watch reads paths with `-z`, so a name with a quote or a newline is matched, warns about a new ref whose name has a component that starts with `-` (it can arrive through `packed-refs`), says so when the start state cannot be stored, recovers a killed client's report at every share start (not only a direct-mode one) and does not take a reused process id for the running client.
- Security (Windows and Linux gateways, Claude CLI rules): the deny rules for `.git`, `.claude` and git's closed names also get a form that matches any case (`[gG][iI][tT]`), because NTFS and the default APFS ignore case (`sub/.GIT/config`). The client's own guard already compared names without case.
- Security (Mac app, Grok): the class prefix of the deny list is no shorter than 6 characters (a session that needs less is refused), the chosen prefix is logged, a hidden root behind a link is listed as what it resolves to too, and a root with a glob character is refused.

### Fixed

- Fixed (Go and Mac clients/gateways): a link in a shared folder is refused when its target passes through an autofs mount (not only `/net` and `/Network`); the check stops at the mount and never looks inside it. A `..` after a link now applies to the link's target, which keeps the WebDAV MOVE guard against moving a folder into its own subfolder effective (a regression against 0.5.29).
- Fixed (Mac client): a refused folder share prints the gateway’s reason, including the synced-folder limit.
- Fixed (#100, Mac): Settings marks refused allowed folders with a warning and keeps them removable; the admin API returns them in `claude.refused`, as on the Go gateway.
- Fixed (#96, Windows and Linux gateways): a folder supplied with the deprecated `minigw serve --folder` option is saved with its identity record, so replacing it is refused on later starts, including when the option names an alias of an already configured folder.
- Fixed (#103, Mac app and Go client/gateway): allowed-folder records now keep a fallback volume identity from the filesystem type and mount source when no volume id is available (preserving existing nonzero Linux IDs), and on Linux/macOS losing a recorded volume id no longer weakens the check to the inode alone.
- Fixed (Mac app, 0.6.0 deny rules; the 0.5.28 regression is fixed in 0.5.29): the deny rules of the gateway's own folders (the app folder with the core, the credential homes) are not applied where the folder of a run (a remote client's mount under `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/mounts`, a sync copy) lives inside them, in the restricted mode, the sandboxed mode and Worktree mode; a deny wins over an allow. The rest of the app folder and the other clients' mounts stay refused (only the run's own folders are allowed). The refusal of overlapping allowed folders is the one of 0.5.29.
- Fixed (Windows client, #99): an elevated `minigw` that cannot set the token owner now refuses every command except exact help/version and `gw`/`router` help forms and `share`/`share list`. Even read-only gateway requests can save a device credential or migrate an encrypted profile. The refusal says to run from a normal command prompt, not elevated, so local state belongs to the user.

- Fixed (#78, both clients, plugin skills): `minigw -p` with a model of another provider now tells the model in its system prompt to put the whole answer in the final message (the ask, codex, review and copilot skills say it in their prompts too), so a weak model no longer refers to text it wrote in an earlier message that `-p` does not return.

### Known limits

- Git watch (direct mode): it reads what git reports. Two packs that hold the same object id with different content are not detected (only an fsck-style check would); a forged `objects/info/commit-graph` or multi-pack-index is ignored by the watch but the owner's own git reads it. A nested bare repository (no `.git` name) cannot be caught by a name rule.
- Allowed folders without a volume UUID use filesystem type, mount source and inode as their fallback identity. Another server export mounted with the same filesystem type and source string at the same path passes if the folder has the same inode number.

- Mac app: the helper `minigw-core` serves the app's requests one at a time, so a slow call delays the next one.
- Mac app: there are no project pages in Settings, only the Projects row; users, projects, members and grants are managed with `minigw gw …`. On the Mac, restore needs the app quit.
- Mac app: the Claude CLI agent reads notes only with "Allow local tools"; Codex only with its network switch on.
- Teams and delegated administration (project admins managing members and grants) are not in 0.6.0: only the owner manages members and grants. A role change cannot take back what a model has already read.
- Revocation stops new reads; a conversation that already holds notes is not resumed after the epoch changed.

### Known problems

- The Settings page of `minigw serve` (in the browser) does not mark refused allowed folders. The Mac Settings and the admin API do.
- The self-check of the Mac sandbox layer for restricted Claude runs can report "pass" when the check run exceeds its time limit (it should report "inconclusive"). A fix is in work.
- Grok agent runs that last longer than a few minutes can fail with 502.

## 0.5.29

- Fixed (Mac gateway, regression in 0.5.28): with "Allow local tools" off (the default) the Claude CLI models could not read or edit any file of a folder that was shared with the gateway in direct or sync mode whose mount or copy lives in the gateway's own folders: the mount under `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway/mounts` and the copy under the Scratch folder. That is every folder of a remote computer, and sync mode also from the gateway Mac itself (its copy is under `Scratch/sync/local/…`). 0.5.28 denied the tools those gateway folders as hidden, and a deny wins over the allow for the session folder. Not affected: Worktree mode, "Allow local tools" on, and a folder of the same Mac used directly. Now a gateway folder is denied to the tools only where no folder of the run is inside it; where one is, the run's own folders are the only allowed paths, so the other clients' mounts and copies and the app's files stay refused.
- Changed (Mac gateway): an allowed folder (Settings, Claude CLI, Allowed folders) that contains, equals or is inside one of the gateway's own folders (the app folder `~/Library/Application Support/Mini Gateway`, the Scratch folder, Copilot's package cache) is refused when saved, and a stored one is skipped at run for every agent model (Claude CLI, Codex, Grok; logged once): the agents would otherwise reach the settings and every remote client's mount. New with this version: `~/Library/Application Support`, folders inside the app folder and folders inside old Scratch folders (`*.old-<date>`); `~` and `~/Library` were refused before.

## 0.5.28

- Security (#111): **the Claude CLI models without "Allow local tools" (the default: tools Read, Grep, Glob and whatever else is configured) could read any file the gateway user can read, and with Edit or Write configured write any file** (for example a LaunchAgent or `~/.zshenv`): the tools were allowed for any path, not only the session's folder. An approved device, or text in a repository that the agent read, could make the agent do it (verified with a real `claude -p`). Exposed in every release since v0.2 (Claude CLI models with tools); gateways on Windows and Linux (`minigw serve`) already scoped the tools to the folders and are not affected by the missing folder scope (a permission mode of `auto` or `plan` there is a separate matter for the next release). What changes when you update:
  - File tools (Read, Grep, Glob, LS, Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit) work only in the session's folder and the allowed folders (Settings, Claude CLI, Allowed folders); WebFetch and WebSearch are unchanged.
  - Without "Allow local tools" only those tools and the web tools may be configured: `Bash`, Task, MCP tools and the rest run without a sandbox and are refused when saved (Settings shows the reason in red and keeps the stored setting, the rest of the Settings window keeps saving and the sidebar says "The Claude CLI settings need attention."; the admin API answers 400). A request that finds a tool outside the list in a stored setting is answered with a 400 that says so; this includes names that older versions let through, such as a stored `Bash(git:*)` or a lower-case `read`. Read-only requests (`--minigw-ro`) are not refused: they use only Read, Grep, Glob, LS, WebSearch and WebFetch from the list, as before. **If your gateway lists `Bash` there, turn on "Allow local tools" (commands then run in a sandbox) or remove it.** Tool names typed with spaces (`Read, Grep, Glob`) are trimmed (they used to be allowed for any path).
  - Permission modes: while "Allow local tools" is off only `dontAsk` and `acceptEdits` are accepted (a real run with `auto` read and wrote outside the folders, `plan` read outside); a stored `auto` or `plan` is used as `dontAsk`, Settings saves it as `dontAsk` with the next change and its picker no longer offers them. With "Allow local tools" on nothing changes.
  - Allowed folders whose path has `( ) * ? [ ] { } , \ !` or a space at an end of a name cannot be used there: refused when saved, skipped at run; if the session folder itself is named that way the agent has no edit tools in it and says so.
  - Without "Allow local tools" the edit tools cannot write `.git` itself, its `config`, `config.worktree`, `hooks`, `info`, `commondir` and `worktrees`, and, in a submodule's git directory `.git/modules/<sub>/`, the same six (`config`, `config.worktree`, `hooks`, `info`, `commondir`, `worktrees`; objects, index, HEAD and refs stay writable, so a commit inside a submodule works), also in nested `.git` folders, in the session folder and the allowed folders; this holds for other letter cases (`.GIT`), folder names with spaces and non-ASCII names (real runs). Unless the setting sources are empty or only `user`, they also cannot write `.claude/**`. The app's own folder and Scratch cannot be read or edited. The submodule rule also applies to `minigw run`, to Copilot and, with "Allow local tools", to the sandboxed commands, whose other `.git` protection is unchanged. In Codex worktree runs all of `.git/modules` is read-only except the git directories of the submodules that exist at the start of the run; those are writable with the same six denied. Known limit, as in earlier versions: in `minigw run` and Copilot an agent that renames a folder above a submodule's git directory can still get around the rule in a repository nested inside the folder. With "Allow local tools", unless the setting sources are empty or only `user`, the agent can no longer edit a repository's own `.claude/` folder (skills, commands, settings) with the edit tools, and sandboxed commands cannot write it whenever any setting source is set (verified with a real run, also as `.CLAUDE`); a project that lets its agent maintain `.claude/` must leave the setting sources empty. Read-only requests (`--minigw-ro`) keep only Read, Grep, Glob, LS, WebSearch and WebFetch from the stored list, with path tools reduced to their bare names and scoped to the folders.
- Fix (#109, all gateways on Mac): the kill timers of agent and `minigw run` processes (git time limit, kill 2 s after an agent or command ends, 5 s after cancel, closing of the output stream) ran on the shared dispatch pool and could start ten or more seconds late when the Mac was busy, so a hung git ran past its limit and an agent's leftover processes lived on until then. They now run on their own threads.

## 0.5.27

- Security (#82, #107, Mac client): the Mac client now does what the Windows and Linux client did in 0.5.26: reads, writes, moves, copies and deletes of the shared folder through WebDAV and sync go through the opened folder (descriptor, `openat`/`renameat`/`unlinkat` without following links), so a parent folder swapped for a link during an operation cannot lead out of the share or into a protected path (`.git`, `.claude/settings.json`, ...). Links inside the share (relative or absolute target) work again for agents with a direct or read-only share: they are listed as the folder or file they point to (a file with its real size) and can be read and written; a link that leaves the share (also a dangling link or a link loop) is listed as an empty file and cannot be read or written; a request for the link itself is refused with 403 (reason "links that lead outside the shared folder are not followed"), which the agent does not see yet. 0.5.26 said the shared-folder change was in both clients; it was not in the Mac client. Moving or deleting a link acts on the link itself. Sync still does not copy links to the gateway (as on Windows and Linux), but copies agent changes back through links that stay inside the share.

## 0.5.26

- Security (allowed folders, all gateways): an allowed folder (Claude CLI **Folders**) is now recorded when you add it: its final path and its identity (volume and inode; on Windows volume serial and file ID). The gateway checks the record at start, on every agent run and right before the agent starts, and also that the folder belongs to the gateway user (on Windows the owner is checked only for a folder that was missing at start, see below). A folder that was replaced by another one (renamed away and a new folder, a disk, a link in its place) is no longer given to agents; the reason is logged once and shown in `agents/claude.refused` (`minigw serve`). If you trust the new folder, remove it from the list and add it again. An existing setting is recorded once at the first start of this version (one log line per folder: `recorded <folder> -> <real path>`); that step trusts what is there. A folder that did not exist when it was recorded takes its identity at its first accepted use. This detects a replaced folder; it does not stop a local process with the gateway user's rights from swapping a folder during the few moments of an agent start.
- Changed (Windows gateway `minigw serve`): the owner of an allowed folder is checked only for a folder that was missing when the gateway started (as before), not on every run: ownership there is not a reliable sign of who controls a folder (Administrators-owned folders, network drives, FAT volumes). The final path and the recorded identity are checked on every run. On Linux the owner is still checked on every run. A folder that is refused at start is listed in `agents/claude.refused` right after the start, as the log says.
- Security (allowed folders): a working folder that is itself an allowed folder is checked at launch like the others (Claude CLI, Codex, Grok). Siri attachments are read only from checked allowed folders. Agent arguments and sandboxes use the checked real path, not the name that was typed (Linux and Mac; on Windows the drive name stays, so mapped drives keep working, and its identity is checked right before launch).
- Security (Linux gateway): with a bubblewrap that supports `--bind-fd` (0.10.0, or 0.6.3), allowed folders are bound by open descriptor, so the folder cannot be swapped between the check and the sandbox; older bubblewrap uses the checked path plus the launch check.
- Security (#82, shared folder, Windows and Linux client): reads, writes, moves, copies and deletes of the shared folder through WebDAV and sync go through a pinned folder handle (`os.Root`), so a parent folder swapped for a link during an operation cannot lead out of the share or into a protected path (`.git`, `.claude/settings.json`, ...). Links and Windows junctions inside the share (also with an absolute target) keep working; links that leave it, for example to a network path (UNC) or another drive, are refused. The Mac client is not changed in this version: it still checks the path by name, and a link inside the shared folder appears to agents as a file that cannot be read when the folder is shared directly or read-only (a git worktree clone keeps relative links). The same change for the Mac client is planned for 0.5.27.
- Fixed (#88, Mac app): the allowed folders are checked on every run (recorded final path, owner, a real folder when it was missing), for Claude CLI, Codex, Grok, Copilot and native access.
- Fixed (#91, #93, `minigw serve`): the same checks on Windows and Linux; a mapped drive keeps its drive name (Claude Code blocks the network path behind it), and a drive that is not attached is kept and used again only when it is a real folder of the user.
- Fixed (#87, Windows gateway): `CODEX_HOME` is passed on to Codex.
- Fixed (#95, Windows gateway): when the gateway runs elevated, the token owner is set to the user.
- Fixed (#90, Windows gateway): a refused Scratch move leaves no half-created folder behind.
- Fixed (#89, both clients): `minigw share add` of another name of an already shared folder (same folder, for example a link) keeps one entry.
- Security (#92, Linux gateway): Copilot's keyring is only reached through a `unix:path=` session bus address.
- Fixed: an ordinary `minigw` command only warns when it cannot set the owner of the token file; `minigw serve` and the tray still stop.
- Fixed: both gateways answer a settings change that carries a `token` field with 400 `bad JSON body: unknown field "token"`.
- Fixed: changes of `serve.json` are locked between processes (`serve.json.lock`, waits up to 5 s), so a `minigw` command and a starting gateway cannot overwrite each other's change.
- Fixed (Mac app): an open Settings window with older Claude settings no longer undoes the identity a folder has just taken.

## 0.5.25

- Fixed (Windows gateway `minigw serve`, #83): an allowed folder below a junction in its path (for example `C:\work\x\proj` where `x` is a junction) was refused with "folder: The system cannot find the path specified."; it is accepted now, and still refused when the junction leads into the config folder.
- Fixed (#83, `minigw serve`, all systems): an allowed folder that does not exist (a drive that is not attached, for example `F:\proj` with no F:) stopped the gateway from starting ("cannot start: folder: … cannot find the path"); it now starts, keeps the folder and Claude CLI and Codex stay on. Agents get the folder only once it exists and passes the folder checks again (real path, not near the config folder); a folder that was missing must also be a real folder of this user with no link in its path, else it stays unused (logged once) until it is removed and added again in the allowed folders (a mapped NAS drive that was missing at start included). Saving the allowed folders still refuses a new folder that does not exist.
- Changed (Windows gateway): Scratch on a network share (a mapped drive to a NAS, a UNC path) is refused with "Scratch is not supported on a network share: the owner of folders there cannot be checked" instead of "is owned by another user" (the check is unchanged since 0.5.24).
- Fixed (settings page, #83): a left-out allowed folder shows its own reason ("cannot be checked" or "scratch folder or Copilot's cache"), and in Slovenian the reason after "Claude CLI in Codex sta izklopljena:" is in Slovenian.
- Changed (#81): Copilot CLI signs in only with your own `copilot login` on the gateway (as the gateway user); the token stays in the system keyring and only Copilot reads it. The Copilot token (`minigw gw copilot token`, the settings field) and the GitHub CLI sign-in (`gh auth token`) are gone; a stored Copilot token is no longer used. Mac app: Copilot reads its sign-in from your Keychain inside its sandbox. Linux `minigw serve`: Copilot needs a Secret Service keyring (GNOME Keyring, KWallet) and `xdg-dbus-proxy`; a headless Linux gateway, where `copilot login` stores the token in plain text, has no Copilot. Without a usable sign-in the settings, `minigw gw status`, `minigw gw copilot login` and the request say why. Windows: unchanged (not supported).
- Changed (Mac app): when Copilot hits a usage or rate limit, the gateway answers with the same fixed text as `minigw serve` ("copilot: usage or rate limit reached; details are in the gateway log, request <id>"); Copilot's error output stays in the gateway log and no longer reaches the client.
- Security (#81): Copilot no longer runs commands on any gateway ("Allow commands" is greyed out with the reason): a command could read Copilot's sign-in from the Keychain or keyring. A stored "Allow commands" is ignored.
- Security (#81): outside worktree mode Copilot sees no git repository (`GIT_DIR=/dev/null`), so a folder's `.git/config` and `.git/info/attributes` (fsmonitor, hooks, filters, external diff) cannot run programs in its sandbox; in every mode its git also gets the worktree git guard and no pager, editor, prompts or network protocols. Mac app and `minigw serve`.
- Security (#81): the old Copilot token is deleted from the gateway; revoke it on GitHub (Settings → Developer settings → Personal access tokens).
- Security (#83, Windows gateway `minigw serve`): the gateway's folder checks did not follow directory junctions (Go's `EvalSymlinks` stopped following them in Go 1.23) and, when a path could not be resolved, compared a partly resolved one. An allowed folder that is a junction to Scratch, to the minigw install folder (an agent could have put its own `minigw.exe` there, run without approval) or to the config folder was not detected. The checks now use the folder's real path (junctions, 8.3 short names, mapped and `subst` drives), resolve a folder that does not exist yet through its nearest existing parent, and refuse, or treat the folder as unsafe, when the path cannot be resolved. An allowed folder that cannot be resolved at start (access denied, a dangling link, a NAS that is offline when Windows does not report it as missing) is left out and turns Claude CLI and Codex off, with the resolve error in the reason; one on a drive that is not there at all stays. Grok's `--deny`, the Codex profile and the Windows sandbox's PATH folders use the real paths too, and the Mac app refuses an allowed folder it cannot resolve when it is saved and leaves it out of runs (agents stay on). The config folder is also denied by its real path in Claude CLI's tool rules and both sandboxes, so a link to it inside an allowed folder does not reach it; Codex on Windows runs outside a sandbox and is not limited by this (unchanged, see **Allow commands without a sandbox**). macOS and Linux: same checks, symlinks as before.
- Security (#82, interim): a gateway read (GET, PROPFIND) of the shared folder could race a move of a parent folder between minigw's path check and the read, and two minigw sessions on the same folder were not kept apart. Reads now share a lock on the folder; writes, copying the agent's changes back and worktree jobs take it exclusively, also between minigw processes (a lock file in minigw's private settings folder, released if minigw crashes). A WebDAV request that waits more than 5 seconds for another session gets 503 "the shared folder is busy"; sync and worktree jobs wait up to the gateway's limit. Also fixed: when minigw did not take the agent's work back (busy folder, no answer, an error), the request still answered as if it had; it now ends with an error that says why, and Claude Code does not run the agent again. Sync changes stay on the gateway and only the next agent request in the folder copies them back; a worktree branch stays on the gateway: the next agent request (same agent) in the session delivers it, otherwise it is kept on the gateway for 30 days. Both clients and both gateways. The full fix (every operation through the opened folder) is planned for 0.5.26.
- Fixed: Ctrl+Z in Claude Code run through minigw gives the terminal back to the shell (`fg` continues it), and when `claude` is ended by a signal, minigw ends with the same signal instead of exit code 128+n. Mac `minigw` and Linux `minigw`.
- Security: minigw recognises that it runs inside Claude Code also when `CLAUDECODE` is not set (for example `env -u CLAUDECODE minigw -p --continue …`): one of its parent processes is Claude Code. Then only the safe options are accepted, as with `CLAUDECODE`. When the parent processes cannot be read, or minigw was left without its parent (`cmd &`, `nohup`, a launchd job), this applies unless stdin is a terminal; such a script sets `MINIGW_ALLOW_UNSAFE_FLAGS=1`. A person at a terminal and your own scripts outside Claude Code keep every option; `MINIGW_ALLOW_UNSAFE_FLAGS=1` still turns the checks off. Both clients.
- Fixed: `minigw --continue` (`-c`) continues the last conversation minigw ran in this folder (as `--resume <id>`), as documented. Before, `claude` got `-c` and continued the newest Claude Code conversation in the folder, also one you had without minigw, and sent its transcript to the gateway. With no earlier minigw conversation in the folder, minigw starts a new one and says so. Only runs outside Claude Code that ended without an error are recorded, so the skills' model calls never replace your conversation. Combined short options with `-c` or `-r` (such as `-pc`) are refused; write them separately. In worktree mode, `--continue` now keeps the conversation's branch name, like `--resume <id>`. Both clients.
- Fixed: while an agent was starting, the gateway could, rarely, see its own Scratch folder as used by another gateway and refuse requests (also right after a settings change). Mac app and `minigw serve` on macOS and Linux.
- Security (#84): `minigw share add` compared folder names, so `/System/Volumes/Data/Users/<you>` (the same folder as your macOS home), `/System/Volumes/Data` (almost all of the disk) and folders that contain your home (`/Users`, `C:\Users`) could be shared. It now compares the folder itself (device and inode; on Windows volume and file index) and refuses your home folder, any folder that contains it, a drive root, and the root of the volume that holds your home folder or the system (also a Linux mount or Windows folder mount of it); other disks, devcontainer folders and Docker volumes stay shareable. A folder it cannot check is refused (on Linux also when `/proc/self/mountinfo` cannot be read). minigw checks again each time it shares: a current folder that is a link or junction to your home folder is not shared, even with `MINIGW_SHARE=1`; a saved folder that is now refused prints `minigw: not sharing … (…); remove it with: minigw share remove "…"` and is not shared, and `minigw share list` marks it "not shared". If the shared folder is replaced by another folder after minigw checked it (before sharing starts or while minigw runs), the folder is not shared and the gateway's requests to it (WebDAV, sync and worktree) are refused. Both clients.
- Fixed (Mac `minigw`): with several gateway profiles, a request could, rarely, get 502 "gateway … is not reachable" for up to 30 s.
- Fixed (`minigw` on all systems): `minigw claude -p` no longer hangs after Claude Code ends when an unread pipe holds its output; it ends at most about 2 s later and prints the branch line. With a paused terminal (Ctrl+S) the branch line appears when the terminal resumes. While nobody reads the output, Windows and Linux `minigw` hold Claude Code back like a plain pipe; the Mac `minigw` keeps up to 16 MB of the answer (and 1 MB of tool lines on stderr) waiting, then drops the waiting output, prints "minigw: output dropped: nobody read stdout (over 16 MB waiting)" and exits with 1. On the Mac, `minigw` no longer crashes when its error output is closed (`2>&-`).

## 0.5.24

- Security: minigw (Windows and Linux) is built with Go 1.27.4, which fixes an HTTP server crash in the Go standard library (GO-2026-6859).
- Security (Windows): a directory junction inside a shared folder let the gateway read or write outside it (affects 0.5.23). A junction (`mklink /J`, which any user or pnpm can make) is now followed like a symlink everywhere minigw checks a path: direct WebDAV, sync, protected paths (`hooks` → `.git\hooks`) and MOVE/COPY.
- Fixed (Windows): paths longer than 260 characters in a shared folder work without turning on `LongPathsEnabled`.
- Changed (Windows): a folder on a drive that does not report real paths (some network or virtual drives) is not shared: `minigw share add` refuses it and `minigw` prints "not sharing …: this drive does not report real paths (network or virtual drive); share a folder on a local disk". Share a folder on a local disk instead.
- Fixed: `minigw share add` refuses a link or junction to your home folder or to a drive root (and on Windows an 8.3 short name such as `C:\Users\ANA~1`), like the home folder and the drive root themselves. Both clients.
- Fixed: the gateway's writing WebDAV requests (PUT, MOVE, COPY, DELETE, MKCOL, PROPPATCH, LOCK) to a shared folder run one at a time; reads stay parallel. Both clients.
- Fixed: after a sync, "synced back from the gateway: N changed, M deleted" no longer counts skipped changes ("invalid path here", a protected or ignored path) or protected deletions ("not deleted"), and the line is not printed when nothing is left to count. Both clients.
- Security (Windows gateway, `minigw serve`): an agent folder (Claude CLI **Folders**) that is a junction to the gateway's settings folder is refused like a symlink, and the Foundry Local port check compares the server's real path, so a program on a mapped drive no longer counts as foreign.
- Fixed (Homebrew 7): every `brew` command warned "Calling `postflight` is deprecated! Use `postflight_steps` instead" (and the same for `uninstall_preflight`) for the `minigw` cask. The cask now uses `postflight_steps` and `uninstall_preflight_steps`. They run in Homebrew's sandbox, opened only for `~/.claude` and `~/.claude.json`, so the behaviour stays: install and upgrade run `minigw plugin install` ("added" or "already in", a `false` you set stays), `brew uninstall` runs `minigw plugin uninstall`, and `brew upgrade` and `brew reinstall` leave the plugin in place. The upgrade to 0.5.24 still runs the 0.5.23 cask's old uninstall step once, so it may print the warning one last time.
- Security (M1, 0.5.24 release gate): agent sandboxes hid only the current Scratch and the Mac's old default `Scratch`, so an allowed device could read through `minigw run cat`, Copilot and, in some layouts, Claude CLI or bwrap: the Mac's fallback Scratch in the default base (used while the chosen disk is unplugged; a regression from 0.5.23), this gateway's `<instance>.old-<date>` folders, other gateways' folders and the old `Mini Gateway Scratch` (Mac). The hidden list is now worked out at each run from the Scratch base and the default base (folders with an owner file, `<instance>.old-<YYYY-MM-DD>[-<HHMMSS>]` folders as Take over names them, the old names, the fallback; never the base itself), with resolved paths. `minigw serve` also leaves an allowed folder inside or around any of them out of the agent's folders (before, bwrap mounted it again and Claude's `allowRead` overrode `denyRead`), as the Mac already refused it. Mac app and `minigw serve` (Linux bwrap, Claude CLI's `denyRead`).
- Security (L1, 0.5.24 release gate): a Scratch base that is, or is inside, another gateway's Scratch folder (a folder with an owner file of another instance) is refused when you set or move Scratch: a later Take over of that folder would have deleted this gateway's folder in it. The check runs when Scratch is set or moved, not at start. Mac app and `minigw serve`.
- Changed (Windows): Copilot CLI is not supported on a Windows gateway (`minigw serve`) in this version. Copilot 1.0.10x cannot start in the Windows sandbox ("Failed to load package index … failed to resolve in-process runtime library: Access is denied (os error 5)": its runtime calls Rust `std::fs::canonicalize`, which the sandbox refuses), and outside it the gateway cannot reuse your `copilot login` without the rest of `~\.copilot` (hooks, MCP servers, plugins, trusted folders, which would run commands as you). The `claude-copilot` models are not listed, Settings → Copilot CLI and `minigw-tui` say "Copilot is not supported on a Windows gateway in this version" and cannot turn it on (`PATCH /agents/copilot {"enabled":true}` and requests: 400 with that text; `GET /agents/copilot` has `"unsupported": true`). Linux and the Mac app are unchanged.
- Fixed: a failed Codex or Grok run now answers with `x-should-retry: false`, so Claude Code does not start the failed agent again (`minigw serve` and the Mac app; Siri's busy queue stays retryable).
- Security (M1, all gateways): Copilot never gets an inherited `GITHUB_COPILOT_*` variable (`GITHUB_COPILOT_PROMPT_MODE_REPO_HOOKS`, `…_WORKSPACE_MCP`, `…_EXTENSIONS` would make it load the shared folder's hooks such as `.github/hooks/*.json`, its MCP servers or extensions, which run commands even with **Allow commands** off). With its fresh home and no trusted folders, Copilot loads none of them.
- Fixed (#74, Mac): native mode works in the gateway Mac's automatic home-folder SMB share (the share named after the gateway's user, for example `uros` = `/Users/uros`), which `sharing -l` does not list; before, it was refused as "not a folder of an SMB share". Only folders inside Allowed folders work. Native mode and `minigw run` never use the home folder itself, a hidden folder in it (`~/.ssh` …) or `~/Library` (keychains, tokens), in any share (also one above the home, such as `/Users`), also through a link (for example `~/Google Drive` → `~/Library/CloudStorage`) and when the whole home is allowed. When home sharing is off (`VirtualHomeShares` false), SMB sharing is off, its settings cannot be read, or an explicit share has the same name, the home share is not used. The gateway shares nothing itself.
- Fixed (Windows): cancel or timeout ends the whole agent tree and frees the run slot; local claude ends with minigw.
- Security (L1′, Windows): the shared `<drive>\minigw-scratch` parent gets a protected access list in which other users may only add their own folder (no delete, rename or permission changes; existing parents of yours are switched to it), and a parent that is a link or gives other users more is refused. Each gateway's folder in it must be a real folder of its own user (no junction), keeps its owner-only access list and is held open while the gateway runs, so nobody can rename or replace it. When it is refused, set your own Scratch (`"scratch"` in serve.json).
- Changed (#78): `minigw -p` without `--output-format` prints every text the model writes as it arrives (all turns, blank line between them), not only the final message; tool use shows as a short `minigw: · <tool>` line on stderr. An error result prints its text and exits non-zero. When the only text refers back ("above", "zgoraj") minigw warns that the answer may be incomplete. An explicit `--output-format` is passed through unchanged.
- Fixed (#78): `minigw -p` with a Claude CLI agent model (for example `claude-cli-haiku`) prints its text as the agent writes it, not all at once at the end, and its tools as `minigw: · <tool>` lines on stderr instead of `⏺ <tool>` in the answer (minigw now also passes `--include-partial-messages`). Codex, Grok, Copilot and Siri AI (Mac) still answer when they finish. A tool name other than letters, digits and `_.:@/-` (at most 80) shows as `tool`, and minigw strips the Unicode line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029) from gateway text. The `minigw: agent work is on branch …` lines come on stderr only after the whole answer is printed, as the skills expect. Both clients.
- Fixed (#78): with `minigw -p` an API error (for example a gateway refusal with HTTP 400) is printed once, as the `claude: …` line on stderr, no longer also on stdout. Both clients.
- Fixed (#78, A3): with `minigw -p` the line the model writes before a tool ends right away, so a reader that reads stdout by lines (a timestamp tool, a script) gets it before the `minigw: · <tool>` line instead of with the next text. Both clients.
- Changed: minigw starts claude with `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false` (unless you set it), so Claude Code no longer warns on every run that claude.ai connectors are disabled; with the gateway's token they could not load anyway. Claude Code's `[claude-code:unrecognized_model]` line for gateway model IDs stays: the only documented way to stop it (`modelOverrides`) would send an Anthropic model's requests to the gateway model.
- Fixed (#78): with a provider model (for example `claude-deepseek/…` on OpenRouter) Claude Code's helper calls go to the same model (`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL`, unless you set them) instead of `claude-haiku-…`.
- Fixed (#78): `count_tokens` for a provider without it (OpenRouter answers 404, shown as model `unknown` in the activity) now gets an estimate from the gateway, as for agent models.
- Fixed (#78): `minigw models` lists `claude-cli-fable`.
- Fixed (Mac): `minigw run` shows the command's output as it arrives (it was held until the end), and `swift build`/`swift test` under `minigw run` are much faster (a per-run xcrun cache instead of no cache; a no-op build went from about 43 s to 12 s).
- Fixed (#72, Mac): `swift test`, and `swift build`/`swift run` of a package with resources, work under `minigw run`, with the usual build system and products in the usual `.build`. Swift Build made its Info.plist temp folder in the macOS user temp folder (`/var/folders/…/T`), which ignores `TMPDIR` and is outside the run sandbox; each run now gets its own macOS temp and cache folder in its Scratch temp folder (`DIRHELPER_USER_DIR_SUFFIX` with links the gateway makes and removes). The sandbox is not widened. After a run the gateway removes the empty `NSIRD_*` and `TemporaryDirectory.*` folders that macOS created during that run (only new ones, never existing folders or other files).
- Changed (#77, Windows): groundwork only: `minigw serve` can find Copilot CLI installed with `npm install -g @github/copilot` (its own `copilot.exe`), but Copilot itself is not supported on a Windows gateway in this version (see above).
- Security (N4, all platforms): two gateways never share a Scratch folder. Each gateway install has a persistent instance ID (`serve.json` `"instance"`, the Mac app's settings), and its Scratch folder holds an owner file (`.minigw-owner`) with it; the first use writes it. A Scratch folder of another gateway is not used, also when that gateway is not running: agents are off (client requests get 503 naming the Scratch setting), nothing in it is cleaned up, and the log, Settings → General → Scratch folder (Mac app, settings page, `minigw gw settings`) and `minigw gw scratch` show "used by another gateway" or "stale owner". A running gateway holds a lock on its folder (Windows: the held handle; macOS and Linux: `flock` on `.minigw-lock`); a Scratch base on a network drive shared by more than one computer is not supported. Only a real folder of your user is taken over or adopted; an unreadable owner file counts as another gateway's. `minigw uninstall` also removes this gateway's default `~/.cache/minigw-scratch/<instance>` (Linux, macOS).
- New (N4): **Take over** in the Scratch row (Mac app, settings page, `minigw gw settings`; `minigw gw scratch take [NAME]` for headless Linux; `POST /general/scratch/take [{"name"}]`), only for a stale owner and disabled while the other gateway runs. The row also lists the other gateways' folders next to this gateway's folder in the base (`general.scratchOthers`, "used by another gateway" or "stale owner", without `*.old-*`), each stale one with its own Take over: the usual case after a reinstall, when the new install has a fresh `<base>/<new instance>` beside the old one. Takeover renames, never merges: the old folder becomes `<base>/<instance>` with this gateway's ID; this gateway's current folder is deleted when it holds no conversations (caches, `gitexec`, `tmp`, `mxc` do not count), else kept as `<instance>.old-<date>`. Logged.
- Changed (decision 3): the Scratch setting names a base folder and the gateway's Scratch is always its subfolder `<base>/<instance>`, on any drive and on all platforms: new installs use `<drive>\minigw-scratch\<instance>` (Windows), `~/.cache/minigw-scratch/<instance>` (Linux) and `~/Library/Caches/dev.trojar.minigateway/<instance>` (Mac app); **Move…**, **Default**, Choose… and `minigw gw scratch move DIR` move to `DIR/<instance>` with the owner file. Existing installs keep their folder (`<drive>\minigw-scratch\<user>`, the user cache folder, `Mini Gateway Scratch`): it is adopted as this gateway's folder, nothing moves.
- Fixed (Mac app): cancel or timeout of a Claude CLI, Codex, Grok or Copilot run ends the agent's whole process group and every process still holding its output pipe (also one in its own session), so a leftover child can no longer keep the run, its slot and its task alive; the same 2 s after a normal end. Processes of the gateway user that left the group without a pipe but work in the run's clone or `$MINIGW_DEPS` are stopped too. The agent's changes go back (sync, worktree branch) only after its processes are gone; if they cannot be stopped the run fails with "its processes did not stop after the run, so the gateway did not take its changes; details are in the gateway log, request …" (the same text as `minigw serve`) and the run's sync clone is not merged into the gateway's copy. In worktree mode the clone stays, and until the gateway has stopped those processes a new turn of that conversation gets 503 "the previous run's processes are still being stopped; try again shortly"; the next turn then starts from the branch with the earlier turns' work, without what the failed run wrote. When a run's processes are stopped, any of your own processes working in a gateway Scratch clone or run folder (for example a Terminal `cd`'d there) are stopped too. Claude Code's version and tools checks are handled the same way.
- Fixed (N2, Mac): `minigw run` removes its temporary folder and links and releases the folder's write lock only after the command's process group is stopped, also when a background process closed its output early; processes working in its temporary folder are stopped too. If they cannot be stopped, the client gets "minigw run: processes of this run did not stop; see the gateway log, request …" and a non-zero exit code, and the folder stays locked for agents and builds until the gateway has stopped them.
- Security (#73, Mac app and `minigw serve`): after each worktree run the gateway checks again that `$MINIGW_DEPS`, `$MINIGW_DEPS/tmp` and the project's shared `.cache` and `.maven` are real folders of its user, logs and makes a replaced one again (before, only at the next run's start). On the Mac, Copilot's sandbox profile resolves the clones folder once, no longer the agent-writable parent of `$MINIGW_DEPS/tmp`.
- Fixed (N3, macOS and Linux client): `minigw` no longer starts claude with Ctrl+C ignored; claude gets the default action again.

## 0.5.23

- Security (#68): `PATCH /general` and `POST /general/token` on `minigw serve` no longer return the connect line with the gateway token; only `GET /general` has it (`minigw gw token rotate` and the settings page read it from there).
- Changed (#68): when the Windows sandbox is on and the Scratch folder is inside your home folder (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\minigw\scratch`, the default of existing installs), the gateway log, Settings → General → About and `minigw gw settings` warn that agents in the sandbox can list the names in your home folder (not read the files): git in the box needs every folder above its working folder to be listable. Move Scratch outside your home with the settings page's Scratch **Move…** button or `minigw gw scratch move`.
- Changed (#68): new Windows installs (`minigw serve init`, the tray's first start) and the settings page's Scratch **Default** button put the Scratch folder outside your home: `minigw-scratch\<Windows user name>` on the first fixed drive that is not the system drive, else on the system drive (for example `D:\minigw-scratch\ana`), with an owner-only protected access list. Existing installs keep their Scratch folder (and the warning when it is inside your home); move it with the settings page's Scratch **Move…** button or `minigw gw scratch move`. Linux and macOS defaults are unchanged (the user cache folder).
- Security (#68): on Windows Codex runs only with "Allow commands without a sandbox", also when its own elevated sandbox is set up: HPX360 showed a Codex command in that sandbox reading `%APPDATA%\minigw\admin.secret` and `serve.json`. When it is set up, each run still gets `-c windows.sandbox="elevated"` as extra protection (`~\.ssh`, profile writes). When `config.toml` asks for `elevated` but it is not set up (no `.sandbox-secrets` in `CODEX_HOME`), the run is refused with "Codex's elevated Windows sandbox is not set up on the gateway: run codex sandbox setup --elevated once at the gateway PC (as administrator)" instead of an unattended UAC prompt (error 1223). `GET /agents/codex` `sandboxed` is false on Windows; the settings page (en, sl) and `minigw-tui` say so.
- Fixed (#68): Maven failed in the Windows sandbox ("Could not find or load main class org.codehaus.plexus.classworlds.launcher.Launcher"): only `apache-maven\bin` from `PATH` was readable, not its `boot` folder. For a `PATH` folder named `bin` the sandbox now also reads the tool's root (Maven, JDK; never a drive root or a folder in or above the home, Scratch or config folders), plus `GRADLE_HOME`. The sandbox may list names only above the folders it may write and its working folder, no longer above read-only program folders (for example the home folder above `~\.local\bin`).
- Fixed (#67, Mac app): in worktree mode with "May ask other models" off, Claude's sandbox could not run the gateway's git wrapper, so `git clone` in the clone failed with "could not lock config file … Operation not permitted" instead of the `$MINIGW_DEPS` hint, a clone of a remote URL into `$MINIGW_DEPS` failed with "git: 'remote-https' is not a git command", and CMake FetchContent could not run its `GIT_EXECUTABLE`. The wrapper is now readable in the sandbox for every worktree run. The agent's instructions give the CMake, SwiftPM and Xcode recipes with the literal folder path, because Claude Code refuses a command with `$MINIGW_DEPS` in it ("A variable in this command can't be checked before it runs"); the git refusal hint does the same (both gateways).
- New (#67): in worktree mode each conversation's agents get a dependencies folder `$MINIGW_DEPS` next to their clone under the gateway's Scratch folder. Repositories an agent creates (`git init`, `git clone`, CMake FetchContent with git sources, SwiftPM checkouts) work there, kept for the whole conversation and removed with the clone; anywhere else, also inside the clone, git refuses with a hint (`cmake -DFETCHCONTENT_BASE_DIR="$MINIGW_DEPS/cmake"`, `swift build --scratch-path "$MINIGW_DEPS/swiftpm"`), the same for every agent. This replaces the 0.5.22 limitation. Only agents that may write get it; a `git clone` of a remote URL with the agent's build network off is refused with the setting's name. Windows without the Windows sandbox (and Codex on Windows) cannot create repositories (the agent note says so).
- Changed (#67): everything agent builds write stays under the Scratch folder: Claude Code's temporary folder (`CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`), the project's Maven repository (as before), shared download caches per project for Go modules (`GOMODCACHE`), npm and pip, and the Go build cache (`GOCACHE`), Cargo (`CARGO_HOME`) and Gradle (`GRADLE_USER_HOME`) per conversation. Agents in worktree mode can no longer write `/tmp` (Mac: Claude, Codex; Linux: Codex). Before every run the gateway checks that the folders an agent may write are still real folders of the gateway user, and makes a replaced one again, so a link planted by an earlier run cannot open another folder to the sandbox. Claude Code's temporary folder is a short link in a private `/tmp/mgw-…` folder that points into `$MINIGW_DEPS/tmp`, because Claude Code needs a short path for its sockets; its files (also those of commands in its sandbox) stay under Scratch. On `minigw serve` the Claude run's `TMPDIR`, which was a folder under `/tmp`, is now the same kind of link (Windows: `$MINIGW_DEPS/tmp` itself); agents cannot write the link folders. If the link cannot be made, the run is refused. Both gateways.
- Fixed (#69): a gateway agent asked to "use codex to …" ran the `codex` program itself instead of asking the gateway (with "May ask other models" off, Claude Code's sandbox on the Mac stopped it at `~/.codex/config.toml` and Windows refused the command). Every gateway agent's instructions now say that other agents are reached only with `minigw -p --model …` and never by running `codex`, `claude`, `grok` or `copilot` directly; with "May ask other models" off they say other agents are not available here, so the agent does the task itself and says so. Mac app and `minigw serve`, every agent kind.
- Fixed (#70): on Windows `minigw serve` in worktree mode, Claude CLI tried `git add` and `git commit` in its clone, which need approval that `claude -p` cannot give, so they were refused (the Mac and Linux allow them through Claude Code's sandbox). On Windows the gateway commits the agent's work on its branch when the run ends, so the agent's instructions there now say "Do not run git add or git commit: the gateway commits your changes on this branch when you finish" (also for nested repositories; every agent kind). Git is not pre-approved on Windows, since git without a sandbox can run any command; the agent's own git commit needs the Windows sandbox (#68).
- Security (#24): every `minigw run` on the Mac gateway now keeps its temporary files in its own folder in the gateway's Scratch folder (`tmp/run-<id>`, removed when the run ends): `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`, the clang module cache and `java.io.tmpdir` of every Java process (`JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`, which replaces the `MAVEN_OPTS` setting, so Maven's forked test JVMs and Gradle get it too; Java prints "Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS"). The build can no longer write the shared `/tmp`. Fixed with it: Maven tests that create temporary files failed in `minigw run` ("Operation not permitted"). The only writes outside Scratch are macOS's own save copies in the user temporary folder, because Foundation and the `swift build` link step ignore `TMPDIR` and no setting moves them: new `TemporaryItems/NSIRD_*` and `TemporaryDirectory.*` folders, never other files there, and existing ones cannot be renamed, deleted or replaced (see How it works, minigw run sandbox). `swift build` and `xcodebuild` work as before. Run folders left by a crash are removed when the app starts. A Scratch folder whose path contains a quotation mark is refused. Mac app only (`minigw serve` has no `minigw run`).
- New (#68): a Windows gateway (`minigw serve`, MSI) runs Claude CLI, Copilot and Grok in the Windows sandbox, Microsoft MXC 1.0.1 (`wxc-exec.exe` in the install folder's `mxc` subfolder, MIT license in `mxc\LICENSE.txt`, no admin rights; Windows 11 build 26100.9278 / 26200.9278 or later; an older build is named in the reason). The whole agent CLI runs in a container that writes only to the session's folder (when allowed), `$MINIGW_DEPS`, caches and its temporary folder under Scratch, reads only the program folders on `PATH`, and cannot read your home folder, other devices' copies or the gateway's settings. "Allow commands without a sandbox" is then not needed (shown as such); Claude runs commands with its PowerShell tool, commits its own work in worktree mode again (#70 now applies only without the sandbox) and can create repositories in `$MINIGW_DEPS` (`git.exe` there is minigw's wrapper). Claude starts each run with a fresh config and home under `Scratch\tmp` (only the conversation's transcript is kept under `Scratch\state`, so `--resume` works) and signs in with the token from `claude setup-token`; Grok gets its own home per device with your `auth.json` and `config.toml` copied in before a run, never back. Folders on `PATH` inside your home (for example `~\.local\bin`) are readable by agents; the settings page warns about them. Settings page → General → About and `minigw gw status` show "Agent sandbox: Windows sandbox (MXC 1.0.1)" or why it is not available; then everything works as in 0.5.22. minigw checks `wxc-exec.exe` against the SHA-256 it was built with before every use, keeps it open without write or delete sharing during a run and refuses an `mxc` folder with other files than `wxc-exec.exe` and `LICENSE.txt`; the probe checks that the box cannot read `admin.secret`. When the box cannot reach 127.0.0.1 (loopback may need a newer cumulative update) the sandbox stays available, but the gateway log and `minigw gw status` say that "May ask other models" does not work there. The `git.exe` wrapper copy under `Scratch\gitexec` is hash-checked, and stale copies are removed at start; the per-second `.git` check also runs in the box. The release build fetches MXC by its exact tag and pinned SHA-256 (`scripts/fetch-mxc.sh`). Codex cannot start inside MXC, so on Windows it still needs "Allow commands without a sandbox" (see the Security bullet on Codex's elevated sandbox); its `TEMP`/`TMP` is the run's folder under Scratch.
- New (#68, decision 7): **Network for builds** on `minigw serve` (settings page → General → Worktree mode, `minigw gw settings` → General, `PATCH general {"worktreeNetwork"}`; on by default), the same switch as on the Mac: off, Claude CLI's commands in worktree mode have no network (Linux: Claude Code's sandbox; Windows: the Windows sandbox, which allows only TCP 443 to the addresses of `api.anthropic.com` resolved when the run starts, so Claude's web fetch and web search fail, and a run is refused when the name cannot be resolved; without the Windows sandbox the switch has no effect) and a remote `git clone` is refused naming "Settings → General → Worktree mode → Network for builds".
- Changed (#68): on Windows, local tools or Bash/PowerShell in the tools without "Allow commands without a sandbox" no longer stop the gateway from loading its settings; without the Windows sandbox each request is refused with the same text instead.
- New (#68): a **Login** row for every agent on `minigw serve` (settings page and the Gateway tray's Settings window, en and sl; `minigw gw settings`; `minigw gw claude|codex|grok|copilot login`; admin API `GET /agents/{claude,codex,grok,copilot}` → `login`), showing only the state (set / not set / logged in) and a one-line hint, never the value. Agents use subscription logins by default and nothing is copied back from a run. Claude CLI: the long-lived token from `claude setup-token` (Claude subscription, valid one year) in Credential Manager or the key store, passed as `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (`minigw gw claude token [remove]`, settings page → Claude CLI → Login, `PATCH /agents/claude {"token"}`); in the Windows sandbox it is required ("Claude CLI in the Windows sandbox needs a long-lived sign-in token: run claude setup-token, then minigw gw claude token", 403) and `~\.claude` is never copied in. Codex: the ChatGPT login state from `~/.codex/auth.json`. Grok: logged in or `grok login` (after Grok refreshes its login you may need `grok login` again). Copilot: its existing token for commands now lives in the same row. Opt-in **Use an API key instead** (Claude CLI `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, Codex `CODEX_API_KEY`, Grok `XAI_API_KEY`; `minigw gw claude|codex|grok apikey [remove]`, `PATCH {"apiKey"}`): stored in the key store, passed only when set, billed through the provider's API, not your subscription. The Mac app needs none of this (Claude uses your Keychain login).
- Changed (#68): Claude CLI conversations on a Windows gateway from 0.5.22 start fresh once (Claude's sessions now live in the box's own folders, not in `~\.claude`).
- Security (#68): the Windows sandbox has no proxy (MXC's `allowedProxyPeer` is a package identity, not a host and port, and MXC's proxying on Windows covers only WinHTTP clients). Without network for builds Claude's box allows only TCP 443 to the `api.anthropic.com` addresses resolved at run start (logged). Loopback is denied unless "May ask other models" is on: then the box can reach all of this computer's 127.0.0.1 services, because the run port is there. The gateway still refuses the agent (the admin API refuses the gateway's agent processes, the main port needs the token, which the agent cannot read, and the run port accepts only that run's processes), but other local services such as LiteLLM or Foundry Local are reachable, so keep the switch off if agents should not reach them.
- Security (#68): API-key environment variables inherited from the gateway's own environment (`ANTHROPIC_*`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `CODEX_API_KEY`, `XAI_API_KEY`, `GROK_CODE_XAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`) no longer reach any agent run, so no agent is billed through an API by accident (`minigw serve` on Windows, Linux and macOS, every agent).
- Fixed (#24): `minigw run -- swift build` failed when the gateway's folder is on another disk than the system disk (for example `/Volumes/Java`): "unable to write manifest … You don't have permission to save the file" (Code 513), because macOS keeps save copies for that disk on the disk itself (`/Volumes/<disk>/.TemporaryItems/folders.<uid>/TemporaryItems`). The sandbox now allows only new `NSIRD_*` folders there, like on the system disk. `swift build|test|run|package` also get `--cache-path` in the run's temporary folder in Scratch, so SwiftPM's manifest cache works ("failed storing manifest … readonly database" is gone); its configuration and fingerprints stay read-only (the two "is not accessible or not writable" warnings stay, as do the harmless `xcrun_db` warnings). Known limitation: `swift test` and `swift run` under `minigw run` do not work yet (#72); empty `NSIRD_*` folders are left. Mac app only.
- Fixed (#72): `minigw run -- xcodebuild -scheme … build` on a Swift package (no `.xcodeproj`) failed with exit 74: "Could not resolve package dependencies: cannot open file '~/Library/Caches/org.swift.swiftpm/manifests/ManifestLoading/….dia' (Operation not permitted)" and "Failed saving result bundle … in the folder T". The gateway now adds `-resultBundlePath`, `-packageCachePath` and `-clonedSourcePackagesDirPath` in the run's temporary folder in Scratch (unless you pass them) and `-IDEDisablePackageManifestCaching=YES`; the result bundle is removed with the run folder, pass your own `-resultBundlePath` to keep it. `.xcodeproj` builds are unchanged. Mac app only.
- Changed (#72): `minigw run -- xcodebuild` keeps DerivedData in the gateway's Scratch folder, `<Scratch>/deriveddata/<id>` per project folder (`-derivedDataPath`, unless you pass your own), kept between runs so builds stay incremental; the build can no longer write `~/Library/Developer`. Old folders are not removed automatically: delete `deriveddata` in the Scratch folder to free space. A Scratch folder moved in Settings takes `deriveddata` along. Mac app only.
- New: a safe Scratch move on `minigw serve` (Windows, Linux): `minigw gw scratch move [DIR]`, the settings page's Scratch **Move…** button (en, sl), `minigw gw settings` → General → Move everything to, and `POST /general/scratch/move {"to"}`. Everything for builds moves together (mirrors, clones, `$MINIGW_DEPS`, caches, agent state). It is refused while minigw sessions or agents are active ("minigw sessions or agents are active; move it when no minigw session runs", as on the Mac), new client requests get 503 during the move, the same drive renames, another drive copies with an owner-only access list on the target first, links copied as links and every file checked (size, SHA-256), then `serve.json` is switched and only then the old folder removed; on any error the old Scratch stays. On Windows a Scratch inside the home (also the old default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\minigw\scratch` stored in `serve.json`) counts as not chosen: it keeps working, and the warning (log, settings page, `minigw gw settings`, `minigw gw scratch`, `general.scratchSuggest`) names the proposed folder and how to move.

## 0.5.22

- Fixed: in worktree mode the agent's `git status` no longer shows an untracked `.build/` folder (the gateway's temporary folder in the clone; both gateways).
- Known limitation: in worktree mode an agent cannot create its own git repositories inside the clone (`git init`, `git clone`, CMake FetchContent with git sources). Build those dependencies in sync mode, or fetch them before the run; a shared dependencies folder for agents comes in 0.5.23.
- Fixed (#62): over SSH, `minigw gw` could unlock the login keychain with any password, because macOS does not check it while the keychain is unlocked, even when it reports it as locked for the SSH session. Now it asks only when macOS reports the keychain as locked, checks the password against the Mac account (Open Directory) before unlocking, and makes sure the keychain is really unlocked afterwards; a wrong password ends with "wrong password: the keychain stays locked", and separate messages cover a password that cannot be checked or does not unlock an out-of-sync keychain. When the keychain is unlocked but the admin item still cannot be read, it asks to quit and open the Mini Gateway app once on the Mac; without a terminal it suggests running `minigw gw` in an interactive SSH terminal before `security unlock-keychain` (macOS only; the Go client has no Keychain admin path).
- Fixed (#63): the Claude Code plugin stayed on an old version (for example 0.5.19 after installing 0.5.21), because Claude Code auto-updates third-party marketplaces only when `autoUpdate` is set. The plugin now updates automatically from the `release` branch, which only a release moves: `minigw plugin install` (Homebrew cask, MSI, `install.sh`) adds the `mini-gateway` marketplace with `"ref": "release"` and `"autoUpdate": true`, and updates the entry that minigw 0.5.21 or older added; an entry you changed (your own `"autoUpdate": false`, another `source`) stays. To opt out, set `"autoUpdate": false` under `extraKnownMarketplaces` → `mini-gateway` in `~/.claude/settings.json`. By hand: `/plugin marketplace add gh-commiter/mini-llm-gateway#release`. `minigw plugin uninstall` removes both forms. `minigw plugin status` prints `claude plugin marketplace update mini-gateway` and `claude plugin update minigw@mini-gateway` when the installed plugin is older than minigw. Both clients, compatible with the Claude Code CLI.
- Fixed (#64): `brew upgrade` printed that the plugin was "added" although it was already there (the old cask removed the entries before the upgrade). The `minigw` cask now leaves them on upgrade and reinstall, so `minigw plugin install` reports "already in"; this takes effect from the upgrade after 0.5.22, since an upgrade runs the old cask's uninstall step. The Windows MSI no longer runs `minigw plugin uninstall --upgrading` on an upgrade either (`--upgrading` is still accepted). The cask caveats now list exactly what `brew uninstall --zap --cask minigw` removes (without asking, as before) and say "gateway Mac" instead of "router Mac".
- Fixed (#65): `minigw plugin uninstall` ran `claude plugin marketplace remove mini-gateway` even when the `mini-gateway` entry was your own (another `source`, your own `ref`, `autoUpdate` false), although the settings cleanup keeps it; now the Claude Code removal runs only for an entry minigw added (both clients). On a Windows MSI upgrade where you untick the "Claude Code plugin" feature, the plugin entry is now removed (before, it stayed against your choice); an upgrade of an install that never had the plugin part (for example `/minigw:gw update`) leaves a plugin you added by hand alone.
- Fixed (#60): when the gateway caps an agent's work mode (#45), minigw's start line said "read-write" and only the gateway note on the first request said the agent "only read the folder". The gateway now lists the capped agents when it registers the folder, so the start line says "syncing … read-write (read-only for Codex: capped by the gateway)", or "read-only (capped by the gateway)" when every agent is capped; in native mode in the folder itself the line ends with ", read-only for Codex (capped by the gateway)". With an older gateway the line stays as before. Both gateways, both clients.
- New (#60): `minigw gw claude|codex|grok|copilot --work=direct|sync|worktree` sets the agent's cap from the command line (as in Settings, the settings page and `minigw gw settings`); without `--work` it shows it, and `minigw gw claude` now also prints `work`. Any other value: "--work: direct, sync or worktree"; `--work` with another command: "--work only applies to minigw gw claude|codex|grok|copilot". Mac app and `minigw serve`.
- Security (#59): a gateway Codex run could load a `.codex/config.toml` from the clone or shared folder (written by the agent or the client) when you trusted that project, its repository or a folder above it in `~/.codex/config.toml`, and its `notify`, `mcp_servers` or hooks then ran outside Codex's sandbox. Now every gateway Codex run (chat, sync, direct, worktree and native mode, resumed sessions, nested agent calls, and the model list `codex debug models`) marks its folder and every folder above it as untrusted for that run, so Codex ignores project settings there (on Windows also through junctions); your `~/.codex/config.toml` itself still applies. Mac app and `minigw serve` (Windows, Linux, macOS).
- Security (#61): in worktree mode the gateway refuses to start an agent (also an agent asked by another agent) when a new `.git` appeared in its start folder in the clone or a folder above it (other than a registered nested repository's), or one of those folders was replaced by a link: "The agent's clone was changed (a .git appeared at <path>): start a new conversation" (400). Nested repositories already in your folder keep working across turns. The agents' git in the clone no longer uses a bare repository it finds by itself (`safe.bareRepository=explicit`), and an agent asked by another agent is refused when the caller's clone `.git` was replaced, like the caller itself. Mac app and `minigw serve` (macOS, Linux, Windows).
- Fixed (#66): in worktree mode, each `minigw -p --continue` (or `--resume <id>`) turn started from your folder, which the agent's previous branch does not change, so the agent did not see the files it said it had made. Now the next turn of the same conversation always starts from the conversation's last branch, also when your folder changed since the last turn (minigw then prints "minigw: worktree: continuing from minigw/…; changes in your folder since the last turn are not in the agent's copy"), and its branch contains the earlier turns; the branch line says "(<base> = your state when this conversation started; this branch includes the earlier turns)". `--continue` takes the last conversation minigw ran in this folder, `--resume <id>` that session, whose branch name then moves forward (`--continue` gets a new branch each turn); `--resume <id> --fork-session` continues from that session's branch as a new conversation; `--resume` without an id starts from your folder. All agents of one run start from the same point; the next turn continues from the branch that came back last. It starts from your folder, with "minigw: worktree: starting from your folder, not from minigw/…: <reason>", when you deleted the branch (delete it to start fresh), when the conversation ran on another gateway, or in a repository with nested repositories; a branch you merged into `HEAD` starts from your folder without a line. minigw keeps the last branch per conversation in `.git/minigw-continue.json`. minigw on Mac, Windows and Linux; no gateway change.

## 0.5.21

- Fixed (#40): in worktree mode, `git -C <nested> …` from Claude CLI could commit into the top repository (the agent's environment carried the top clone's `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and `GIT_COMMON_DIR`; with `GIT_DIR` of the nested clone set by hand git then also said "could not parse HEAD"), and Codex could not commit at all, not even in the top repository (its sandbox made every `.git` read-only: "blocked by sandbox permissions on .git/index.lock"). Now agents other than Codex get no `GIT_DIR`: the `git` on their PATH picks the clone per call, and git outside it finds the clone it runs in. Codex runs with its own permission profile in a clone: every clone's `.git` is writable except `config`, `config.worktree`, `hooks`, `info`, `commondir` and `worktrees`; its commands get the `git` wrapper and no top `GIT_DIR`, while Codex itself keeps the top clone. Mac app and Linux/macOS `minigw serve`.
- New: remote administration of the Mac app is `ssh` to the gateway Mac and `minigw gw …` there. When the login keychain is locked in that SSH session, `minigw gw` in a terminal now asks for the Mac password (without echo) and unlocks it with macOS (`SecKeychainUnlock`, never the `security` command), then reads the admin key again; a wrong password ends with "wrong password: the keychain stays locked". The keychain stays unlocked for the SSH session as macOS keeps it. Without a terminal (scripts, Claude Code skills) nothing is asked and the message is as before. macOS only (the Go client has no Keychain admin path).
- Fixed (#40): in worktree mode, Claude CLI agents could not run git at all, on the Mac app gateway (Claude Code's sandbox denied `~/.gitconfig`) and on Linux `minigw serve` (the sandbox made `.git/commondir` unreadable; clones now get a `commondir` of `.`), and no agent could commit in a nested clone without an environment prefix that Claude Code refuses. Now agent git in the clone ignores the gateway owner's git config (identity, signing, credential helpers, `insteadOf`, global ignore file), commits are authored as the agent (`Claude (Mini Gateway) <claude@minigw.invalid>`, likewise Codex, Grok and Copilot), and `cd <path> && git …` or `git -C <path> …` works in that nested clone (a `git` on the agent's PATH picks the registered clone; it never uses a repository the agent made). The agent author and the unused owner config apply on every gateway (Windows too); `cd`/`git -C` in nested clones on the Mac app and Linux `minigw serve`, every agent. On Windows `minigw serve` the nested note and the automatic commit stay as before. Security: on the Mac app every nested clone is a Codex writable folder, so Codex's sandbox keeps its `.git` read-only; on Linux `minigw serve` every folder on the way to a nested clone is pinned (or watched), so an agent cannot rename it and plant another `.git`.
- Fixed (#50): in worktree mode, a nested repository that could not come back (moved or replaced by the agent, branch larger than 64 MB, or not saved) dropped the agent's top-level work for that run, and on Linux `minigw serve` an agent without its own process namespace blocked nested repositories for the rest of the session. Now the top-level branch and the other nested repositories still come back (without the refused repository's files), minigw prints one line per refused repository with the reason, and the agent's files from a clone with work that did not come back are kept on the gateway for 30 days as `worktrees/…/<clone>-kept-<time>` in its Scratch folder (files only, every `.git` renamed `.git.agent` so no agent git settings or hooks run; at most 3 per repository; not for a clone refused as too large). On Linux such an agent run in a clone with nested repositories is refused before it starts (that request only, "use sync"). Mac app and `minigw serve`, both clients.
- New: the gateway's owner can cap the work mode per agent (Claude CLI, Codex, Grok, Copilot): **Changes in the client's folder** = in the folder or a copy (any mode, the default), only in a copy or on a branch (sync, worktree), or only on a branch (worktree). When a session's mode is above the agent's cap, the agent still runs but read-only, and the client prints "Mini Gateway lets Codex change files only in worktree mode, so it only read the folder (in a git repository, run minigw --minigw-work=worktree)"; nested agent calls are capped by the asked agent's kind. The cap covers work in the client's folder only; agents working in the gateway's own allowed folders (chat) are not capped. The gateway takes the mode from the session it registered, never from a header. Mac Settings (each agent pane), the settings page, `minigw gw settings` → Agents, admin API `work` on `/agents/*`. Both gateways. Both clients now print gateway notes in every mode (older clients only in worktree and native mode).
- New: project counters on both gateways: requests, errors, refusals, time and tokens per device, folder, UTC day and agent kind (claude, codex, grok, copilot, other), kept 31 days (at most 5000 rows, 1250 per device) also after the sessions are gone. Settings → Sessions and `minigw gw settings` → Sessions show **Projects (7 days)**; `minigw gw usage [--days N]` and `GET /usage` list them; Clear History clears them too. Tokens are what the provider reports.
- Session history now records the restrictions a run actually applied (read-only from an agent without writing in worktree mode, and from the new cap, shown as "read-only (cap worktree)"), not values recomputed after the run. Both gateways.
